L44: ## 1 Executive Summary
L45: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L46: Umbralisib is a phosphoinositide-3-kinase-delta (PI3K\delta) inhibitor in development for the treatment of patients with hematologic malignancies. While both the gamma and the delta isoforms of PI3K are restricted to cells of hematopoietic origin, the delta isoform has the highest expression on leukocytes. Umbralisib also inhibits Casein Kinase 1 epsilon (CK1\epsilon) which regulates the non-canonical Wnt/planar cell polarity pathway. This pathway is involved in lymphoid malignancy pathogenesis via regulation of cell survival, proliferation, chemotaxis, and migration. Regulation of the Wnt/\beta-catenin pathway also modulates T-cell transcription factor-1, which affects the development of regulatory T cells (Tregs). The Applicant purports that this support of regulatory T-cells is the mechanism by which T-regs clones are supported, and the development of immune-related toxicity may be potentially mitigated.
L47: On 15 June 2020, TG Therapeutics submitted Part 2 of their rolling NDA 213176 submission under 21 CFR 314.50 and Section 505(b)(1) of the Federal Food, Drug and Cosmetic Act, seeking accelerated approval of umbralisib at the recommended dose of 800 mg by mouth daily for the following indication:
L48: - Treatment of adult patients with marginal zone lymphoma (MZL) who have received at least one prior anti-CD20-based regimen.
L49: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L50: The efficacy of umbralisib is based on data from 69 patients with MZL from a cohort of the UTX-TGR-205 trial, an open-label, multi-center, single-arm, multi-cohort trial. Patients with MZL were required to have prior treatment with 1 or more lines of therapy, including at least 1 anti-
L51: CD20-based regimen. The trial excluded patients with prior exposure to a PI3K inhibitor. The primary endpoint was overall response rate (ORR) assessed by an independent review committee (IRC) based on the 2007 International Working Group criteria using computerized tomography (CT). Secondary endpoints included duration of response and progression-free survival.
L52: Of the 69 patients with MZL [extranodal (N=38), nodal (N=20), and splenic (N=11)], the median age was 67 years (range: 34 to 88 years), 52% were female, 83% were White, 7% were Black, 3% were Asian, and 97% had a baseline ECOG performance status of 0 or 1. Patients had a median number of prior lines of therapy of 2 (range: 1 to 6), with 26% being refractory to their last therapy. The median follow-up time was 20.3 months (range 15.0 to 28.7 months). The ORR per IRC was 49% (95% CI: 37.0, 61.6), with 16% achieving a complete response. The median duration of response was not reached (95% CI: 9.3, not evaluable). Of the 34 patients that responded, 65% maintained a response at 6 months, 50% at 9 months, and 38% at 12 months.
L53: The data in patients with MZL support the determination that umbralisib has clinically meaningful activity in adult patients with relapsed or refractory MZL. To support accelerated approval, the efficacy data is considered in the context of available therapy for patients with MZL in the 2nd line setting and beyond. Available therapy includes rituximab and the immunotherapy combination rituximab plus lenalidomide, which provides response rates ranging from 44% to 65% with associated durability (Table 1). Thus, the ORR of 49% with durability for umbralisib provides data to support a clinically meaningful therapeutic benefit in the context of available therapy for patients with relapsed or refractory MZL in the 2nd line setting and beyond. Therefore, the review team recommends accelerated approval of umbralisib for:
L54: - Treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory marginal zone lymphoma (MZL) who have received at least one prior anti-CD20-based regimen.
L55: ## **Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment**
L56: Umbralisib is a specific and orally bioavailable dual kinase inhibitor of phosphoinositide-3-kinase-delta (PI3Kδ) and casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1ε).
L57: ## **Efficacy:**
L58: Efficacy of umbralisib in adult patients with previously treated marginal zone lymphoma (MZL) is based on the results from a single-arm cohort of Study UTX-TGR-205, a multicenter, single-arm, open-label, multi-cohort trial, which included 69 patients with MZL. The scheduled umbralisib dose was 800 mg by mouth daily until progressive disease or unacceptable toxicity. The primary endpoint was overall response rate (ORR) assessed by an independent review committee (IRC) based on the 2007 International Working Group criteria using computerized tomography (CT). Among the 69 patients with MZL, the median follow-up time was 20.3 months (range 15.0 to 28.7 months). The ORR per IRC was 49% (95% CI: 37.0, 61.6), with 16% achieving a complete response. The median duration of response was not reached.
L59: ## **Safety:**
L60: The safety profile was supported by analysis of the 69 patients with marginal zone lymphoma from the study UTX-TGR-205 and a combined safety database of 371 patients with hematologic malignancies [marginal zone lymphoma (22%), follicular lymphoma (40%), diffuse large B-cell lymphoma or mantle cell lymphoma (20%), and other hematologic malignancies (18%)] who received umbralisib doses ≥800 mg daily.
L61: In the 69 patients with MZL, the median duration of treatment was 9.4 months (range 5 days to 23 months) and the most common adverse events (≥20% of patients) were increased creatinine, diarrhea-colitis, AST/ALT increased, fatigue, neutropenia, nausea, musculoskeletal pain, thrombocytopenia, cough, headache, rash, decreased appetite, upper respiratory tract infection, anemia, edema, and vomiting. Grade ≥ 3 adverse events occurred in 62% and those occurring in at least 5% of the MZL population included diarrhea-colitis, AST/ALT increased, and neutropenia. Adverse events that led to treatment discontinuation occurred in 23% of patients, most often due to AST/ALT increased and diarrhea-colitis.
L62: The primary safety issues identified with umbralisib include serious, including fatal, infections and cutaneous reactions, and serious diarrhea or colitis, hepatotoxicity, and neutropenia. In the 371 patients with hematologic malignancies, 51% of patients
L63: experienced a grade 3 or 4 toxicity, the most common including neutropenia (17%), diarrhea-colitis (8%), and increased AST/ALT (6%). Fifteen percent of patients discontinued umbralisib due to an adverse event, most often due to diarrhea-colitis and increased AST/ALT.
L64: ## **Benefit-Risk:**
L65: In the single-arm cohort with 69 patients with MZL, patients had to of received prior treatment with 1 or more lines of therapy, including at least 1 anti-CD20-based regimen. The median number of prior systemic therapies was 2 (range 1 to 6) with 22% refractory to their last therapy. The ORR of 49% with associated durability and a tolerable safety profile constitutes a meaningful clinical benefit and a favorable benefit-risk for these patients. To support accelerated approval, the efficacy data is considered in the context of available therapy for patients with MZL in the 2nd line setting and beyond. Available therapy includes rituximab and the immunotherapy combination rituximab plus lenalidomide, which provides response rates ranging from 44% to 65% with associated durability (Table 1). Thus, the ORR of 49% with durability for umbralisib provides data to support a clinically meaningful therapeutic benefit in the context of available therapy for patients with relapsed or refractory MZL in the 2nd line setting and beyond. Therefore, the benefit-risk of umbralisib is deemed favorable to support accelerated approval under 21 CFR 314.510 Subpart H for the following indication:
L66: - Treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory marginal zone lymphoma who have received at least one prior anti-CD20-based regimen.
L67: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	Approximately 7,500 cases of marginal zone lymphoma (MZL) are diagnosed each year in the United States. Patients with relapsed or refractory MZL are considered incurable.
The course of MZL is characterized by remitting and relapsing phases, and though most patients respond to initial chemo-immunotherapy, they will require multiple therapeutic regimens over the course of their disease.
MZL is a serious and life-threatening disease.
L68: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Median survival, regardless of therapy is generally between 8 and 12 years.

Current Treatment Options	Treatment options for relapsed and refractory MZL include chemotherapy, immunochemotherapy, and anti-CD20 monoclonal antibodies.
Numerous patients with relapsed or refractory MZL cannot tolerate intensive chemotherapy due to age or comorbidities.
New treatments are needed for patients with relapsed or refractory MZL, including chemotherapy-free treatments.
Benefit	Umbralisib was administered at 800 mg orally once daily
Study UTX-TGR-205 was an open-label, multicenter, single-arm multi-cohort trial which included 69 patients with relapsed or refractory MZL.
Overall response rate (ORR) per independent review committee was 49% (95% CI: 37.0, 61.6), with 16% achieving a complete response. The median duration of response was not reached.
Based on ORR with durability, umbralisib has clinically meaningful activity in patients with relapsed or refractory MZL who have received at least one prior anti-CD20-based regimen.
Risk and Risk Management	In the 69 patients with MZL treated with umbralisib 800 mg daily:The median duration of treatment was 9.4 months (range 5 days to 23 months)
The most common adverse events (\ge 20\% of patients) were increased creatinine, diarrhea-colitis, AST/ALT increased, fatigue, neutropenia, nausea, musculoskeletal pain, thrombocytopenia, cough, headache, rash, decreased appetite, upper respiratory tract infection, anemia, edema, and vomiting.

The safety profile of umbralisib is acceptable in the intended patient population.
The USPI includes warnings and precautions for infections, neutropenia, diarrhea or colitis, hepatotoxicity, and severe cutaneous reactions.
L69: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Grade \ge 3 adverse events occurred in 62% and those occurring in at least 5% of the MZL population included diarrhea-colitis, AST/ALT increased, and neutropenia.
Adverse events that led to treatment discontinuation occurred in 23% of patients, most often due to AST/ALT increased and diarrhea-colitis.

L80: ## 2 Therapeutic Context
L81: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L82: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L83: Marginal zone lymphoma (MZL) is the second most common form of indolent non-Hodgkin lymphoma (iNHL), accounting for 5% to 15% of all non-Hodgkin lymphoma (NHL) (Zucca et al. 2020), with an estimated 7,500 new United States (US) cases annually (Leslie et al. 2019). MZL arises from B lymphocytes at the margins or edges of lymph nodes and various tissues, including the stomach, salivary glands, thyroid gland, eyes, lungs, and spleen (Zucca et al. 2020). There are 3 subtypes of MZL: splenic, nodal, and mucosal-associated lymphoid tissue (MALT), which respectively comprise 20%, 10%, and 70% of MZL cases (Zucca et al. 2020). While all 3 subtypes arise from memory B lymphocytes originating in the marginal zone of lymphoid follicles, they exhibit distinct genetic mutations, anatomical presentation, and response to therapies (Zinzani 2012).
L84: B-cell activating autoimmune conditions, chronic infectious pathogens (such as *Helicobacter pylori*), and genetic variation in the major histocompatibility complex have been implicated in the etiology of MZL (Zucca et al. 2020). *H. pylori* and hepatitis C virus are precursors to many instances of gastric MALT-MZL and splenic MZL (Zucca et al. 2014), respectively, warranting antibiotic (Strati et al. 2019) and antiviral (Arcaini et al. 2014) therapy. Despite these attempts to eradicate the underlying pathogen and mitigate chronic inflammation, MZL often becomes antigen-independent (Zucca et al. 2014) and a majority of patients will relapse, necessitating subsequent therapy (Zinzani 2012, Strati et al. 2019).
L85: The clinical disease characteristics of MZL vary depending on the subtype and lymphoma site of origin (Denlinger et al. 2018). For patients with extranodal MZL or MALT MZL, the disease is commonly limited to the site of origin, with rare peripheral lymph node and bone marrow (BM) involvement. For patients with splenic MZL, splenomegaly without lymphadenopathy is the hallmark clinical feature, with most patients exhibiting BM and blood involvement (Rosand et al. 2018). Most patients with nodal MZL present with non-bulky disseminated peripheral, abdominal, and thoracic lymph node involvement with B symptoms (i.e., fever, night sweats, and weight loss) and elevated lactate dehydrogenase levels commonly observed; BM involvement can occur, but peripheral blood involvement and cytopenias are rare (Rosand et al. 2018). Although the clinical manifestations vary, chronic activation of the B-cell receptor, which provides proliferation and survival signals, has been implicated in the pathogenesis of B cell lymphoproliferative disorders in general, including MZL (Noy et al. 2017).
L86: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L87: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L88: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L89: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L90: Currently, no standard-of-care treatment exists for first-line or relapsed/refractory MZL, and treatments vary depending on the MZL subtype, clinical manifestations, etiology, lymphoma site of origin, patient's age, and overall health (Denlinger et al. 2018, Lymphoma Research Foundation 2018, Rosand et al. 2018). Accordingly, consensus guidelines (National Comprehensive Cancer Network [NCCN 2020]) offer a variety of options for first-line therapy of MZL with chemoimmunotherapy regimens including bendamustine plus rituximab or rituximab, cyclophosphamide, doxorubicin, vincristine, and prednisone (R-CHOP), with milder regimens suggested for patients who are elderly or infirm, including rituximab monotherapy. Second-line and subsequent therapeutic options include repeat chemoimmunotherapy, lenalidomide+rituximab, ibrutinib, radioimmunotherapy, phosphoinositide 3-kinase (PI3K) inhibitors, or rituximab monotherapy. The only fully approved therapy for previously treated MZL is REVLMID® (lenalidomide) in combination with rituximab (R2). Table 1 summarizes the Food and Drug Administration (FDA)-approved treatment options in the MZL relapsed setting.
L91: **Table 1: Summary of FDA Approved Treatment Options in the MZL Relapsed Setting**

Source: Leonard et al 2019, Drugs@FDA
L96: Despite the various treatment options available, MZL remains an incurable, life-threatening condition. Limitations of initial therapeutic approaches, such as surgical intervention, radiation therapy, radioimmunotherapy, and chemoimmunotherapy, include surgical complications and development of secondary malignancies. Furthermore, none of these strategies are capable of completely eradicating MZL, which results in relapse and necessitates subsequent lines of therapy. In the relapsed setting, R2 is the only full FDA-approved treatment; however, this combination did not improve outcomes and resulted in shorter progression-free survival (PFS) compared to rituximab alone among MZL patients (Thieblemont et al. 2019). Ibrutinib received accelerated approval; however, it failed to generate deep remissions, with only 3% of patients achieving a complete response (CR) (IMBRUVICA® package insert 2019). While there are various interventions in the frontline setting, treatments for relapsed/refractory MZL are largely investigational. This scarcity of treatment options for relapsed/refractory MZL patients represents a high unmet medical need.
L97: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L98: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's assertion that lenalidomide in combination with rituximab did not provide an improvement in clinical outcomes for patients with relapsed or refractory MZL. For patients with MZL, the combination of lenalidomide and rituximab was evaluated in the AUGMENT and MAGNIFY trials (Table 1). In AUGMENT, there were 31 patients with MZL who received lenalidomide in combination with rituximab and 32 patients who received rituximab plus placebo. Notably in AUGMENT, patients with rituximab refractory disease were ineligible. For the 31 patients receiving the combination, the ORR was 65% (95% CI: 45%, 81%) with a median duration of response of 17.4 months (95% CI: 13.2, not evaluable). For the 32 patients receiving rituximab plus placebo, the ORR was 44% (95% CI: 26%, 62%) with a median duration of response not reached (95% CI: 8.4, not evaluable). In the MAGNIFY trial, 45 patients with MZL received lenalidomide in combination with rituximab. In MAGNIFY, patients with rituximab refractory disease were eligible and 41% of the 45 patients with MZL had rituximab refractory disease. In the 45 patients, the ORR was 51% (95% CI: 36%, 66%) and the median duration of response was not reached (95% CI: 8, not evaluable) with a median follow-up of 11.5 months. For the combination of lenalidomide and rituximab, the response rates of 51% to 65% with associated durability are supportive of a clinically meaningful benefit in patients with MZL.
L99: For the time-to-event outcomes, such as progression-free survival (PFS), the limited number of patients with MZL in the AUGMENT and MAGNIFY trials preclude any meaningful interpretation of the PFS outcomes and should be considered exploratory and descriptive.
L100: The FDA considers lenalidomide in combination with rituximab available therapy for patients with relapsed or refractory MZL. To support accelerated approval, the data with umbralisib in patients with MZL was required to demonstrate a clinically meaningful therapeutic benefit in the context of available therapy.

L114: ## 4 Significant Issues from Other Review Disciplines Pertinent to Clinical Conclusions on Efficacy and Safety
L115: ## 4.1. Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)
L116: Clinical site inspections were requested for two clinical sites in the U.S. with the highest accrual for Study UTX-TGR-205, along with inspection of the Applicant, TG Therapeutics. The clinical sites included 1) Site HOB - MD Anderson (Felipe Samaniego, MD; Houston, TX), which enrolled 24 patients and 2) Site CLA - Levine Cancer Institute (Nilanjan Ghosh, MD; Charlotte, NC), which enrolled 19 patients. Based on the clinical site inspections, the study data from the clinical sites were considered reliable. Additionally, the Applicant was inspected concerning its oversight of Study UTX-TGR-205. Based on the inspection, the Applicant's oversight of the clinical trial and the quality of conducting the study were determined to be adequate. The audited study data submitted to the Agency was deemed acceptable in support of the NDA application, in compliance with Good Clinical Practice.
L117: ## 4.2. Product Quality
L118: Umbralisib tosylate is a small chiral BCS class II molecule that is produced as a single enantiomer that is manufactured by Alembic Pharmaceuticals Limited, India, as a single enantiomer in a linear, two-stage synthesis. The drug product is presented as a 200 mg immediate-release tablet for oral administration. It is a green film-coated, oval-shaped tablet,
L119: debossed with “L474” on one side and plain on the other.
L120: The recommended starting dose for the drug product is 800 mg orally, once daily until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L121: Based on the information provided by the Applicant, the Office of Product Quality (OPQ) has determined that the data is adequately supportive and the potential risks to patient safety, product efficacy, and product quality are mitigated appropriately. Therefore, OPQ recommends approval of umbralisib and grants a (b)(4) month retest for the drug substance and a 36-month shelf-life for the drug product when stored in HDPE bottles at 20°C and 25°C (68°F to 77°F).

L126: ## 5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L127: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L128: Umbralisib (also known as RP-5264, TGR-1202, [REDACTED](b)(4), and RP-5307) is a small molecule inhibitor of phosphoinositide-3-kinase delta (PI3K\delta) and casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1\epsilon). The targets mediate tumor-progressive signaling in a variety of hematologic malignancies.
L129: Umbralisib binds to human PI3K\delta and CK1\epsilon with dissociation constants (Kd) of 6.2 nM and 180 nM, respectively. A screen of 442 kinases incubated with 1 \muM of umbralisib showed that the phosphorylated Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1 containing the mutation F317I (ABL1 (F317I)-phosphorylated) kinase was inhibited at levels comparable to CK1\epsilon. In vitro growth-inhibitory activity was assessed in a panel of 13 human leukemia and lymphoma cell lines. Growth inhibitory (GI50) values ranged from 0.6 to 66 \muM after the cells were treated for 72 or 96 hours with umbralisib. The test article also inhibited cytokine-mediated adhesion and migration in vitro.
L130: The in vivo antitumor activity of umbralisib was evaluated in a MOLT-4 xenograft model. MOLT-4 tumor-bearing mice were dosed orally with umbralisib at 150 mg/kg/day for 25 days, with the measurement of tumor growth continuing until 41 days after dosing initiation. The test article dosing group had tumors growing at a slower rate than the control group tumors.
L131: Safety pharmacology studies assessed the effects of umbralisib on cardiovascular, central nervous system (CNS), and respiratory function. Umbralisib had no toxicologically-significant effects on cardiovascular function in a cardiovascular study in dogs, and had no effects on neurobehavioral function or temperature in mice. Respiratory function was affected by umbralisib with decreases in tidal volume as much as 24%, respiration rate as much as 38%, and minute volume as much as 46% observed in respiratory studies conducted in mice administered a single umbralisib dose of 50 to 500 mg/kg.
L132: The oral bioavailability of umbralisib in mice and dogs were 47% and 34% respectively for the 2 species. A mass balance study in male mice demonstrated that 89% and 5% of umbralisib is excreted in feces and urine, respectively. Umbralisib metabolism was evaluated in vitro with liver microsomes. After 60 minutes of incubation with mouse, dog, and human liver microsomes, 75%, 77%, and 86% respectively of umbralisib remained.
L133: The repeat-dose studies in mice and dogs referenced above were conducted to assess the general toxicity of umbralisib. The drug was administered once daily for 26 weeks in mice and
L134: 39 weeks in dogs, with the doses and durations informed by the experience of the 13-week repeat-dose studies in both species. The studies were conducted using the oral route of administration, which is consistent with the intended clinical route of administration. There were several unscheduled deaths in the mouse study, while dose reductions and dosing holidays were applied to the dog study. Drug-related findings included decreased white blood cells (WBCs) and differentials, increased liver weights and alkaline phosphatase (ALP) levels, and lymphoid depletion.
L135: In the 26-week mouse study, umbralisib was administered by oral gavage at 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day with 4 weeks of recovery. Umbralisib-induced mortality was observed in the study with half of all observed mortalities and unscheduled euthanasia's occurring in the 300 mg/kg dose group. The exact causes of death in these animals were undetermined. In the surviving animals, toxicities included reduced lymphocyte levels, increased liver weights and ALP levels, and inflammatory findings. In the 39-week dog study, dogs were initially dosed orally with umbralisib at 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg/day, but doses were reduced to 8, 20, and 50 mg/kg/day due to notable weight loss as the study proceeded. The high-dose group (100/50 mg/kg/day) also had a dosing holiday due to continued weight loss. Reduced RBCs, hemoglobin, hematocrit, and reticulocyte levels were observed in all umbralisib-dosed conditions. Microscopic findings were limited to decreased cellularity in the lymph nodes and the thymus. Additional findings of decreased weight and the presence of debris in the lumen, and tubular degeneration and atrophy in the testes were noted in the 13-week repeat-dose study in dogs.
L136: A combined male and female fertility study was conducted in mice with umbralisib administered once daily at doses of 50, 150, or 300 mg/kg/day, starting 28 days prior to pairing and through mating in males and starting 14 days prior to pairing through Gestation Day (GD) 7 in females. Umbralisib-related findings included reduced testis and epididymis weights, and reduced sperm count and motility in the mid- and high-dose groups. Findings in female mice included increased post-implantation loss and increased resorption. Exposure at 150 mg/kg/day is approximately 1.3 times the recommended human clinical dose based on the toxicokinetic (AUC) values.
L137: In an embryo-fetal development study, daily oral administration of umbralisib at 100, 200, or 400 mg/kg/day to pregnant mice on GD 6-15 resulted in increased litter losses, visceral findings and fetal skeletal abnormalities in all dose groups. Malformations noted were increased frequency of cleft palates in the mid- and high-dose groups and low incidence nasopharyngeal fistula (one fetus) at high dose. The 100, 200, and 400 mg/kg groups had exposures that were 0.9, 1.4, and 2.4 times the exposures at the recommended clinical dose based on AUC. In an embryo-fetal development study in female rabbits, daily administration of umbralisib at 30, 100, or 300 mg/kg/day on GD 6-19 resulted in maternal toxicity characterized by a 50% reduction in food intake and an 8% reduction in body weight by the end of the dosing period.
L138: Fetal weights were lower in the high-dose group. Elevated fetal abnormalities were noted in the mid- and high-dose groups compared to the concurrent controls, although the levels were considered within the normal range for this strain of rabbit. The 30, 100, or 300 mg/kg/day doses resulted in exposures that were 0.03, 0.08, and 0.03 times the exposures at the recommended clinical dose based on AUC. There was high inter-animal variability in the toxicokinetic values in this study within each dose group, reduced exposures at high dose, and lack of dose-dependence in exposures observed in rabbits.
L139: Umbralisib was not mutagenic in the in vitro bacterial reverse mutation test or clastogenic in either the in vitro chromosomal aberrations assay or in the in vivo bone marrow micronucleus assay in mice. No carcinogenicity studies have been conducted or are warranted to support the marketing of umbralisib for the current indications.
L140: The nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology data submitted to this NDA are adequate to support the approval of umbralisib for the proposed indications.

L143: The following NDAs and Drug Master Files (DMFs) are referenced in this application: Investigational New Drug (IND) (b)(4), IND 116762, IND (b)(4), DMF (b)(4), DMF (b)(4), and DMF (b)(4).
L144: ## 5.3. Pharmacology
L145: ## Primary pharmacology
L146: ## FDA's Assessment:
L147: In vitro and in vivo pharmacology studies conducted with umbralisib assessed the mechanism of action and the pharmacological activity. The binding affinity for CK1\epsilon and PI3K was determined with a competitive binding assay based on phage display (Study # TGT027-01-S-00001). Umbralisib was incubated with CK1\epsilon and PI3K variants \alpha, \beta, \delta, and \gamma for an hour at room temperature prior to an assessment of binding affinity (Kd). The resulting Kds were 180 nM for CK1\epsilon and 6.2 for PI3K\delta. The Kds for the other PI3K variants were in the \muM range.
L148: ## Inhibitory activity of umbralisib
L149: Umbralisib Kd (nM)
Kinase	CK1\epsilon	PI3K\alpha	PI3K\beta	PI3K\delta	PI3K\gamma
180	>10000	>10000	6.2	1400
L150: The selectivity of umbralisib was determined by evaluating its binding to a panel of 442 kinases that included PI3K variants and CK1\epsilon (Study # INO006-01-P-00001). Using the same platform that determined the K_d for umbralisib, the test article was incubated with the panel of kinases at 1 \muM for an hour at room temperature prior an assessment of binding inhibition normalized to control conditions. Beyond the intended targets, phosphorylated Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1 containing the mutation F317I (ABL1 (F317I)-phosphorylated) was the only kinase in the panel that was inhibited by the test article at levels beyond the 65% threshold. With an inhibition level of 72%, ABL1 was inhibited at levels comparable to CK1\epsilon, which had an inhibition level of 73% in the same assay.
L151: The in vitro inhibitory effect of umbralisib on malignant cell proliferation was evaluated with a panel of 13 leukemia and lymphoma cell lines (RPMI-8226, U266B1, MM1.S, MM1.R, DLBCL, Toledo, Daudi, Raji, CCRF-CEM, MOLT-4, U937, THP-1, and Jurkat. Study # IVT-5264-LCA-01). Umbralisib was incubated with the cell lines for 72 or 96 hours at 37°C. Growth was determined with a 3-(4,5-Dimethylthiazol-2-yl)-2,5-diphenyltetrazolium bromide (MTT) assay and normalized to untreated conditions. Growth inhibitory (GI_{50}) values ranged from 14.9 \muM (Daudi, a Burkitt's lymphoma cell line) to 0.58 \muM (Toledo, a DLBCL/non-Hodgkin's lymphoma cell line).

L155: To determine if umbralisib inhibits cell adhesion (Study # VT-5264-VCAD-29), Daudi cells were treated with the test article at 1 \muM for an hour prior to a brief plating on VCAM- and CXCL12- or CCL19-coated plates with centrifugation at room temperature. The plates were washed and the remaining cells were fixed and stained prior to plate reading for absorbance at 570 nm. Umbralisib inhibited CXCL12-mediated adhesion by 50% and CCL19-mediated adhesion by 70%.
L156: Umbralisib inhibition of cell migration was also evaluated with Daudi cells (Study # IVT-5264-VCMIIG-28). The cells were added to VCAM-coated migration chamber inserts which were then added to chambers containing CXCL12, CXCL13, or CCL19, with umbralisib at 1 \muM for 5 hours at room temperature. Cells that migrated to the underside of the migration chamber insert were stained prior to plate reading for absorbance at 570 nm. Umbralisib inhibited CXCL12- and CCL19-mediated migration by 70% and CXCL13-mediated migration by 80%.
L157: Umbralisib's in vivo antitumor activity was evaluated in a Molt-4 xenograft model (Study # Incozen-CP-P006). The Molt-4 cell line was established subcutaneously in female NOD/SCID mice prior to oral dosing with umbralisib at 150 mg/kg for 25 days. Tumors were measured 3 times per week and the mice were weighed weekly. The tumor volumes were determined and normalized to the untreated condition. A group of mice were dosed with Ara-C as a dosing control with known antitumor effects on the model. The animals were euthanized on day 41. Umbralisib-dosed animals had reduced tumor growth rates compared to untreated animals.
L158: ## **Antitumor activity of umbralisib (ICZ-515; excerpted from Applicant's submission)**
L159: ## Secondary Pharmacology
L160: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L161: Umbralisib demonstrated selectivity against a panel of 442 kinases in a KINOMEsan™ competition binding assay. At a fixed concentration of 1 \mu\text{M}, umbralisib inhibited ligand binding of only 3 kinases at greater than the 65% inhibition cutoff: PI3K\delta (100% inhibition), casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1\epsilon) (73% inhibition), and phosphorylated Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1 (ABL1)(F317I)-phosphorylated (72% inhibition).
L162: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L163: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that umbralisib at a concentration of 1 \mu\text{M} inhibited 3 out of 442 kinases at levels greater than 65%. One of the 3 kinases, ABL1(F317I), is not an intended target and was inhibited at levels comparable to CK1\epsilon at the concentration tested.
L164: ## Safety Pharmacology
L165: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L166: In a cell-based human ether-à-go-go-related gene (hERG) assay, the \text{IC}_{50} for umbralisib was found to exceed 100 \mu\text{M} (highest tested concentration), a concentration which is beyond the relevant human concentrations of umbralisib in the circulation. In electrocardiogram (ECG) evaluations in dogs, oral doses of umbralisib up to 250 mg/kg (equivalent to 5,000 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 or
L167: 9,722 mg in a 70 kg human) did not result in any changes in cardiovascular parameters. In a central nervous system (CNS) evaluation in mice, after a single dose of umbralisib at doses up to 300 mg/kg (equivalent to 900 mg/m2 or 1707 mg in a 70 kg human), there were no changes in functional observational battery observations. In mice, after a single dose of umbralisib at doses up to 500 mg/kg (equivalent to 1,500 mg/m2 or 2917 mg in a 70 kg human), there were dose-dependent decreases in tidal volume, respiration rate, and minute volume, which were considered minimal and non-adverse.
L168: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L169: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary for safety pharmacology. Additional details regarding the safety pharmacology assessments are provided below.
L170: ## A. Central Nervous System
L171: CD-1 mice that were part of the 26-week GLP toxicology study were subjected to a CNS functional observation battery (FOB). The animals were dosed with umbralisib daily with 30, 100, or 300 mg/kg. Overall, umbralisib had no significant effects on neurobehavioral function at doses up to 300 mg/kg.
L172: ## B. Respiratory
L173: In a study evaluating the effects on respiratory function (Study # 037274), CD-1 mice were administered a single oral dose of umbralisib (50, 150, or 500 mg/kg) or vehicle (0.5% methylcellulose and 10% PS80 in water). Respiratory measurements including tidal volume, respiratory rate, and derived minute volume, were taken at 0.25 through 8 hours after dosing. Compared to control conditions, umbralisib caused a 24% decrease in tidal volume at the high dose by the end of the testing period and transient decreases in respiratory rate (lowest of 36% and 38% at 1 hour for the 50 and 150 mg/kg groups, and 24% at 1.75 hours for the 500 mg/kg) and tidal volume at all doses tested (lowest of 11% at 2.25 hours for the 50 mg/kg group, and 15% at 4.25 hours for the 150 mg/kg group). Minute volumes in the umbralisib-treated conditions were at their relative lowest between 1 and 2 hours after dosing, with maximum differences of 43% and 47% for the 50 and 150 mg/kg groups at 1 hour, and 39% for the 500 mg/kg group at 1.75 hours.
L174: ## C. Cardiovascular
L175: An in vitro hERG channel inhibition study (Study # CYP0358-R10) with hERG-expressing cells incubated with umbralisib at concentrations up 100 µM showed that the test article had an IC50 greater than the highest dose tested. In vivo cardiovascular assessments were conducted as part of a 13-week repeat dose general toxicology study in dogs. ECG examinations showed that umbralisib at doses 30, 100, or 250 mg/kg did not result in any umbralisib-related effects on CV system parameters.
L176: ## 5.4. ADME/PK
L177: ## The Applicant's Position:
L178: After a single oral dose, umbralisib absorption was faster in mice, with a time to maximum concentration (T_{max}) of 0.5 hours, compared to 2, 7, and 3 hours in rats, dogs, and monkeys, respectively. The absolute oral bioavailability, ranked from highest to lowest, was &gt;100% in rats, 51.4% in dogs, 46.8% in mice, and 30.3% in monkeys. Following repeat oral administration to mice and dogs, the systemic exposures generally increased in a less-than-dose proportional manner over a dose range of 30 to 750 mg/kg/day in mice and 30 to 500 mg/kg/day in dogs. Little to no drug accumulation occurred in mice or rats, but 2- to 13-fold drug accumulation was apparent in dogs. No consistent sex-related effects were apparent in any animal species tested. Plasma protein binding of umbralisib ranged from 99.7% to 99.8% in animals and humans. In mice, umbralisib was widely distributed, with the highest concentrations in the gastrointestinal, metabolic/excretory, fatty, secretory, and endocrine tissues and the lowest concentration in brain, demonstrating penetration of umbralisib occurred in richly perfused organs, while passage to the CNS was limited. There was no meaningful ocular distribution. Fecal elimination was the primary excretion pathway.
L179: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L180: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's statement. See the review below for additional details regarding the PK studies.

L185: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L186: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L187: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L188: Umbralisib was well tolerated after a single dose, with a maximum tolerated dose (MTD) of &lt;1000 mg/kg/day in mice and &gt;2000 mg/kg/day in rats. The tolerability, potential toxicities, target organs, and toxicokinetics of umbralisib were evaluated in repeat-dose toxicity studies up to 26 weeks in mice and 39 weeks in dogs. Umbralisib was generally well tolerated through acute, subchronic, and chronic dosing; test article-related mortality occurred at \ge 100 mg/kg/day in mice (equivalent to 300 mg/m^2 or 584 mg in a 70 kg human), and adverse clinical findings necessitating dose reductions occurred at \ge 10 mg/kg/day in dogs (equivalent to 200 mg/m^2 or 389 mg/kg in a 70 kg human) when administered chronically.
L189: The liver was identified as the main target organ in mice and the gastrointestinal tract in dogs. Increased liver weights were observed in all studies in mice for the high-dose groups, but these findings did not correlate with increases in liver enzymes, and liver histopathology findings were minimal to mild. Another major finding was damage to the non-glandular stomach mucosa after subchronic and chronic administration, but the clinical relevance of these findings in humans cannot be determined, as humans do not have a non-glandular stomach. Other findings in mice included decreases in leukocytes and lymphocytes and increases in cholesterol that were considered minimal, non-adverse, or related to the pharmacologic action of umbralisib. All findings in mice were reversible upon treatment cessation. None of these findings were observed in dogs upon treatment with umbralisib. In dogs, diarrhea, body weight reductions, and decreased food consumption were noted in all studies and increased in severity and frequency in a dose-dependent manner. Dogs with body weight loss and decreased food consumption were offered canned food to increase appetite and food consumption. Dose holidays and dose reductions were necessitated during chronic administration due to low body condition scores. There were no gross pathology or histopathology findings that correlated to the clinical signs. Clinical pathology showed minor changes in metabolic parameters that were considered secondary to the clinical findings. There were no other adverse findings upon umbralisib treatment in dogs.
L190: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L191: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment; see additional information provided below for the 26-week and 39-week repeat-dose toxicity studies in mice and dogs, respectively.
L192: **Study title/Study number: A 26-week Study of TGR-1202 by Oral Gavage in Mice with a 28-day Recovery Period/20150163**
L193: ## Key Study Findings
L194: - There were 20 unscheduled deaths, with all but 1 occurring in the umbralisib-dosed groups and half occurring in the high-dose group. While umbralisib induced mortality occurred in a dose-dependent manner, the deaths were due to undetermined causes.
- Principle hematology findings included reduced lymphocyte levels in mid-dose and high-dose male and female animals.
- Hepatotoxicity: Liver weights, ALP levels, and microscopic signals (minimal necrosis, inflammation, and hypertrophy) were increased in umbralisib-dosed animals.
L195: Conducting laboratory and location: (b) (4)
L196: GLP compliance: Yes

Route of administration:	Oral gavage
Formulation/Vehicle:	0.5% (w/v) methylcellulose and 10% (v/v) Tween 80 in deionized water
Species/Strain:	CD-1 mice

L199: ## Observations and Results: changes from control
L200: Parameters	Major findings
Mortality	
There were 20 unscheduled deaths during the study.
Control: One control group female mouse was found dead on day 177.
LD: A total of 5 animals died or were euthanized with 3 of the 5 animals dying due to housing-related incidents unrelated to umbralisib. The other 2 were females euthanized on day 72. These animals exhibited ulcerative dermatitis, which was determined as the cause of death.
MD: A total of 4 animals died. Two of the 4 animals died due to housing-related incidents unrelated to umbralisib. The other 2 were females found dead on days 50 and 164. These animals had no macroscopic findings and microscopic findings, including inflammatory signals in the liver and lymph nodes, were not considered causal to their deaths by the Applicant's pathologist.

L207: ## **Study title/Study number: A 39-week Study of TGR-1202 by Oral Capsule in Dogs with a 28-day Recovery Period/20150164**
L208: ## **Key Study Findings**
L209: - Due to weight loss and thin appearance, most notably in the mid- and high-dose animals, all umbralisib-dosed groups had dose modifications. High-dose animals received 2 dose modifications, a dosing holiday, and early euthanization on day 183 (week 26).

L219: LD: low dose; MD: mid dose; HD: high dose
L220: ## Additional toxicology studies
L221: Below is a summary of the 13 week toxicology study in dogs, titled "TGR-1202: 13-Week Oral Toxicity Study in Dogs with a 30-Day Dose-Free Recovery" (Study 034717):
L222: Beagle dogs (3/sex/group) were administered umbralisib orally at doses of 0, 30, 100, or 250 mg/kg/day for 13 weeks. There were no test article-related mortalities. Treatment with umbralisib resulted in:
L223: - GI toxicities as presented with soft, mucoid, and/or liquid feces, and emesis.
- Hematological changes included reductions in RBC parameters (hemoglobin, hematocrit, and mean corpuscular hemoglobin concentration).
- Notable but reversible changes in clinical chemistry parameters were most evident in the HD group, with reductions in total protein, albumin, globins, electrolytes, cholesterol, and triglycerides. These changes may be the secondary effect of the clinical changes (weight loss, emesis, reduced food intake).
- Lower thymus and spleen weights with microscopic observations of thymic atrophy and lymphoid depletion of lymph nodes
- Lowered weights in the testes and epididymides. MD and HD males displayed testicular changes that included degeneration, multinucleated giant cells, vacuolation, and sloughed cells in the tubular lumen within the seminiferous epithelium.
L224: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L225: ## The Applicant's Position:
L226: Umbralisib was negative for mutagenic potential in an in vitro Ames assay at concentrations up to 5000 µg/plate and in an in vitro micronucleus assay. In vivo mutagenicity was also assessed in vivo after dosing CD-1 mice with 2 oral gavage doses of umbralisib up to 1000 mg/kg administered once daily. No increases in the scored micronuclei of reticulocytes in treated mice compared to control mice were detected under the study conditions.
L227: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L228: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Additional details regarding the studies are provided in the review below.

L250: ## 5.5.3. **Carcinogenicity**
L251: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L252: In accordance with the International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) guideline ICH S9 (2009), no carcinogenicity studies have been conducted because umbralisib is intended for use in patients with advanced cancer.
L253: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L254: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L255: ## 5.5.4. **Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology**
L256: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L257: The potential for umbralisib exposure to impact male and female fertility parameters and pregnancy parameters was evaluated in mice at doses up to 300 mg/kg/day. In male mice, umbralisib administration at 300 mg/kg/day (equivalent to 900 mg/m2 or 1707 mg in a 70-kg human) for 4 weeks resulted in reduced testis weights accompanied by reduced sperm count and motility. The magnitude of these changes was small and did not impact overall male reproductive health.
L258: The potential for umbralisib exposure to impact maternal health, pregnancy parameters, and embryonic development was evaluated in embryo-fetal development studies in mice and rabbits. In mice, doses \ge200 mg/kg (equivalent to \ge600 mg/m2 or \ge1138 mg in a 70 kg human) administered during the period of organogenesis resulted in no effects on maternal health but impacted pregnancy parameters (resorptions and post-implantation losses) and the developing fetus (increased incidence of cleft palate, nasopharyngeal fistula, and minor skeletal abnormalities). Treatment-related findings included an increase in the number of litters that exhibited 100% resorptions and post implantation resorption; and external (cleft palate), visceral (nasopharyngeal fistula), and skeletal fetal abnormalities. In rabbits, doses of 300 mg/kg (equivalent to 3600 mg/m2 or 6774 mg in a 70 kg human) administered during the period of organogenesis resulted in maternal toxicity of reduced body weights and food consumption with no other reported findings on pregnancy and fetal development in rabbits.
L259: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L260: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The FDA's review of the fertility and early embryonic development study in mice and embryo-fetal development studies in mice and rabbits is provided below.
L261: ## **Fertility and Early Embryonic Development**
L262: ## **Study title/ number:** TGR-1202: Study of Fertility and Early Embryonic Development in the CD-1 Mouse by Oral Gavage Administration/YC15PK
L263: - There were notable reductions in testis and epididymis weights in all umbralisib-dosed groups, reduced sperm counts in the 150 and 300 mg/kg/day groups, and reduced sperm motility in the 300 mg/kg/day group. These reductions were within historic ranges.
- Increased resorption and post-implantation loss were observed in the mid-dose group.
- While the fertility index is comparable to, or is higher than, the values in the concurrent control group, the low number of pregnancies in the concurrent control group makes it difficult to make a conclusion on the fertility index for umbralisib-treated animals
- The doses of 50, 150, and 300 mg/kg/day doses resulted in exposures that were 0.7, 1.3, and 1.9 times the clinical exposure at the recommended dose of 800 mg/day.

L319: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L320: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L321: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L322: Umbralisib is a small molecule inhibitor of phosphoinositide-3-kinase delta (PI3K\delta) and casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1\epsilon). The Applicant is seeking approval of umbralisib for the treatment of patients with marginal zone lymphoma (MZL) who have received at least one prior anti-CD20-based regimen. The proposed dosing regimen is 800 mg QD taken orally with food.
L323: The Clinical Pharmacology section of the NDA is supported by single and multiple dose pharmacokinetics (PK) characterization, population PK and exposure-response analyses, QT assessment, human mass balance, relative bioavailability, and in vivo food effect studies, in vitro metabolic and transport studies, and physiologically-based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modeling.
L324: The clinical pharmacology review focused on the appropriateness of the proposed dosing regimen and dose recommendations for patients with hepatic impairment, and drug-drug interactions.
L325: ## Recommendations:
L326: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information submitted in NDA213176(b)(4). This NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized in the table below.
L327: **Table 2: Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues by FDA**
L328: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and Supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from pivotal study UTX-TGR-205. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by the overall efficacy of umbralisib with an objective response rate (ORR) of 49% (95% CI: 37.0, 61.6) in patients with relapsed or refractory marginal zone lymphoma (MZL) who have received at least one prior anti-CD20-based regimens.
General dosing instructions	The proposed umbralisib dosing regimen of 800 mg given orally with food is acceptable for approval from a clinical pharmacology perspective:Favorable anti-tumor activity: This dosing regimen demonstrated an ORR of 49% in patients with MZL. There was a flat exposure-response relationship for ORR; efficacy data at lower dose levels (< 800 mg QD) were limited.
L329: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Positive exposure-response relationships for safety: Higher exposure resulted in higher incidence of adverse events, particularly for diarrhea and elevated ALT and AST levels.

Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	No dose adjustment is recommended for patients with mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le upper limit of normal [ULN] with AST > ULN or total bilirubin >1 to 1.5 \times ULN with any AST). There is no data available for patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment (>1.5 \times ULN and any AST).
No dose adjustment is recommended for patients with mild or moderate renal impairment (CLcr 30-89 mL/min). There is no data available for patients with severe renal impairment (CLcr 15-29 mL/min).
Based on PopPK analyses, no dose adjustment is required based on age (18 to 87 years old), sex, race (White and Black), and body weight (44 to 165 kg).

Drug-drug interactions	No clinically significant differences in umbralisib pharmacokinetics were observed when used concomitantly with omeprazole, a proton pump inhibitor.
The available data do not support the Applicant's claim that co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact the PK of P-gp sensitive substrates.
The available data do not support the Applicant's claim that co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact the PK of cytochrome (CYP) sensitive substrates.
The available data do not support the Applicant's claim that co-administration of strong CYP3A inhibitors is not expected to significantly impact the PK of umbralisib.
There is no data to evaluate the effect of CYP2C9 modulators on the PK of umbralisib.

Labeling	Overall, the proposed labeling recommendations are acceptable contingent upon the Applicant's agreement with the FDA revisions to the label. Clinical pharmacology labeling recommendations are detailed in Section 2.1, 8.6, 8.7, 12.2, and 12.3.
L330: ## *Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments*
L331: PMR or
PMC	Key Issue(s)
to be
Addressed	Rationale	Key Considerations for
Design Features
PMR	Umbralisib dose in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment	Hepatic metabolism plays a major role in the elimination of umbralisib. The recommended dose of umbralisib in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment (based on NCI-ODWG criteria) has not been established. The clinical study will determine whether dose adjustment is needed in this specific population.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to determine an appropriate safe dose of umbralisib in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment (based on NCI criteria).
PMR	The effect of strong CYP3A and moderate CYP2C9 inhibitors on the exposure of umbralisib	CYP3A and CYP2C9 plays a role in the elimination of umbralisib. The recommended dose of umbralisib when used concomitantly with a strong CYP3A or moderate CYP2C9 inhibitor has not been established. The clinical study will determine whether dose adjustment is needed in these patients.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of CYP3A and CYP2C9 inhibitors on the single dose pharmacokinetics of umbralisib to determine appropriate dosing recommendations.
PMR	The effect of umbralisib on the exposure of CYP and P-gp substrates	In vitro, umbralisib has the potential to inhibit CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4 and P-gp. However, repeated dosing of umbralisib on the PK of CYP and P-gp sensitive substrates in clinical trials is not fully characterized. The clinical study will evaluate the effect of repeated dosing of umbralisib on these CYP and P-gp sensitive substrates and address the potential for excessive drug toxicity.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of umbralisib on the single dose pharmacokinetics of sensitive CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A and P-gp substrates to address the potential for excessive drug toxicity.
L332: PMR or
PMC	Key Issue(s)
to be
Addressed	Rationale	Key Considerations for
Design Features
PMR	The effect of umbralisib on QT interval	The effect of umbralisib on the QT interval is not fully characterized as umbralisib exposure observed in TGR-1202-102 for QT assessment is 37% lower than the expected maximum therapeutic exposure (C_{max,ss}). Additional ECG data are needed to assess the risk of QTc prolongation with umbralisib.	The QT study can be conducted as a dedicated study or as a sub-study of ongoing trials to characterize QTc prolongation risk with umbralisib at steady-state maximal concentrations.
PMC	The effect of strong CYP3A inducers on the exposure of umbralisib	CYP3A (b) (4) plays a role in the elimination of umbralisib. The recommended dose of umbralisib (b) (4) when used concomitantly with a strong CYP3A (b) (4) inducer has not been established. The clinical study will determine whether dose adjustment is needed in these patients.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of CYP3A (b) (4) inducers on the single dose pharmacokinetics of umbralisib to determine appropriate dosing recommendations.
L333: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L334: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L335: ## The Applicant's Position:
L336: The clinical pharmacology of umbralisib has been well characterized. The data in the application to support the use of umbralisib 800 mg once daily (QD) in subjects with MZL comprise in vitro experiments using human biomaterials; clinical pharmacology studies with umbralisib in healthy subjects and subjects with cancer including single and repeat-dosing pharmacokinetic (PK) studies, biopharmaceutic studies (food effect), intrinsic factors (sex), extrinsic factors
L337: (drug-drug interactions [DDIs]); and population PK analyses.
L338: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L339: The FDA does not agree that the clinical pharmacology of umbralisib has been well characterized. The PK of umbralisib in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment, potential drug interactions of umbralisib as a perpetrator of CYP enzymes (CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4) and P-gp and a victim (CYP3A and CYP2C9 inhibitors and inducers), and the effect of umbralisib on the QT interval are not well characterized. Additional studies are needed (see Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments, Section 13).
L340: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L341: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L342: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L343: During the course of the development of umbralisib, absorption, distribution, metabolism, excretion, and DDI properties were evaluated. The appropriateness of umbralisib for the MZL indication was evaluated in the exposure-response (E-R) analyses for efficacy and safety across five dose levels: 400, 600, 800, 1000, and 1200 mg. The pooled population pharmacokinetics (PPK)/E-R analyses evaluated biologically inter-dependent endpoints of efficacy and safety. Overall, the dose-response results demonstrate an acceptable balance between benefits and risk at the recommended dose of 800 mg and justify the use of this dose regimen in patients with MZL.
L344: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L345: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the recommended dosing regimen of 800 mg QD with food for patients with MZL is reasonable based on the flat E-R relationship for efficacy and positive E-R relationships for safety.
L346: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L347: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L348: No therapeutic individualization is needed in the proposed indication based on demographic factors (age, sex, race, body weight), DDIs (use of acid-reducing agent [ARA], concomitant use of strong 3A4 inhibitor, P-glycoprotein [P-gp] substrates), or in special populations of mild hepatic impairment or mild and moderate renal impairment. The impact of umbralisib on moderate and severe hepatic impairment and severe renal impairment has not been investigated.
L349: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L350: Based on the population PK (PPK) analysis, FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is needed for demographic factors (age, sex, race and body weight) and patients
L351: with mild to moderate renal impairment. However, the FDA does not agree with the Applicant's position regarding the dose recommendations for concomitant use of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors based on the PPK analysis.
L352: In the Applicant's evaluation of the impact of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, the frequency and dosing time of the co-administered strong CYP3A4 inhibitors were not available. In addition, in a pooled PK analysis (Study TGR-1202-101 and Study UTX-TGR-205) of 10 patients who were co-administered strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, only 4 patients had intense PK data available, while 6 patients had sparse PK of 1-3 PK samples. Based on the limited PK data and information on the administration time of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, the Applicant's current conclusion that no dose adjustment would be needed for concomitant administration of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors is not acceptable.
L353: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is needed for patients with mild hepatic impairment. In the Applicant's PPK evaluation of the impact of mild hepatic impairment, PK data were only available for 6 patients with mild hepatic impairment (classified by NCI-ODWG criteria). Out of the 6 patients, 4 patients had only one PK datapoint. The available data were insufficient to use the PPK approach to assess the effect of mild hepatic impairment on the PK of umbralalisib; however, the observed dose-normalized umbralalisib concentrations from patients with mild hepatic impairment were within the range of those from patients with normal hepatic function (Figure 1), supporting no dose adjustment in patients with mild hepatic impairment. There are no data for patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment.
L354: **Figure 1: Comparison of umbralalisib observed concentrations between patients with normal hepatic function and mild hepatic impairment**
L355: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L356: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L357: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L358: None
L359: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L360: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that there are no outstanding issues. The PK characterization of umbralisib in patients with hepatic impairment, potential drug interactions of umbralisib as a perpetrator of CYP enzymes (CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4) and P-gp and a victim (CYP3A and CYP2C9 inhibitors and inducers), and the effect of umbralisib on the QT interval are not well characterized. Refer to the Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments, Section 13.
L361: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L362: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L363: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L364: Based on comprehensive clinical pharmacology evaluations of umbralisib, the following characteristics of umbralisib were observed.
L365: ## **Absorption and Pharmacokinetics:**
L366: - From Study TGR-1202-101, the observed median T_{max} at 800 mg was 2.1 and 3.9 hours on Days 1 and 29 of dosing, respectively.
- Linear PK was observed over a dose range of 200 to 1000 mg by non-compartmental analysis and 200 to 1800 mg by PPK analysis, although the number of subjects at 1800 mg was limited (n=2).
- Following repeat dosing at 800 mg, the observed accumulation ratio for area under the plasma/serum concentration-time curve (AUC) and maximum concentration (C_{max}) were 5.2- and 2.8-fold, respectively; the PPK model-estimated median steady state accumulation ratio for AUC was 6.4-fold (95% confidence interval [CI]: 3.7, 10.9). Ninety percent of the AUC at steady state was achieved after 35.5 doses (95% CI: 20, 70) of 800 mg QD.
- The PPK model-estimated relative oral bioavailability of drug product (DP)
L367: containing (b) (4) drug substance (DS) was 193% compared to DP containing (b) (4) DS.
L368: ## **Distribution:**
L369: - The geometric mean (percentage coefficient of variation [CV%]) apparent volume of distribution at steady state in healthy subjects was 1900 L/h (38%). This value is approximately 30 times greater than the volume of total body water in humans (Davies and Morris 1993), suggesting wide tissue distribution of umbralisib. This observation is expected for a Biopharmaceutics Classification System Class 2 compound, such as umbralisib.
- Umbralisib plasma protein binding was high in humans at concentrations of 2 (99.7%) and 5 (99.8%) \mu\text{mol/L}. The PPK model-estimated C_{\text{max}} at steady state and minimum plasma concentration at steady state (C_{\text{min,ss}}) were 11 and 8 \mu\text{mol/L} (6.3 and 4.7 \mu\text{g/mL}), respectively.
L370: ## **Elimination:**
L371: ## **Metabolism:**
L372: - In plasma, umbralisib was minimally metabolized. In a radiolabeled mass balance study, unchanged umbralisib was the predominant radioactive component found in plasma (79.2% of total radioactivity area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero to 48 hours post-dose [AUC_{0-48}]) with 2 minor metabolites identified. Each plasma metabolite represented &lt;5% of total radioactive AUC_{0-48}.
- The proposed metabolites were formed by O-de-alkylation, mono-oxidation, methylation, oxidative defluorination, hydrogenation, glucuronidation, or combinations thereof.
L373: ## **Excretion:**
L374: - Hepatobiliary elimination was the major excretion pathway for umbralisib, with fecal excretion accounting for 81% and urine excretion accounting for 3% of the total radioactive dose administered orally.
- In Study TGR-1202-904, the geometric mean (CV%) umbralisib apparent clearance was 8.72 (31%) L, with an apparent terminal phase half-life of 151 (33%) hours.
L375: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L376: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment on general pharmacology and pharmacokinetic characteristics of umbralisib.
L377: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L378: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that umbralisib is minimally metabolized. Based on data from the human mass balance study (TGR-1202-902), the majority of radioactivities in feces and urine were observed to be metabolites. Thus, umbralisib is mainly eliminated through metabolic pathways.
L379: Differences in PK exposure was observed between healthy subjects and patients. Based on the PPK analysis, healthy subjects (n = 24) appeared to have 30% lower relative bioavailability (F) in Study 1102 compared to subjects with hematological malignancies in Study 101 (n=83). Central volume of distribution (Vc/F) in healthy subjects (n=24) appeared to be 79.5% lower than those in patients (n = 223) with hematological malignancies. The impact of food effect and formulations (b)(4) on relative bioavailability was taken into account in the PPK analysis and could not fully explain the exposure difference observed between Study 101 and Study 205. The rationale for the difference is currently unknown.
L380: The general overview of umbralisib ADME and clinical PK information as assessed by FDA are presented in the table below.
L381: **Table 3: Highlights of Clinical Pharmacology for umbralisib**

L383: Pharmacology
Mechanism of Action	Umbralisib is a small molecule inhibitor of phosphoinositide-3-kinase delta (PI3Kδ) and casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1ε). The mechanism of action (MOA) involves competitive binding of umbralisib to PI3Kδ and CK1ε, inhibiting cell proliferation and inducing apoptosis of lymphoma and leukemic cell lines and primary tumor cells.
Active Moieties	Umbralisib was observed to be the major active species in plasma (79.2%).
QT/QTc Prolongation	Umbralisib exhibits no large mean increases in the QTc interval (i.e., >20 msec). However, the effect of umbralisib on the QT interval is not fully characterized as umbralisib exposure observed in TGR-1202-102 for QTc assessment is 37% lower than that reported by the PPK model. The effect of food and formulation differences in clinical trials and bioanalytical assays were not considered factors for this PK differences.
General Information
Bioanalysis	
Two validated PK assays were used to measure umbralisib concentrations in human plasma (MET126) and serum (MET193). A cross-validation study of plasma and serum (MET126 Addendum 1) indicated 12.5% bias between the methods, which is not a significant factor. The majority of PK data were obtained from plasma PK samples (91.6%).
A summary of the method validation reports is included in Appendix 19.4.1.

Healthy Volunteers vs. Patients	Based on population PK analysis, F and Vc/F in healthy subjects were 30% and 79.5% lower when compared to those in patients with hematological malignancies. The reason for this difference is currently unknown.
Drug exposure at steady state following the therapeutic dosing regimen	In adult patients with hematological malignancies who received umbralisib 800 mg QD with food, geometric steady-state (CV%) C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of umbralisib were 7.3 (39%) µg/mL and 141 (46%) µg*h/mL, respectively, based on the PPK model. Noncompartmental pooled PK analysis was not conducted as there were a limited number of patients (n=3) with intense PK sampling available in Phase 1 portion of Study TGR-1202-101.
Minimal effective dose or exposure	Not determined.
L384: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L385: Maximal tolerated dose or exposure	In Study TGR-1202-101, the MTD of umbralisib as a single-agent treatment in patients with hematological malignancies was not reached at doses up to 1800 mg due to PK saturation in absorption.
Dose Proportionality	Umbralisib(b)(4) appears to show dose-proportionality for both C_{max} and AUC in the dose range of 200 to 1000 mg following a single dose and at steady state.
Accumulation	The median accumulation ratios (R_{acc}) following umbralisib 800 mg QD with food was approximately 6.4-fold for AUC_{tau} and 3.8-fold for C_{max} based on PPK analysis.
Variability	Based on PPK analysis, the inter-subject variability (CV%) of apparent clearance (CL/F) and central volume of distribution (V_c/F) were 51.9% and 185%, respectively.
Absorption
Bioavailability	Absolute bioavailability study was not conducted.
T_{max}	The median time to reach peak plasma concentration (T_{max}) of umbralisib following 800 mg QD with food was about 3.9 hours.
L386: Relative Bioavailability
(b) (4)	Study TGR-1202-PK102 was an open-label, randomized, 2-way crossover study in 26 healthy subjects to evaluate the PK of umbralisib with (b) (4) tablets. The results showed that the exposure of umbralisib was increased 60% and 2.2-fold for AUC and C_{max}, respectively, following a single dose of 200 mg (b) (4) tablet and a single dose of 200 mg (b) (4) tablet in healthy subjects under fasting condition (Table 5).

L387: Food effect (Fed/fasted)	
There were two studies (TGR-1202-PK101 and TGR-1202-908) evaluating the effect of food on the PK of umbralisib with two different formulations (b) (4).
Study TGR-1202-PK101 was an open-label, single-dose, randomized, crossover study to determine whether high-fat, high calorie food has an effect on the PK of umbralisib in 24 healthy subjects who received a single dose of 200 mg (b) (4) tablet. Results showed that the exposure of umbralisib was increased 67% and 2.7-fold for AUC and C_{max}, respectively, with food following a single dose of 200 mg (b) (4) tablet in healthy subjects (Table 6).

L388: Proton Pump Inhibitor (PPI) effect (omeprazole)	
Study TGR-1202-904 was an open-label, single-dose, crossover study to determine whether repeated doses of omeprazole (40 mg QD for 4 consecutive days), a proton pump inhibitor (PPI), has an effect on the PK of umbralisib in 18 healthy subjects who received a single dose of 800 mg (b)(4) tablet under fed conditions. Results showed that there was no clinically meaningful PK changes on the exposure of umbralisib, following a single dose of 800 mg (b)(4) tablet in healthy subjects with food (Table 8).
