Distribution	
Volume of distribution	
Based on the PPK analysis, the geometric mean apparent volume of distribution of the central compartment (Vc/F) of umbralisib was estimated to be 312 L in patients with hematological malignancies.

Plasma protein binding	
In vitro, protein binding of umbralisib was 99.7-99.8%.

Blood to plasma ratio	
The mean blood-to-plasma ratio was 0.61-0.64 for umbralisib.

Elimination	
Half-life	
Following a single 800 mg dose in healthy subjects, the mean apparent elimination half-life (t1/2) of umbralisib was approximately 110 hours based on noncompartmental analysis.
In patients with hematological malignancies who received 800 mg QD, the geometric mean effective t1/2 of umbralisib was approximately 91 hours (42% CV) based on PPK analysis.
L389: Clearance	Based on the PPK analysis, the geometric mean apparent clearance of umbralisib was estimated to be 15.5 L/h in patients with hematological malignancies.
Metabolism
Primary metabolic pathway(s)	In vitro, umbralisib appears to be metabolized by CYP3A4 and CYP2C9 and to a lesser extent CYP1A2. The metabolic assay [low incubation time (1 hr) of umbralisib with human recombinant P450 enzymes] was not adequate to determine the exact enzyme contribution due to low hepatic extraction of umbralisib.
Inhibitor/Inducer	In vitro,Umbralisib inhibits CYP2C8 (IC_{50} value of 10.6 \mumol/L), CYP2C9 (IC_{50} value of 3.4 \mumol/L), CYP2C19 (IC_{50} value of 13.5 \mumol/L), and CYP3A4 (IC_{50} value of 3.9 to 4.6 \mumol/L), but does not inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP2D6.
Umbralisib induces CYP3A4.
Umbralisib is not a substrate to P-gp, BCRP, OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, MATE1, and MATE2-K.
Umbralisib inhibits P-gp (IC_{50} value of 0.0653 \mumol/L).

Excretion
Primary excretion pathways (% dose) \pmSD	In the human mass balance Study TGR-1202-902 with mean total radioactivity recovery of 57.0-86.8% over 600 hours period, the mean radioactivity recovery in feces and urine in 6 healthy subjects were 81.0% (16.9% unchanged umbralisib) and 3.2%, respectively.
L390: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L391: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L392: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L393: Yes, at 800 mg QD, a 61% objective response rate (ORR) was observed among subjects with MZL for whom PK data was available for E-R analysis, and 59% of subjects with MZL achieved &gt;50% decrease in target lesion dimensions. The E-R model predicted ORR rate was 56.9 (90% CI: 43.2, 69.8) at 800 mg. The model predicted percent change from baseline (%CfB) (90% CI) in target lesion dimensions was -52.3% (-60.1, -44.3). There were no significant (p&gt;0.05) exposure-efficacy relationships for ORR, duration of response (DOR), PFS, time to response (TTR), and %CfB in subjects with MZL. The PPK model-predicted C_{min,ss} (bound and unbound
L394: umbralisib) after 800 mg QD was at least 7-fold higher than the concentration achieving 90% of maximal inhibition of PI3K\delta inhibition for the MZL population.
L395: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L396: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that flat exposure-response relationships for efficacy were observed for ORR, DOR, PFS, TTR and %Cfb of target lesion dimensions in patients with MZL.
L397: ## **6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?**
L398: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L399: Yes, the proposed dose of 800 mg QD is effective and generally well tolerated with no dosing difference observed in PK for age, sex, race, body weight, use of ARA, concomitant use of strong 3A4 inhibitor, mild hepatic impairment, or mild and moderate renal impairment.
L400: During umbralisib development, 800 mg under fed conditions was implemented as the clinical dose for Phase II and later studies. The appropriateness of umbralisib for the MZL indication was evaluated in the PK-efficacy relationships across several dose/exposure levels, including the clinical dose of 800 mg. This assessment used the final population PK model for all efficacy and safety endpoints. The integrated view of absorption, distribution, metabolism, excretion, and DDI properties of umbralisib and the dose-exposure-response relationships showed consistent trends that support the proposed clinical dose of 800 mg for patients with MZL.
L401: Exposure-response analysis indicated no positive exposure-response relationship between average steady-state concentration and adverse events of special interest (AESIs), with the exception of diarrhea (any grade) and elevated alanine aminotransferase (ALT)/aspartate aminotransferase (AST) (any grade and Grade \ge3). Exposure-corrected QT interval (QTc) analyses indicated no cardiac safety-related risk at the recommended umbralisib dose of 800 mg or at a supratherapeutic dose of 1200 mg. Other additional covariate analyses supported no dose adjustment for subjects with hepatic or renal impairment.
L402: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L403: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposed dosing regimen for the general patient population. However, the FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that there is no risk of QTc prolongation with umbralisib. The Applicant's exposure-corrected QT interval (QTc) analyses were conducted using steady state exposure (C_{max,ss}) data in Study TGR-1202-102. The geometric mean C_{max} on Cycle 2 Day 1 was 4012 ng/mL (n=20), which was lower than the therapeutic exposure (7266 ng/mL) obtained from PPK analysis. The 1200 mg QD dose group in Study TGR-1202-102 did not provide higher exposure (4172 ng/mL) as compared to the 800 mg QD dose group. Thus, the Applicant's QT assessment was not adequate to extrapolate the risk of QTc prolongation to the higher exposure range. The FDA agrees that there were no large
L404: mean increases (i.e., 20 msec) with umbralisib treatment.
L405: ## 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L406: ## The Applicant's Position:
L407: No. Based on the assessment of intrinsic factors, no dose adjustment or change in regimen is required based on demographics or in special populations.
L408: **Specific Populations:** No clinically significant differences in the PK of umbralisib were observed based on age (&lt;65 or ≥65 years old), sex, race (White or non-White), body weight (&lt;78.5 or ≥78.5 kg), or mild and moderate renal impairment (estimated glomerular filtration rate [eGFR] 60 to &lt;90 or 30 to &lt;60 mL/min, respectively, estimated by Modification of Diet in Renal Disease equation). The effect of severe renal impairment (eGFR &lt;30 mL/min) and dialysis on umbralisib PK is unknown.
L409: **Hepatic Impairment:** Umbralisib is minimally metabolized in the liver. No significant difference in the PK of umbralisib was observed in patients with mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt;1 to 1.5 × upper limit of normal and any AST/ALT, by National Cancer Institute - Organ Dysfunction Working Group criteria). The effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on umbralisib PK is unknown.
L410: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L411: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is required based on age (18 to 87 years old), sex, race [83.8% (n=207) White and 5.7% (n=14) Black], body weight (44 to 165 kg) or mild to moderate renal impairment (CLcr 30 to 89 mL/min, estimated by the Cockcroft-Gault equation). In the Applicant's PPK evaluation of the impact of mild hepatic impairment, only 6 patients with mild hepatic impairment were included. Of the 6 patients, 4 patients had only 1 available PK sample for analysis. The available data were insufficient to use the PPK approach to assess the effect of mild hepatic impairment on the PK of umbralisib; however, the observed dose-normalized umbralisib concentrations from patients with mild hepatic impairment were within the range of those with normal hepatic function (Figure 1), supporting no adjustment in patients with mild hepatic impairment.
L412: ## 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L413: ## The Applicant's Position:
L414: The clinically relevant food-drug interactions and DDIs with concomitant medications are described in further detail below with their respective recommendation for management.
L415: **Food:** Umbralisib is recommended to be taken with food as administration of a single dose with a high-fat meal in healthy subjects increased AUC and C_{max} of umbralisib by 61% and 115%, respectively, relative to fasting condition.
L416: ## **Effect of Other Drugs on Umbralisib:**
L417: **ARAs:** Repeat dosing of omeprazole, an ARA, had no apparent impact on the exposure of a single dose of umbralisib. The area under the plasma/serum concentration-time curve from time zero to the time of the last quantifiable concentration (AUC_{0-t}) and area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero to infinity (AUC_{0-inf}) were both increased by 20% and the C_{max} decreased by 8% in the coadministration group, relative to the umbralisib alone group.
L418: **Cytochrome P450 (CYP)3A4 Inhibitors or Inducers:** PPK analysis revealed that concomitant use of CYP3A4 inhibitors with umbralisib did not impact the PK of umbralisib. Use of strong CYP3A4 inducers (n=9) on the PK of umbralisib is unknown.
L419: **Transporter Systems:** Umbralisib was not a substrate of organic anion transporter (OAT)1, OAT3, organic cation transporter (OCT)2, organic anion transport protein (OATP)1B1, OATP1B3, multidrug and toxic compound extrusion transporter (MATE)1, MATE2 K, P-gp, and breast cancer resistance protein. Thus, no impact on the PK of umbralisib is expected with concomitant use of these transporter substrates.
L420: ## **Effect of Umbralisib on Other Drugs:**
L421: **P-gp Substrates:** Umbralisib increased the absorption rate of fexofenadine (a sensitive P-gp substrate) producing a 42% higher C_{max} and a 1 hour decrease of T_{max} in the coadministration group relative to the fexofenadine alone group. However, the overall extent of fexofenadine exposure was unaffected, with the AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf} increased by 9% to 10%, respectively, in the coadministration group relative to the fexofenadine alone group. Therefore, co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact the PK of P-gp substrates.
L422: **CYP3A4 Substrates:** Simultaneous simulation using a physiologically-based PK (PBPK) model suggested umbralisib was a weak (\ge 1.25- to \le 2-fold changes in C_{max} and AUC ratios, respectively) inhibitor. Specifically, multiple doses of umbralisib increased AUC and C_{max} of single dose midazolam 1.56- and 1.52-fold, respectively. These simulated exposure increases of midazolam suggested that umbralisib did not induce CYP3A4.
L423: **CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 Substrates:** Simulation using PBPK modeling suggested that multiple doses of umbralisib did not impact the exposures of single doses of repaglinide (CYP2C8 substrate), tolbutamide (CYP2C9 substrate), or S-mephenytoin (CYP2C19 substrate). Specifically, the predicted C_{max} and AUC ratios with and without simulated coadministration of the probe substrate for each CYP isozyme were &lt;1.25-fold under simulated steady state conditions of umbralisib.
L424: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L425: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that umbralisib should be taken with food.
L426: ## **Effect of omeprazole on PK of umbralisib:**
L427: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is required for the use of omeprazole.
L428: ## **Effect of umbralisib on PK of P-gp substrates:**
L429: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact the PK of P-gp substrates. In Study TGR-1202-901, the study design is only sufficient to examine the effect of intestinal P-gp inhibition as only a single oral dose of umbralisib (800 mg with food) and fexofenadine (180 mg) were administered to healthy subjects. However, in vitro data suggest that umbralisib inhibits P-gp with an IC_{50} value of 0.064 \mu\text{mol/L}. Umbralisib has a high accumulation ratio (6.4-fold), a long half-life (effective half-life of 91 hours), and a small fluctuation between C_{\text{max}} and C_{\text{min}} (0.008 to 0.011 \mu\text{mol/mL}) at steady state. Under a worst case scenario when steady state of umbralisib is reached, there is a high potential that systemic inhibition of P-gp (liver and kidney) may affect the PK of P-gp sensitive substrates. Thus, the current data do not support the claim that umbralisib does not affect the PK of P-gp sensitive substrates.
L430: ## **Effect of strong CYP3A inhibitors on PK of umbralisib:**
L431: The FDA disagrees with Applicant's position that co-administration of strong CYP3A inhibitors is not expected to significantly impact the PK of umbralisib. In the Applicant's evaluation of the impact of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, the frequency and dosing time of the co-administered strong CYP3A4 inhibitors were not available. In addition, of the 10 patients who were co-administered strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, only 4 patients had intense PK, while 6 patients only had sparse PK data of 1-3 PK samples. Based on the limited PK data and information on the administration time of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, the Applicant's current conclusion that no dose adjustment would be needed for concomitant administration of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors is not acceptable.
L432: ## **Effect of umbralisib on PK of CYP substrates:**
L433: The FDA disagrees with Applicant's position that co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact CYP substrates. The effect of umbralisib on CYP3A, CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 substrates could not be confidently predicted using the PBPK modeling approach due to uncertainties about the in vitro to in vivo extrapolation. The potential of drug-drug interactions of umbralisib with substrates of these enzymes cannot be ruled out.

L470: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralalisib)
L471: Abbreviations: CLL, chronic lymphocytic leukemia; CR, complete response; DCR, disease control rate; DLBCL, diffuse large B-cell lymphoma; DLT, dose-limiting toxicity; DOR, duration of response; ECG, electrocardiogram; ECOG, Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group; FL, follicular lymphoma; FOLFOX, 5-fluorouracil/leucovorin/oxaliplatin; HL, Hodgkin lymphoma; iNHL, indolent NHL; IRC, Independent Review Committee; IV, intravenous; MCL, mantle cell lymphoma; MR, minor response; MTD, maximum tolerated dose; MZL, marginal zone lymphoma; NCT, National Clinical Trial; NHL, non-Hodgkin lymphoma; No., number; ORR, overall response rate; PFS, progression-free survival; PD, progressive disease; P-gp, P-glycoprotein; PI3K, phosphoinositide 3-kinase; PK, pharmacokinetic(s); PO, oral; PR, partial response; PTCL, peripheral T-cell lymphoma; QD, once daily; SLL, small lymphocytic lymphoma; TEAE, treatment-emergent adverse event; TTR, time to response; U2, ublituximab+umbralalisib; UK, United Kingdom; US, United States; VGPR, very good partial response; WM, Waldenstrom's macroglobulinemia.
L472: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L473: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L474: The clinical review of efficacy was primarily based on an analysis of the single-arm cohort of study UTX-TGR-205, an open-label, multi-center, multi-cohort trial (NCT02793583) that included 69 patients with relapsed or refractory MZL.
L475: The review of safety is primarily based on the pooled data from 371 patients with hematologic malignancies in three single-arm, open-label trials (Study TGR-1202-101, TGR-1202-202, and UTX-TGR-205) and one open-label extension trial (Study UTX-TGR-501). Of note, data from UTX-TGR-103, described in the table above, was not included in the NDA submission.
L476: All major efficacy and safety analyses were reproduced or audited. Statistical analyses by the reviewers were performed using SAS/JMP 13.0 (SAS Institute, Inc., Cary, NC) and MedDRA-Based Adverse Event Diagnostics (MAED) 1.8 (Enterprise Performance and Lifecycle System Design).
L477: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L478: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L479: The efficacy of umbralisib in subjects with MZL was evaluated in a Phase I study, TGR-1202-101, and 2 Phase II single-arm studies, UTX-TGR-205 and TGR-1202-202. The pivotal study to characterize the efficacy of umbralisib in MZL is the ongoing Phase IIb Study UTX-TGR-205; efficacy data of subjects with MZL are summarized as of the cutoff date of 30 November 2019. Efficacy data from Study TGR-1202-101 and Study TGR-1202-202 are used as supportive information.
L480: ## 8.1.1. Study UTX-TGR-205
L481: ## Trial Design
L482: ## The Applicant's Description:
L483: ## Basic Study Design
L484: Study UTX-TGR-205 is an ongoing, Phase IIb, randomized study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of single agent umbralisib and umbralisib in combination with ublituximab with or without bendamustine in subjects with previously treated iNHL, mantle cell lymphoma (MCL), and diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL). Subjects were enrolled into 1 of 4 cohorts (MZL, follicular lymphoma [FL] and small lymphocytic lymphoma [SLL], MCL, or DLBCL) based on tumor type.
L485: ## Trial Location
L486: This study is being conducted at 83 sites in the US and 38 sites outside of the US (10 sites in Poland, 6 sites in the United Kingdom, 5 sites each in Spain and South Korea, 4 sites each in Israel and Italy, 3 sites in Australia, and 1 site in Slovakia).
L487: ## Choice of Control Group
L488: On the basis of the disease types and settings being studied, an open-label Phase II design without an active controlled standard of care arm was selected.
L489: ## Diagnostic Criteria
L490: Subjects were enrolled with histologically confirmed diagnosis of B-cell NHL with histological subtype MZL (splenic, nodal, or extranodal) based on criteria established by
L491: the World Health Organization 2008 classification of tumors of hematopoietic and lymphoid tissues.
L492: ## Key Inclusion/Exclusion Criteria
L493: Male or female subjects \ge 18 years of age with MZL were required to have prior treatment with 1 or more lines of therapy, including at least 1 anti-CD20-based regimen (either as monotherapy or chemoimmunotherapy). Additional requirements included documented failure to achieve at least partial response (PR) or documented progressive disease (PD) after the most recent systemic treatment regimen and must have documented evidence for requiring treatment (1 or more of the following: bulky disease [&gt;5 cm], high lactate dehydrogenase level, B symptoms, threatened organ function, splenomegaly, cytopenias due to lymphoma, or effusions).
L494: Subjects with MZL also had to have measurable disease, defined as at least 1 measurable disease lesion &gt;1.5 cm in at least 1 diameter by computed tomography (CT), positron emission tomography (PET)-CT, or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) in an area of no prior radiation therapy, or in an area that was previously irradiated that has documented progression.
L495: Subjects were excluded if they were receiving cancer therapy (i.e., chemotherapy, radiation therapy, immunotherapy, biologic therapy, hormonal therapy, surgery, and/or tumor embolization) or any investigational drug within 21 days of Day 1 of Cycle 1 or had prior exposure to drugs that specifically inhibit PI3K.
L496: ## Dose Selection
L497: Based on the results from a Phase I dose-escalation study of umbralisib (TGR-1202-101) and 2 Phase I PK studies in healthy volunteers (TGR-1202-PK101 and TGR 1202-PK102), a dose of 800 mg QD of umbralisib under fed condition was selected.
L498: ## Study Treatments
L499: Umbralisib 800 mg QD was taken orally with food during all cycles in the study. Umbralisib was to be continued daily until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal from the study for other reasons.
L500: ## Assignment to Study Treatment
L501: In Stage 1, eligible MZL subjects received single agent umbralisib.
L502: ## Blinding
L503: This was an open-label study.
L504: ## Dose Modification
L505: For adverse events (AEs) suspected to be related to umbralisib, management guidance and dose delays and/or modifications were provided in the protocol. Supportive care was to be considered for any subject who experiences Grade \ge 2 cytopenias, or Grade \ge 1 non-hematologic toxicities. If a subject experienced a treatment-related AE requiring dose modification during the course of therapy, then study drug administration was to be held, as necessary, until the AE resolves or stabilizes to an acceptable degree.
L506: ## Administrative Structure
L507: The best clinical response as well as disease progression was determined by an Independent Central Radiology Group, also referred to as an Independent Review Committee (IRC).
L508: An independent Data Safety Monitoring Board was established to advise the Sponsor on safety for subjects enrolled in the study. Given that this was an open-label, non-comparator Phase II trial, the Sponsor evaluated preliminary efficacy and safety data periodically to guide continued development of ublituximab and umbralisib.
L509: ## Procedures and Schedule
L510: During the study period, all subjects were evaluated for response by CT, PET-CT, and/or MRI at the end of Cycle 3 and then within 14 days prior to Day 1 of Cycles 6, 9, and 12. After Cycle 12, response assessments occurred every 6 cycles or more frequently. Subjects remained on study drug until the occurrence of definitive disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal from the study due to Investigator decision or other reasons. Subjects who discontinued from study drug for reasons other than disease progression were followed for progression.
L511: ## Treatment Compliance
L512: Study drug compliance was reviewed with the subject at the beginning of each new treatment cycle and as needed.
L513: ## Subject Completion
L514: Subjects continued on study treatment until the occurrence of definitive disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal from the study due to Investigator
L515: decision or other reasons. Subjects who discontinued from study treatment (either for toxicity or physician choice) and have not progressed were followed for progression.
L516: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L517: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L518: ## Study Endpoints
L519: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L520: The study endpoints ORR and PFS are all accepted and well-recognized for oncology trials. The primary efficacy outcome, ORR, was defined as the percentage of subjects who achieved CR or PR, according to the Revised Criteria for Malignant Lymphoma (Cheson 2007). The primary analysis for ORR was based on the IRC assessments.
L521: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L522: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position and has the following comment. In the absence of a control arm and due to the open-label single arm design of the trial, it is difficult to interpret and is unlikely to provide evidence to support the time-to-event endpoints, PFS and OS. The major limitation is that the measurements in isolation are out of context and comparing them to historical controls is prone to biases. The durability of the ORR will be assessed by using the duration of response.
L523: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L524: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L525: The Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) was agreed upon and finalized prior to the conduct of any efficacy analysis.
L526: ## Analysis Populations
L527: The intent-to-treat (ITT) Population included all subjects who were enrolled in the MZL cohort (Stage 1) and who received at least 1 dose of single agent umbralisib. The ITT Population was used for the efficacy analyses of ORR, DOR, PFS, CR, and TTR. To be included in the ITT population, an MZL subject must have:
L528: - been enrolled in the MZL cohort Stage 1 (last enrollment 31 Aug 2019) to receive single agent umbralisib, and indicated as MZL at Screening
- received at least 1 dose of study drug
- had 1 or more prior lines of systemic therapy including an anti-CD20 regimen.
L529: The Per Protocol (PP) Population included all ITT subjects without major protocol
L530: deviations related to efficacy parameters and who had at least 1 post-baseline response assessment.
L531: ## Interim Analysis
L532: This was an interim analysis based on Amendment 7, Version 5.1 of the study protocol as of the data cutoff date of 30 November 2019. Results from the following analyses were presented in the interim clinical study report (CSR):
L533: - Efficacy assessments of single agent umbralisib in MZL cohort (Stage 1)
- Safety assessments for single agent umbralisib and by disease type, if appropriate
L534: ## Efficacy Analysis
L535: The primary efficacy outcome was ORR defined as the percent of subjects who achieved CR or PR, according to the Revised Criteria for Malignant Lymphoma (Cheson 2007). The primary analysis for ORR was conducted in the ITT population based on the IRC assessments. The estimated response rate and the exact 2-sided 95% CI (Clopper-Pearson method) were presented. Waterfall plots of maximum percentage decrease in sum of the products of longest diameters (SPD) by IRC assessment was presented, also showing MZL subtypes. Additionally, the Investigator assessments were summarized in supportive analyses, and agreement in best overall response between IRC and Investigator assessments was also summarized.
L536: Subject follow-up time (months) was calculated as follows: (date of efficacy data cutoff - date of first dosing +1)/30.4375.
L537: The DOR was defined as the interval from the first documentation of CR or PR to the earlier of the first documentation of definitive disease progression or death from any cause. The DOR was calculated based on subjects who achieved a response. For subjects who responded but never progressed in the study, the DOR was censored at the time of last tumor assessment using the same censoring algorithm as for PFS. The DOR was summarized by Kaplan-Meier method.
L538: The TTR was defined as the interval from enrollment to the first documentation of CR or PR. The Kaplan-Meier estimate for TTR was presented for the ITT population only in subjects who achieved CR or PR.
L539: PFS was defined as the time from the date of enrollment until the date of first documentation of definitive disease progression or date of death from any cause, whichever occurred first. The median duration of PFS and the proportion of subjects who were progression-free at 6, 12, and 18 months were estimated using the Kaplan-
L540: Meier method. For each estimate, a 95% CI was reported. Additional sensitivity analyses were also performed for PFS.
L541: Efficacy endpoints were examined for subgroups of the ITT population. The same methodology as for the primary analysis of ORR, PFS, CR, and DOR was used. Forest plots were produced for ORR.
L542: ## Safety Analysis
L543: Study treatment exposure was summarized for the ITT and Safety populations. The number and percentage of subjects with treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), Grade \ge 3 TEAEs, drug-related (to umbralalisib and to ublituximab) TEAEs, drug-related Grade \ge 3 TEAEs, serious TEAEs, Grade \ge 3 serious TEAEs, drug-related serious TEAEs, TEAEs resulting in discontinuation of study treatment (umbralalisib or ublituximab), and TEAEs with fatal outcome were summarized by tumor type, system organ class (SOC), and preferred term (PT). The number and percentage of subjects with AESIs was also summarized by AESI category and PT. Laboratory data for hematology and serum chemistry tests, vital signs, and Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance status were also summarized with shift tables from baseline.
L544: ## Planned Subgroup Analyses
L545: Analyses using following subgroups were performed for efficacy endpoints ORR, CR, PFS, and DOR:
L546: - Gender
- Age group (&lt;65, \ge 65 years)
- Race (Caucasian, Non-Caucasian)
- Geographical region (US, Non-US)
- Baseline ECOG Performance status score (0, \ge 1)
- MZL disease subcategory (Splenic, Nodal, Extranodal)
- Relapse or refractory to last therapy (Relapse, Refractory)
- Bulky disease (&gt;5 cm)
- Splenomegaly
- Number of prior lines of therapy (&lt;2, \ge 2 for subjects)
- Previously treated with a Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) inhibitor (Yes, No)
L547: - Previously treated with lenalidomide (Yes, No)
- Previously treated with lenalidomide in combination with anti-CD20 antibody (Yes, No)
- Previously treated with lenalidomide in combination with rituximab (Yes, No)
- Previously treated with Alkylating agent (Yes, No)
L548: ## Changes to the Planned Analyses
L549: The following changes to the planned subgroup analyses were revised from Version 1.0 of the SAP:
L550: - Previously treated with a BTK inhibitor (Yes, No) for subjects with MZL
- Previously treated with alkylating agent (Yes, No) for subjects with MZL
- Previously treated with lenalidomide in combination with rituximab (Yes, No) was added as a new subgroup.
L551: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L552: The FDA agrees, in general, with the Applicant's position and has the following comments.
L553: According to the SAP, the planned sample size was 60 patients. When using a test having one-sided 2.5% false positive error rate to assess the null hypothesis that the IRC-reviewed ORR is 25%, the trial would have 90% power (respectively, 97.5% power) against the alternative hypothesis that ORR is 45% (respectively, 49%). Using a confidence interval of the ORR derived using the Clopper-Pearson exact method, an estimated ORR of 38% (i.e., 23 subjects responding of 60 MZL subjects evaluated) would achieve a 1-sided 97.5% lower bound of 25.0%.
L554: Although there were no planned interim analysis, updated efficacy data was submitted on 9/15/2020 during the review of this application. The updated efficacy results will be presented in the FDA's assessment section.
L555: ## Protocol Amendments
L556: ## The Applicant's Description:
L557: The study protocol was amended 7 times during the study. None of the implemented changes impacted the integrity of the trial or the interpretation of the results. Important modifications/amendments to the study protocol included the following:
L558: - Amendment 3 (Version 2.0, 12 April 2017 and Version 2.0 UK 12 April 2017):Live virus vaccines were prohibited within 4 weeks prior to enrollment, during therapy, or for 6 weeks post-treatment.
- Amendment 4 (Version 3.0, 01 October 2017):Inclusion criterion was modified to require subjects with FL or SLL to be relapsed or refractory after \ge 2 prior lines of therapy, and all subjects must have received an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent.
- Inclusion criterion was modified to require creatinine clearance \ge 30 mL/min for subjects with FL, SLL, or MZL and \ge 40 mL/min for subjects with DLBCL.
- Exclusion criterion was modified to allow use of concomitant medications known to cause QT prolongation or torsades de pointes with caution and at the Investigator's discretion.
- The FL/SLL cohort was modified to include a Stage 1 to evaluate single agent umbralisib in up to 150 subjects prior to proceeding to Stage 2, which randomized subjects to either umbralisib alone or in combination with ublituximab.
- More specific instructions were given around dose modifications in the event of diarrhea and/or colitis.
- Futility stopping rules were added for the MZL cohort and the FL/SLL cohort based on Simon's 2-stage design.
- Amendment 5 (Version 4.0, 15 February 2018):Mention of copanlisib was added as an excluded prior therapy.
- Amendment 6 (Version 5.0, 20 August 2018)The primary objectives of the study were revised to reflect ORR as the primary endpoint for all cohorts.
- The key secondary objectives of CR rate for subjects with FL or SLL and PFS for subjects with DLBCL were revised to be secondary objectives.
- Inclusion criterion was amended to require subjects with FL, SLL, or MZL to have documented evidence for requiring treatment.
- Statistical assumptions for the MZL, FL/SLL, and DLBCL cohorts were revised in accordance with recent SAPs.
- Amendment 7 (Version 5.1, 20 September 2018)Updated to include cytomegalovirus surveillance for all subjects

- Updated to provide clarification on the liver toxicity dose modification guidelines

L587: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L588: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L589: In the ITT Population, major protocol deviations were reported in 22 MZL subjects (31.9%). The most frequently reported deviations were those related to laboratory assessments (11 MZL subjects [15.9%]).
L590: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L591: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position, though there is additional pertinent information. Per review of the protocol deviation database (ADDV), there were 41 major protocol violations in 22 patients. Major protocol deviations in the MZL patient population included deviations in five patients who experienced at least one SAE which was reported late, in some cases years after the event. The most significant event occurred in a patient with exfoliative dermatitis who developed staphylococcus epidermidis sepsis, leading to fatal organ failure and death. Following an information request, the Applicant noted delayed reporting from the individual clinical sites for the noted serious adverse events. Once the safety data was communicated to the Applicant from the clinical sites, the Applicant informed the relevant regulatory authorities
L592: appropriately.
L593: There were 13 major protocol deviations that related to efficacy assessments in seven patients. The majority of these were missing CTs at various follow-up points. Following review, the missing CT assessments were not likely to impact the overall efficacy results for patients with MZL.
L594: Events that could potentially affect patient safety occurred in multiple patients;
L595: - Five patients did not have CMV serology assessed at the specified timepoints.
- HBV serologies were missing for one patient at enrollment, and ECG for another.
- There were two major violations related to concomitant medications, including inadequate antiviral prophylaxis and &gt; 10 mg of prednisone daily for approximately 1 month.
- One patient had inadvertently taken 1,200 mg of umbralisib for two consecutive days.
L596: In general, the protocol deviations related to safety were not likely to substantially impact the safety evaluation for umbralisib.

L614: ## The Applicant's Position:
L615: The MZL subtypes were extranodal (38 subjects [55.1%]), nodal (20 subjects [29.0%]), and splenic (11 subjects [15.9%]) (Table 11). Most of the subjects were Stage IV (63.8% of subjects). Three subjects (4.3%) previously received lenalidomide and anti-CD20 antibody, all of whom received R2.

L617: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. In the 69 patients with MZL, the median number of prior lines of therapy was 2 (range: 1 to 6), with 26% being refractory to their last therapy.
L618: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L619: ## The Applicant's Position:
L620: ## **Treatment Compliance**
L621: No subjects with MZL discontinued due to non-compliance with study.
L622: ## **Concomitant Medications**
L623: All 69 subjects with MZL received at least 1 concomitant medication. Concomitant medications were categorized according to their potential for inhibition/induction of P450 isozymes and inhibition of transporters. In the category of P450 inhibition, the most frequently used drugs (at least 10% of subjects overall) were acyclovir (66.7% of subjects), allopurinol (34.8% of subjects), and omeprazole (23.2% of subjects), all of which are classified as weak inhibitors based on FDA guidance (FDA: Drug development and drug interactions). Overall, P450 inducers were used infrequently (\le 1.1\%). The most frequently used clinical OATP1B1/OATP1B3 inhibitor was clarithromycin (11.6% of subjects), which is also an inhibitor of P-gp. Other frequently used clinical P-gp inhibitors included carvedilol (2.9%).

L625: Not applicable

L645: Subgroup analyses of ORR based on IRC assessments for the ITT population are presented in Figure 3. For the 4 subjects previously treated with a lenalidomide containing regimen, 3 subjects (75%) achieved a response, of which all 3 had prior R2 treatment. ORR was also consistent among patients with or without prior treatment with an alkylating agent (50% versus 47.6%).

L649: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L650: The FDA agrees with Applicant's presentation of ORR results and the subgroup analyses results. Some of the subgroups had insufficient number of patients needed to draw meaningful conclusions, e.g., the previously treated with lenalidomide and BTK inhibitor categories have insufficient number of patients in the "Yes" subgroups to draw reasonable conclusions. In addition, these analyses are exploratory with no multiplicity adjustment and no pre-specified hypotheses. The outlier subgroups with wide confidence intervals can be attributed to the small number of patients and responders in that subgroup.
L651: For the completeness of the demographic subgroups, the ORR results by ethnicity are as follows:
L652: There were 2 responders out of 2 Hispanic or Latino patients, 28 responders out of 58 non-Hispanic or -Latino patients and 3 responders in the 8 patients who had not reported or had missing ethnicity.
L653: For race, some of the subgroups had insufficient numbers, limiting the conclusions in this study to a broad patient population.
L654: Based on the efficacy update provided on 9/11/2020 using the data cutoff of 15 July 2020, the ORR remained unchanged at 49.3% (95% CI: 37.0, 61.6).

L684: ## Dose/Dose Response
L685: ## The Applicant's Position:
L686: PPK and E-R relationship after umbralisib administration was performed using data from Studies TGR-1202-101 and UTX-TGR-205. The exposure-efficacy analyses included 37 subjects with MZL. The pooled PPK/E-R analyses robustly evaluated biologically interdependent endpoints of efficacy and safety. Overall, the dose response results demonstrate an acceptable balance between benefits and risk at the recommended dose of umbralisib 800 mg and justify the use of this dose regimen in subjects with MZL.
L687: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L688: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L699: ## 8.1.3. Supportive Study for Efficacy - Study TGR-1202-101
L700: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L701: Study TGR-1202-101 is a completed, Phase I, first-in-human, open-label study of umbralisib, administered as a single agent to subjects with relapsed or refractory
L702: hematologic malignancies. The study included eligible subjects aged 18 years or older with histologically confirmed Hodgkin lymphoma, B-cell NHL, peripheral T cell lymphoma, CLL, SLL, MZL, or other B-cell lymphoproliferative disorders whose disease had relapsed after, or was refractory to, at least 1 previous treatment regimen.
L703: This study consisted of a dose-escalation phase and a dose-expansion phase. The dose-escalation phase used a traditional 3+3 design to determine the MTD. Subjects were to receive umbralisib daily in the absence of disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal from treatment. In the initial dose-escalation phase, subjects received [REDACTED] (b)(4) umbralisib under a fasted condition starting at 50 mg orally QD with increasing doses for each subsequent cohort until the MTD was identified. For the majority of the study, subjects received [REDACTED] (b)(4) umbralisib, which was subsequently used in Study UTX-TGR-205 and Study TGR-1202-202. Each treatment cycle was 4 weeks (28 days), with the first efficacy evaluation at 8 weeks (\pm 7 days) and at 8-week intervals thereafter. After Cycle 12, restaging was performed every 6 months at the discretion of the treating physician on a subject-by-subject basis secondary to the clinical presentation of the subject. Disease assessments were conducted by treating investigators. Subjects with PD or unacceptable toxicity were discontinued, and subjects achieving stable disease or better were allowed to continue daily treatment with umbralisib. Subjects on umbralisib who received benefit at the conclusion of this study were eligible to enroll in an open-label extension study, Study UTX TGR-501, to continue to receive single agent umbralisib.
L704: The primary objective of this study was to evaluate safety and MTD of umbralisib in subjects with hematologic malignancies and to evaluate the PK effects of umbralisib. The secondary objectives of the study included evaluation of ORR and DOR in subjects with hematologic malignancies treated with umbralisib.
L705: Of the 90 subjects enrolled in this study, 5 subjects had MZL. Treatment was discontinued for all subjects: 2 subjects due to PD and 1 subject each due to AE, other (lack of improvement in disease burden), and subject request to discontinue treatment. The median age was 62.0 years (range: 43 to 76 years). The mean duration of exposure to umbralisib was 15.2 months (range: 4.2 to 24.6 months); 3 of the 5 subjects were exposed for \ge 12 months.
L706: **The FDA's Assessment:**
The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L707: ## 8.1.4. Efficacy Results - Study TGR-1202-101
L708: The Applicant's Position:
L709: Results from the Phase I dose expansion phase of Study TGR-1202-101 provide additional support for efficacy of umbralisib in subjects with MZL. However, because of the small number of subjects in this study and the differences in response evaluation and dosing of umbralisib, direct comparison with the MZL cohort in Study UTX-TGR-205 is not feasible.
L710: Of the 5 MZL subjects enrolled in this study, 1 subject achieved PR, yielding ORR of 20.0%. The disease control rate (DCR) was 100%. Median (95% CI) DOR was not estimable.
L711: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L712: The FDA's assessment of efficacy was based primarily on Study UTX-TGR-205. The efficacy data from Study UTX-TGR-101 is descriptive and considered supportive.
L713: ## 8.1.5. **Supportive Study for Efficacy - Study TGR-1202-202**
L714: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L715: Study TGR-1202-202 is an ongoing Phase II study designed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of single agent umbralisib 800 mg QD in previously treated subjects with non-follicular iNHL. Subjects were required to have non-follicular iNHL (MZL and Waldenstrom's macroglobulinemia) that relapsed or were refractory following at least 1 prior standard therapy regimen. The primary objective was to evaluate the ORR (CR+PR+very good partial response +minor response) and DOR for umbralisib in subjects with relapsed or refractory non follicular iNHL. The secondary objectives were to determine the PFS of umbralisib in subjects with relapsed or refractory non-follicular iNHL, to evaluate time to treatment failure with umbralisib, and to evaluate the safety profile of umbralisib. Disease assessments were conducted by treating investigators.
L716: As of the cutoff date of 01 September 2019, a total of 8 subjects with MZL were enrolled in the study, of whom 4 subjects were discontinued from the study and 4 subjects were ongoing. The median age was 73 years (range: 58 to 82 years). Most of the subjects had nodal (50.0%) or extranodal (37.5%) MZL disease. Stage at diagnosis was IV for 37.5% of subjects. The mean duration of exposure to umbralisib was 8.0 months (range: 2 to 18 months). Six of the 8 subjects were exposed to umbralisib for &lt;12 months, and 2 subjects were exposed to umbralisib ≥12 months.
L717: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L718: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L719: ## 8.1.6. **Efficacy Results - Study TGR-1202-202**
L720: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L721: Preliminary efficacy results from the Phase II Study TGR-1202-202 provide additional support for efficacy of umbralisib in subjects with MZL. However, because of the small number of subjects in this study and the differences in response evaluation and dosing of umbralisib, direct comparison with the MZL cohort in Study UTX-TGR-205 is not feasible.
L722: For the 8 MZL subjects as of the cutoff date for efficacy (30 November 2019), the ORR was 37.5%, with CR in 12.5% of subjects and PR in 25.0% of subjects. The DCR was 87.5%. For the 2 responders with more than 1 response assessment and no disease progression as of the data cutoff date, the DOR was censored at the last tumor assessment and were estimated as 3.0 and 7.2 months.
L723: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L724: The FDA's assessment of efficacy was based primarily on Study UTX-TGR-205. The efficacy data from Study UTX-TGR-202 is descriptive and considered supportive.
L725: ## 8.1.7. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L726: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L727: Not applicable, the primary efficacy data for umbralisib is from Study UTX-TGR-205 as reviewed in the above sections
L728: ## 8.1.8. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L729: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L730: Study UTX-TGR-205 was the pivotal study. Data from this study provide primary support for efficacy of umbralisib in subjects with MZL. Data from the other 2 studies (TGR-1202-101 and TGR-1202-202) that included subjects with MZL provide additional support for efficacy of umbralisib in subjects with MZL. However, because of the small number of subjects in these 2 studies and the differences in response evaluation and dosing of umbralisib, direct comparison with the MZL cohort in Study UTX-TGR-205 is not feasible, and efficacy results are instead presented side by side in Table 15. DOR and TTR were not calculated for TGR-1202-101 and TGR-1202-202 because of the limited number of subjects.

L740: ## 8.1.9. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L741: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L742: The primary study to characterize the efficacy of umbralisib in MZL is Study UTX-TGR-205. Two additional studies with single agent umbralisib included subjects with MZL: Study TGR-1202-101 (5 subjects with MZL) and Study TGR-1202-202 (8 subjects with MZL).
L743: A comparison of the efficacy results across studies was not conducted due to the limited number of subjects with MZL enrolled outside of the pivotal study UTX-TGR-205 as well as the differences in response evaluation and dosing of umbralisib. Given the minimal number of additional subjects with MZL for these 2 studies, interpretation of these results will be limited and will not meaningfully contribute to the description of efficacy.
L744: Unlike Study UTX-TGR-205, Study TGR-1202-101 did not require MZL subjects to be relapsed following prior anti-CD20-based therapy. Unlike Study UTX-TGR-205, Study TGR-1202-202 did not require MZL subjects to be relapsed following prior anti-CD20-based therapy and allowed subjects to have prior treatment with a PI3K inhibitor.
L745: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L746: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the data from the three trials cannot be pooled together due to the differences in the response evaluation, dosing, inclusion/exclusion criteria of the three trials. The evaluation of efficacy is based on the data from UTX-TGR-205.
L747: The efficacy of umbralisib is based on data from 69 patients with MZL from a cohort of
L748: the UTX-TGR-205 trial, an open-label, multi-center, single-arm, multi-cohort trial. Patients with MZL were required to have prior treatment with 1 or more lines of therapy, including at least 1 anti-CD20-based regimen (either as monotherapy or chemoimmunotherapy). The trial excluded patients with prior exposure to a PI3K inhibitor. The primary endpoint was overall response rate (ORR) assessed by an independent review committee (IRC) based on the 2007 International Working Group criteria for Malignant Lymphoma using computerized tomography (CT). Secondary endpoints included duration of response and progression-free survival.
L749: Of the 69 patients with MZL [extranodal (N=38), nodal (N=20), and splenic (N=11)], the median age was 67 years (range: 34 to 88 years), 52% were female, 83% were White, 7% were Black, 3% were Asian, and 97% had a baseline ECOG performance status of 0 or 1. Patients had a median number of prior lines of therapy of 2 (range: 1 to 6), with 26% being refractory to their last therapy. The median follow-up time was 20.3 months (range 15.0 to 28.7 months). The ORR per IRC was 49% (95% CI: 37.0, 61.6), with 16% achieving a complete response. The median duration of response was not reached (95% CI: 9.3, not evaluable). Of the 34 patients that responded, 65% maintained a response at 6 months, 50% at 9 months, and 38% at 12 months.
L750: The data in patients with MZL support the determination that umbralalisib has clinically meaningful activity in adult patients with relapsed or refractory MZL after at least one prior anti-CD20-based regimen.
L751: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L752: ## The Applicant's Position:
L753: Safety data for subjects exposed to single agent umbralalisib from the pivotal Study UTX-TGR-205 as of the cutoff date of 01 September 2019 were integrated with data from 4 other studies (Studies TGR-1202-101, TGR-1202-202, UTX-TGR-103, and UTX-TGR-501) to form an integrated database (IDB), which included a total of 371 subjects (82 subjects with MZL, 147 subjects with FL, and 142 subjects with other hematologic malignancies) hereafter referred to as the Umbralalisib Safety Population.
L754: Safety data from Study TGR-1202-102, a completed Phase I study in 32 subjects with solid tumor cancers, and safety data from 6 clinical pharmacology studies in healthy subjects (Studies TGR 1202-PK101, TGR 1202-PK102, TGR-1202-901, TGR-1202-902, TGR-1202-904, and TGR-1202-908) were not integrated with the other studies.
L755: Data from the IDB allow for an informed assessment of the safety profile of umbralalisib
L756: 800 mg QD and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk in subjects with MZL. This safety population is also considered appropriate for the detection and characterization of common AEs and to provide guidance on toxicity management.
L757: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L758: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L759: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L760: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L761: The Umbralisib Safety Population included all subjects who received at least 1 dose of single agent umbralisib at doses \ge 800 mg and was used for all analyses for single agent umbralisib. Pooled safety analyses included TEAEs, laboratory assessments, vital signs, and subgroup analyses (gender [male, female], age group [&lt;65 years, \ge 65 years], race [Caucasian, non-Caucasian], geographic region [US, non-US], prior lines of therapy [&lt;2, \ge 2], prior lenalidomide therapy [yes, no], previous treatment with lenalidomide in combination with anti-CD20 therapy [yes, no], eGFR at baseline [mildly decreased, moderately decreased, severely decreased renal function, or end stage renal disease], and hepatic impairment [mild, moderate, severe]). Physical examinations and ECGs were not pooled and were examined by study.
L762: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L763: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The FDA reviewed data from the pivotal trial as well as the Safety Pool population (N = 371) and the 120-day Safety update, extending data collection to January 2020.
L764: Safety endpoints listed below were used to evaluate the safety and tolerability of single agent umbralisib in this population of patients with MZL and patients with hematologic malignancies:
L765: - Incidence and severity of all Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAE)
- Grade 3-5 TEAEs
- Serious adverse events
- TEAEs leading to treatment modifications
- Adverse events of Special Interest
- Clinical laboratory test abnormalities, including lab shift analysis
- Prior therapies
L766: The frequency of TEAEs was tabulated by the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) Version 22.1. The NCI CTC/CTCAE version 4.03 was used to grade and summarize adverse events.
L767: To inform the safety review, FDA used grouped preferred terms for more sensitive and informative safety analyses. The FDA preferred terms are listed in Section 19.5
L768: ## 8.2.2. Review of the Safety Database

L770: ## The Applicant's Position:
L771: Overall, 371 subjects received at least 1 dose of umbralisib, 82 subjects with MZL, 147 subjects with FL, 74 subjects with DLBCL/MCL, 43 subjects with CLL/SLL, and 25 subjects with other tumor types (Table 16). The median duration of exposure to umbralisib was 5.91 months (range: 0.1 to 75.1 months). A total of 92 subjects (24.8%) were exposed to umbralisib for 12 to &lt;24 months, and 15 subjects (4.0%) were exposed for ≥24 months.

L786: ## **Relevant characteristics of the safety population:**
L787: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L788: The Umbralisib Safety Population included subjects with MZL, FL, and other hematologic malignancies from 5 studies. Overall, the median age was 67 years (range: 22 to 95 years); 56.3% of subjects were male, 82.7% of subjects were White, and 72.8% of subjects were enrolled in the US. The ECOG performance status was 0 or 1 for most of the subjects (96.0%).
L789: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L790: For subjects with MZL, the median age was 68 years (range: 34 to 88 years); 47.6% of subjects were male, 81.7% of subjects were White, and 63.4% of subjects were enrolled in the US.
L791: Overall, the median number of prior systemic therapy was 2 (range: 1 to 14); 76.5% of subjects had \ge 2 prior lines of therapy, and 49.1% of subjects had \ge 3 prior lines of therapy. For subjects with MZL, the median number of prior systemic therapy was 2 (range: 1 to 7); 53.7% of subjects had \ge 2 prior lines of therapy, and 30.5% of subjects had \ge 3 prior lines of therapy.
L792: Overall, 11.9% of subjects had prior lenalidomide therapy, and 7.8% of subjects had prior lenalidomide plus anti-CD20 therapy.
L793: Median time since the most recent progression was 2.1 months (range: 0.3 to 447.4 months); 55.8% of subjects relapsed from prior treatment (progression of disease occurring greater than 6 months after last treatment), and 39.1% of subjects were refractory to prior treatment (progression during or within 6 months of completing prior therapy).
L794: Overall, at baseline, the percentage of subjects with mild, moderate, and severe renal impairment were 53.6%, 26.1%, and 0.5% of subjects, respectively. No subjects had severe hepatic impairment, 14.8% of subjects had mild hepatic impairment, and 0.5% of subjects had moderate hepatic impairment.
L795: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L796: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L797: ## **Adequacy of the safety database:**
L798: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L799: The evaluation of safety is based on data for subjects exposed to single agent umbralisib from the pivotal Study UTX-TGR-205 as of the cutoff date of 01 September 2019 and from 4 other studies (Studies TGR-1202-101, TGR-1202-202, UTX-TGR-103, and UTX-TGR-501). Further details are provided in Section 8.2. This population allows for an informed assessment of the safety profile of umbralisib and a judgment of the overall benefit-risk in subjects with MZL.
L800: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L801: The safety database was acceptable for review. The initial submission did not include toxicity grades in the laboratory (ADLB) dataset. An information request was sent to the Applicant to enable a lab-shift analysis by the reviewers.
L802: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L803: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L804: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L805: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and individual case narratives; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections; data in the case report forms and AE databases were consistent.
L806: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L807: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L808: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L809: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L810: Each AE term was mapped to a SOC and PT using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) version 22.1. The AE severity was graded using Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Event (CTCAE) version 4.03.
L811: A TEAE was defined as an AE that first occurred or worsened in intensity from the first dose of study drug through 30 days after the last dose of study drug. The AEs that started before the first dose of study drug but considered related to study drug by the Investigator were assumed worsened in intensity during treatment and therefore were reported as TEAEs. The analysis of AEs included subjects with any TEAE, serious TEAE, CTCAE Grade \ge 3 TEAE, TEAEs that led to death, TEAEs that led to discontinuation of study drug, TEAEs that led to dose reduction or interruption of study drug, AESIs, and drug-related AESIs. In addition, the number and percentage of subjects with TEAEs, serious TEAEs, TEAEs leading to discontinuation of study drug, TEAEs leading to dose reduction, TEAEs leading to dose interruption, and TEAEs leading to death were summarized by period of exposure to umbralisib (&lt;3 months, 3 to &lt;6 months, 6 to &lt;12 months, 1 to &lt;2 years, and \ge 2 years).
L812: Adverse events of special interest (AESIs) were predefined in the integrated SAP; the criteria used to define AESIs were drug causality assessment, serious adverse event (SAE) rate, severity grade, frequency of TEAEs, class effect, discontinuation rate, and clinical context. All AESIs had SAEs (at least possibly related) plus one or more of the other criteria. Standardized MedDRA Queries relevant to the AESI list were used to select any additional PTs associated with AESI.
L813: The Umbralisib Safety Population (N=371) supported predominantly by the pivotal UTX-TGR-205 study, provide the primary source for adverse drug reaction (ADR) determination. Safety data were reviewed systematically for TEAEs irrespective of Investigator's causality assessment, which includes serious TEAEs, TEAEs leading to dose modification, and AESIs. This was
L814: accompanied by individual case reviews of selected events. New or worsening laboratory parameters were also analyzed.
L815: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L816: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L817: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L818: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L819: Laboratory assessments were pooled for the Umbralisib Safety Population and included changes from baseline, laboratory abnormalities based on CTCAE grade, shifts from baseline to post-baseline worst value based on CTCAE grade for hematology and serum chemistry (including liver function tests) parameters measured in the clinical trials.
L820: The clinical monitoring of subject safety was considered adequate for the expected toxicities associated with umbralisib. Subjects were questioned about AEs at each clinic visit. In addition, AEs could also be detected when reported by the subjects during or between visits or through physical examination, laboratory test results, or other assessments. Further to the standard safety evaluations outlined above, AE categories expected to be associated with umbralisib were also analyzed.
L821: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L822: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L823: ## 8.2.4 Safety Results
L824: ## Deaths
L825: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L826: Overall, 4 deaths due to AEs (pleural effusion, septic shock, myocardial infarction, and pneumonia legionella) were reported, and all 4 subjects died within 30 days of last dose of umbralisib (Table 18). The events of pleural effusion, septic shock, and myocardial infarction were considered not related to umbralisib; pneumonia legionella was considered unlikely related to umbralisib.
L827: Other deaths include death due to PD in 22 subjects (5.9%) and death due to "other/unknown/missing" after 30 days of last dose of umbralisib in 3 subjects (0.8%) due to heart failure, myelodysplasia, and respiratory failure.

L833: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L834: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's contention that no deaths in patients with MZL were related to an adverse event. In the table above, the patient listed as death due to other/unknown/missing was adjudicated by FDA as a death due to an adverse event. Patient (b) (6) an 88 year-old female with MZL, received the first dose of umbralisib on (b) (6) and the final dose on (b) (6). Relevant past medical history included hypertension and hypothyroidism. On study day 37, she presented with facial edema with erythematous lesions on her upper back and extremities and umbralisib was interrupted. Umbralisib was restarted on study day 39 and discontinued again on day 45 due to continued symptoms. The event progressed to a grade 3 serious adverse event of exfoliative dermatitis requiring hospitalization. The patient continued treatment for exfoliative dermatitis and was discharged after partial improvement. Umbralisib was interrupted on study day 45 and permanently discontinued on study day 72. On study day 73, the patient was readmitted with grade 3 heart failure, grade 3 staphylococcal sepsis, severe edema of right hand and both legs, and continuation of the exfoliative dermatitis. The patient developed worsening and ultimately fatal complications secondary to toxicity. The temporal sequence of development of exfoliative dermatitis followed by staphylococcal bacteremia and sepsis and organ dysfunction provides support that the event can be considered related to umbralisib.

The most frequently reported serious TEAEs (at least 2% of total pooled subjects) were pneumonia (3.0% of subjects) and diarrhea (2.4% of subjects). Serious TEAEs reported in at least 2 subjects with MZL were diarrhea (4 subjects), pneumonia (3 subjects), and anemia (3 subjects).

L844: Grade \ge 3 serious TEAEs were reported in 82 subjects (22.1%) overall, including 24 subjects (29.3%) with MZL. Of these, 41 subjects (11.1%) had Grade \ge 3 serious TEAEs related to umbralisib, including 19 subjects (23.2%) with MZL.
L845: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L846: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of all-cause serious TEAEs in 371 patients with hematologic malignancies. Overall, serious TEAEs were reported in 98 subjects (26.4%). For additional information, SAEs are shown in the table below using grouped preferred terms (Section 19.5), which allows for increased sensitivity and informative analyses. There was a higher incidence of any grade SAEs in the MZL cohort compared to the safety pool (39.1% versus 26.4%) with diarrhea/colitis and sepsis more pronounced. In patients with MZL, the most common SAEs were diarrhea/colitis (5.8%), sepsis, pneumonia, and anemia (4.3% each).

L850: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L851: ## The Applicant's Position:

L897: ## *TEAEs of at Least Grade 3*
L898: Overall, 189 subjects (50.9%) had Grade \ge3 TEAEs. Of these, 124 subjects (33.4%) had Grade \ge3 TEAEs related to umbralisib. The most frequently reported Grade \ge3 TEAEs (at least 5% of total pooled subjects) were neutropenia (11.3% of subjects), diarrhea (7.3% of subjects), ALT increased (5.7% of subjects), and AST increased (5.7% of subjects). The most frequently reported Grade \ge3 TEAEs related to umbralisib (at least 5% of total pooled subjects) were neutropenia (8.9% of subjects), diarrhea (6.7% of subjects), ALT increased (5.4% of subjects), and AST increased (5.4% of subjects).
L899: ## *Adverse Drug Reactions*
L900: The Umbralisib Safety Population, supported predominantly by the pivotal Study UTX-TGR-205, provide the primary source for ADR determination. Safety data were reviewed systematically for TEAEs irrespective of Investigator's causality assessment, which includes serious TEAEs, TEAEs leading to dose modification, and AESIs, observed in the Umbralisib Safety Population. This was accompanied by individual case reviews of selected events. New or worsening laboratory parameters were also analyzed. Each TEAE and laboratory parameter evaluated for ADR determination were assessed against pre-specified criteria as follows: frequency, severity, and seriousness; clinical experience to date with umbralisib; observed dose-response relationship; association with mechanism of action of umbralisib as an inhibitor of PI3K\delta and CK1\epsilon; consistency with non-clinical findings with umbralisib; underlying malignancy, comorbidities, or concurrent illness; and/or clinically meaningful shift for laboratory parameter.
L901: Common adverse reactions reported in \ge10% of subjects with MZL, FL, and other hematologic malignancies treated with umbralisib are summarized in Table 26. ADRs of any grade were reported in 351 subjects (94.6%), and ADRs of Grade 3 and Grade 4 were reported in 135 subjects (36.4%) and 31 subjects (8.4%), respectively. The most frequently reported ADRs of any grade (\ge20%) were diarrhea (52.3%), nausea (41.5%), fatigue (38.8%), musculoskeletal pain (28.6%), and vomiting (23.2%); none of these were of Grade 4.

L922: Based on the use of grouped of preferred terms (Section 19.5), the following table provides a summary of the most common adverse events.

L939: ## Vital Signs
L940: ## The Applicant's Position:
L941: Vital sign results were not pooled due to the heterogeneity of assessment schedule across studies. In general, the changes from baseline were not clinically meaningful. Of the TEAEs associated with vital signs, the most frequently reported in the Umbralisib Safety Population were pyrexia (12.7%), hypertension (7.0%), and weight decreased (7.0%).
L942: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L943: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position
L944: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L945: ## The Applicant's Position:
L946: ECG data were summarized by study for Study TGR-1202-101 and Study TGR-1202-102. No pooled analyses based on IDB were performed due to limited data and heterogeneity of assessment schedules across studies. Of the TEAEs associated with ECG parameters, the most frequently (at least 1% total) reported in the Umbralisib Safety Population were atrial fibrillation (1.3%) and sinus tachycardia (1.1%). No TEAEs of supraventricular tachycardia or ventricular extrasystoles were reported.
L947: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L948: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L949: ## QT
L950: ## The Applicant's Position:
L951: A concentration-QTc analysis based on data from the 32 subjects of Cohort S (umbralisib monotherapy) in Study TGR-1202-102 was conducted to characterize the relationship of the change from baseline QTc interval with umbralisib plasma concentration. The concentration QTc analysis did not reveal a significant relationship or risk of \ge 20 ms corrected QT interval by Fredericia (QTcF) prolongation at the time points evaluated. The predicted \DeltaQTcF was higher at the 4-hour post dose time point than for other time points, reaching 3.6 ms with a 90% CI upper bound of 10.2 ms.
L952: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L953: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that there is no risk of QTc prolongation with umbralisib. The Applicant's exposure-corrected QT interval (QTc) analyses were conducted using steady state exposure (C_{max,ss}) data in Study TGR-1202-102. The geometric mean C_{max} on Cycle 2 Day 1 was 4012 ng/mL (n=20), which was lower than the therapeutic exposure (7266 ng/mL) obtained from PPK analysis. The 1200 mg QD dose group in Study TGR-1202-102 did not provide higher exposure (4172 ng/mL) as compared to the 800 mg QD dose group. Thus, the Applicant's QT assessment was not adequate to extrapolate the risk of QTc prolongation to the higher exposure range. The FDA agrees that there were no large mean increases (i.e., 20 msec) with umbralisib treatment.
L954: ## Immunogenicity
L955: ## The Applicant's Position:
L956: There are no immunogenicity data about umbralisib in this application.
L957: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L958: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L996: ## Adverse Events of Special Interest
L997: The FDA's incidence of AESIs, differed in some instances due to grouping of preferred terms, consistent throughout this review. In the following section, the FDA considerations are provided for each AESI. Any grade AESIs occurred in 62 patients (90%) with MZL in the UTZ-TGR-205 MZL cohort (N = 69).
L998: ## Diarrhea
L999: Diarrhea or colitis is an important identified risk for patients who receive treatment with umbralisib. The Applicant identified events of diarrhea or colitis based on selected MedDRA preferred terms for diarrhea or colitis. Because the clinical presentation of diarrhea or colitis overlaps, FDA used a grouped MedDRA PT (Appendix 19.4) to evaluate the incidence and pattern of umbralisib-associated diarrhea or colitis.
L1000: Diarrhea/colitis was the most common toxicity reported in the safety pool (52.3%), as well as the MZL population in UTX-TGR-205 (62.3%). Grade 3 diarrhea/colitis occurred in 7.3% and 7.2% of patients in the safety pool and MZL cohort, respectively.
L1001: Based on the 371 patients in the safety pool, the incidence of any grade diarrhea/colitis reported as a SAE was 3.2% (2.4% grade ≥3). Diarrhea or colitis led to dose interruption in 12.3%, dose reduction in 2.2%, and treatment discontinuation in 4.8% of patients. It is one of
L1002: the most common reasons for treatment modification. For all grade diarrhea or colitis, the median time to onset was 1 month, with 75% occurring within 2.8 months. For patients with grade \ge 3 diarrhea or colitis, the median time to onset was 4.5 months, with 75% occurring within 8 months.
L1003: Based on the data, there is no established standard of treatment for umbralisib-associated diarrhea or colitis. Labeling will include a Warning and Precaution for diarrhea or colitis and dose modifications to ensure safe use of the drug. Patients should be aware that umbralisib should be discontinued in the event of severe diarrhea or colitis.
L1004: ## Hepatotoxicity
L1005: Based on lab-shift analyses in the safety pool population (N = 371), any grade transaminase elevation occurred in 29% (ALT) and 30% (AST) of the population, and grade 3-4 toxicities were 7% and 6% respectively. These incidences were higher in the UTX-TGR-205 MZL population in which 43% (ALT) and 41% (AST) of patients experienced any grade AST/ALT elevation with 10% reaching severity of grade 3-4. In the safety pool, the median onset of grade 3-4 hepatotoxicity was 2 months, with 75% occurring by 3 months. Transaminase elevation led to umbralisib dose interruption in 4.6%, dose reduction in 1.1%, and treatment discontinuation in 3.2%. The Applicant reported no cases of liver failure in patients with hematologic malignancies. The study did not enroll patients with moderate or severe hepatic dysfunction.
L1006: Given the concerns for hepatotoxicity with umbralisib, labeling will include hepatotoxicity as a Warning and Precaution to ensure safe use of umbralisib. The pattern of umbralisib-associated hepatotoxicity, specific monitoring procedures, and dose modifications will need to be included in labeling to inform healthcare providers and patients.
L1007: ## Rash
L1008: In the safety pool, one patient developed a fatal case of Grade 3 exfoliative dermatitis. The event of exfoliative dermatitis led to bacteremia and sepsis and multi-organ failure. In the MZL cohort of study UTX-TGR-205, 39% of the patients developed all grade rash. The incidence in the safety pool of any grade rash was 18.9%, with 3% experiencing Grade 3 rash events. Rash lead to umbralisib dose interruption in 1.6% of the patients.
L1009: In addition, severe cutaneous reactions, including SJS, TEN, and DRESS, are a known class effect with PI3K inhibitors. Based on a fatal case of exfoliative dermatitis with umbralisib, the incidence of cutaneous reactions, and the known class effect, a Warning and Precaution for severe cutaneous reactions should be included in labeling.
L1010: ## Neutropenia
L1011: Based on lab-shift analyses in the safety pool population, any grade neutropenia occurred in 36% of the population, and grade 3-4 neutropenia of 17%. In patients with MZL, any grade
L1012: neutropenia was 33%, with 19% being Grade 3-4.
L1013: For the 371 patients in the safety pool, the median time to onset of grade 3-4 neutropenia was 2 months, with 75% occurring by 3.7 months. Neutropenia led to umbralisib dose interruption in 5%, reduction in 2%, and discontinuation in 1%.
L1014: Given the concerns for Grade 3-4 neutropenia coupled with the potential risk for infection with umbralisib, labeling will include neutropenia as a Warning and Precaution to ensure safe use of umbralisib. The pattern of umbralisib-associated neutropenia, monitoring procedures, and dose modifications will need to be included in labeling to inform healthcare providers and patients.
L1015: ## Anemia
L1016: Based on lab-shift analysis data in the safety pool population, any grade anemia occurred in 30% of the population and grade 3-4 anemia in 5%. In patients with MZL, any grade anemia was 22%, with 4% being Grade 3-4.
L1017: ## Thrombocytopenia
L1018: Based on lab-shift analysis data in the safety pool population, any grade thrombocytopenia occurred in 25% of the population and grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia in 5%. In patients with MZL, any grade thrombocytopenia was 30%, with 6% being Grade 3-4. In patients that developed thrombocytopenia, there was no association with clinically significant bleeding event.
L1019: ## Pneumonia
L1020: Overall in the umbralisib safety population, pneumonia was reported 6.5% of the patients, including 3.5% with Grade \ge 3 pneumonia. In the MZL cohort of study UTX-TGR-205, 8.7% and 2.9% of the patients had any grade and Grade \ge 3 pneumonia, respectively. Incidence of SAE in the safety population and MZL cohort were 0.8% and 4.3%, respectively.
L1021: ## Infection
L1022: Though not included as an AESI, infection is a significant issue with umbralisib use. In the safety pool of 371 patients, there were 160 patients (43%) with the reported event of infection, with 37 (20%) of grade 3-4 severity. There were two deaths attributable to infection. These included pneumonia legionella and sepsis. Sepsis occurred in eight patients (2%) and led to two deaths.
L1023: Given the concerns for the risk of infection and the incidence of Grade 3-4 neutropenia, labeling will include infection as a Warning and Precaution to ensure safe use of umbralisib.
L1024: ## Pneumonitis
L1025: In the Safety Population, pneumonitis was reported in 1% of patients, with &lt;1% being Grade 3. Pneumonitis was reported as a SAE and led to treatment discontinuation in 2 patients (&lt;1%) each, respectively.
L1026: ## 8.2.5. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1027: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1028: Not applicable.
L1029: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1030: There were no clinical outcome or patient-reported outcome data submitted with this application.
L1031: ## 8.2.6. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1032: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1033: Subgroup analyses were conducted to identify potential safety issues restricted to particular subpopulations; no clinically meaningful differences were observed as compared to the overall Umbralisib Safety Population.
L1034: ## **Sex**
L1035: Overall, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting were reported more frequently in female subjects than male subjects. Neutropenia was reported with a similar incidence between male and female subjects overall but more frequently in male than female subjects with MZL. The observed differences by gender were not considered clinically meaningful.
L1036: ## **Age**
L1037: Overall, Grade \ge 3 TEAEs, serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to discontinuation of umbralisib were reported more frequently in subjects in the age group \ge 65 years than subjects &lt; 65 years (54.5% versus 46.3%, 31.6% versus 17.9%, and 18.2% versus 11.7%, respectively). Nausea was reported more frequently in subjects in the age group \ge 65 years than subjects &lt; 65 years (44.0% versus 38.3%) overall and in subjects with MZL (41.2% versus 22.6%). Neutropenia and anemia were reported more frequently in subjects in the age group \ge 65 years than subjects &lt; 65 years (17.2% versus 11.7% and 15.3% versus 6.2%, respectively) overall and in subjects with FL (18.9% versus 8.2% and 14.9% versus 4.1%, respectively). The observed differences by age were not considered clinically meaningful.
L1038: ## **Race**
L1039: The incidences of TEAEs, serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to discontinuation of umbralisib were similar between White and Non-White subjects. Vomiting was reported more frequently in Non-White than White subjects (28.1% versus 22.1%) overall and in subjects with MZL (26.7%
L1040: versus 17.9%). The incidence of hepatic dysfunction and rash were also higher in Non-White than White subjects (23.4% versus 17.9% and 18.8% versus 13.0%, respectively). The observed differences by race were not considered clinically meaningful.
L1041: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1042: There was limited representation of racial or ethnic minorities in the MZL population of UTX-TGR-205 with few African American (7.2%) or Asian (2.9%) patients to allow generalization of these results. In addition, only 2.9% were of Hispanic/Latino ethnicity. This reflects an overall limitation in the data as it applies to the US population and a postmarketing commitment was issued to address this concern (Section 13).

L1047: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

The presence of umbralisib in human milk and its effects on the breastfed child or on milk production have not been investigated.
