L1080: ## **8.2.10. Integrated Assessment of Safety**
L1081: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1082: The safety of single agent umbralisib was evaluated in 371 subjects, including 82 subjects with MZL. Key safety findings were as follows:
L1083: - Death due to TEAEs were reported in 4 subjects (1.1%), and none were in subjects with MZL.
L1084: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L1085: - The common TEAEs of diarrhea, nausea, fatigue, and vomiting were reported at similar rates across duration of exposure groupings suggesting that these TEAEs occurred at generally the same rate regardless of the duration of exposure to umbralisib.
- Common ADRs of any grade (\ge 10\%) were reported in 94.6% of subjects, and ADRs of Grade 3 and Grade 4 were reported in 36.4% and 8.4% of subjects, respectively. The most frequently reported adverse reactions of any grade (\ge 20\%) were diarrhea (52.3%), nausea (41.5%), fatigue (38.8%), musculoskeletal pain (28.6%), and vomiting (23.2%); none of these were of Grade 4.
- Overall, TEAEs of special interest were reported in 77.6% of subjects. The most frequently reported TEAEs of special interest (at least 10% of total pooled subjects) were diarrhea (52.3% of subjects), ALT increased (15.1% of subjects), AST increased (14.8% of subjects), neutropenia (14.3% of subjects), and anemia (11.1% of subjects).
- The most frequently reported laboratory abnormalities with umbralisib treatment (&gt;30\% of subjects and at least 5% of subjects with Grade \ge 3) were leukocytes decreased (38.8%, Grade 3: 5.1%), neutrophils decreased (36.7%, Grade 3: 9.0%), hemoglobin decreased (30.4%, Grade 3: 5.4%), and AST increased (30.1%, Grade 3: 6.0%).
L1086: In conclusion, single agent umbralisib was well tolerated and had a manageable safety profile in the overall and MZL populations.
L1087: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1088: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the safety data demonstrates that umbralisib demonstrated a tolerable safety profile in patients with marginal zone lymphoma. Although, given the median exposure of 9.4 months in patients with MZL and approximately 6 months in patients with hematologic malignancies, there is limited data to support longer-term safety.
L1089: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1090: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1091: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1092: There are no major statistical issues identified from this submission. The trials included in the review of this application were designed to be single arm open-label studies with different study objectives. No statistical inference on comparison was conducted. The primary efficacy endpoint was the overall response rate (CR + PR). The single-arm aspect of the analyses limits the ability to perform any comparative evaluations.
L1093: There were insufficient number of patients in the race and ethnicity demographic subgroups to provide an assessment of efficacy in this patient subpopulation.
L1094: In the absence of a control arm, it was difficult to interpret the time-to-event endpoint of PFS in these trials. The results of the analysis were descriptive and considered exploratory.
L1095: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1096: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1097: The benefit-risk assessment is favorable for umbralisib for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory marginal zone lymphoma who have received one prior systemic therapy, including an anti-CD20-based regimen.
L1098: ## Efficacy:
L1099: Efficacy of umbralisib in adult patients with previously treated marginal zone lymphoma (MZL) is based on the results from a single-arm cohort of Study UTX-TGR-205, a multicenter, single-arm, open-label, multi-cohort trial, which included 69 patients with MZL. The scheduled umbralisib dose was 800 mg by mouth daily until progressive disease or unacceptable toxicity. The primary endpoint was overall response rate (ORR) assessed by an independent review committee (IRC) based on the 2007 International Working Group criteria using computerized tomography (CT). Among the 69 patients with MZL, the median follow-up time was 20.3 months (range 15.0 to 28.7 months). The ORR per IRC was 49% (95% CI: 37.0, 61.6), with 16% achieving a complete response. The median duration of response was not reached.
L1100: ## Safety:
L1101: The safety profile was supported by analysis of the 69 patients with marginal zone lymphoma from the study UTX-TGR-205 and a combined safety database of 371 patients with hematologic malignancies [marginal zone lymphoma (22%), follicular lymphoma (40%), diffuse large B-cell lymphoma or mantle cell lymphoma (20%), and other hematologic malignancies (18%)] who received umbralisib doses \ge800 mg daily.
L1102: In the 69 patients with MZL, the median duration of treatment was 9.4 months (range 5 days to 23 months) and the most common adverse events (\ge20% of patients) were increased creatinine, diarrhea-colitis, AST/ALT increased, fatigue, neutropenia, nausea, musculoskeletal pain, thrombocytopenia, cough, headache, rash, decreased appetite, upper respiratory tract infection, anemia, edema, and vomiting. Grade \ge 3 adverse events occurred in 62% and those occurring in at least 5% of the MZL population included diarrhea-colitis, AST/ALT increased, and neutropenia. Adverse events that led to treatment discontinuation occurred in 23% of patients, most often due to AST/ALT increased and diarrhea-colitis.
L1103: The primary safety issues identified with umbralisib include serious, including fatal, infections and cutaneous reactions, and serious diarrhea or colitis, hepatotoxicity, and neutropenia. In the 371 patients with hematologic malignancies, 51% of patients experienced a grade 3 or 4
L1104: toxicity, the most common including neutropenia (17%), diarrhea-colitis (8%), and increased AST/ALT (6%). Fifteen percent of patients discontinued umbralisib due to an adverse event, most often due to diarrhea-colitis and increased AST/ALT.
L1105: ## **Benefit-Risk:**
L1106: In the single-arm cohort with 69 patients with MZL, patients had to of received prior treatment with 1 or more lines of therapy, including at least 1 anti-CD20-based regimen. The median number of prior systemic therapies was 2 (range 1 to 6) with 22% refractory to their last therapy. The ORR of 49% with associated durability and a tolerable safety profile constitutes a meaningful clinical benefit and a favorable benefit-risk for these patients. To support accelerated approval, the efficacy data is considered in the context of available therapy for patients with MZL in the 2nd line setting and beyond. Available therapy includes rituximab and the immunotherapy combination rituximab plus lenalidomide, which provides response rates ranging from 44% to 65% with associated durability (Table 1). Thus, the ORR of 49% with durability for umbralisib provides data to support a clinically meaningful therapeutic benefit in the context of available therapy for patients with relapsed or refractory MZL in the 2nd line setting and beyond.
L1107: Therefore, the benefit-risk of umbralisib is deemed favorable to support accelerated approval under 21 CFR 314.510 Subpart H for the following indication:
L1108: - Treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory marginal zone lymphoma who have received at least one prior anti-CD20-based regimen.

L1150: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1151: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1152: The review team recommends five postmarketing requirements (PMR) and two postmarketing commitments (PMC). The goal of the postmarketing requirements are to confirm clinical benefit in patients with FL and MZL, determine a safe and appropriate dose in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment, characterize QTc prolongation risk with umbralisib, and evaluate drug-drug interactions. The postmarketing commitments are to provide information on racial and ethnic minorities consistent with the U.S. FL and MZL population and evaluate the PK of umbralisib with a strong CYP3A inducer.
L1153: ## Postmarketing Requirements
L1154: - Conduct a randomized, Phase 3 clinical trial that verifies and describes the clinical benefit of umbralisib in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma and marginal zone lymphoma. The trial should include sufficient numbers of racial and ethnic minority patients to better reflect the U.S. patient population and allow for interpretation of the results in these patient populations. Patients should be randomized to receive immunotherapy with or without umbralisib. The primary endpoint should be progression-free survival, with secondary endpoints that include overall survival and objective response rate
L1155: Final Protocol Submission:	06/2021

Final Report Submission:	06/2027

Design and conduct the study in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling.

L1215: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1216: ## 19.4.1. Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1217: *Were relevant metabolite concentrations measured in the clinical pharmacology and biopharmaceutics studies?*
L1218: Plasma, feces and urine concentrations or amount of umbralisib were measured in clinical pharmacology trials (TGR-1202-902, TGR-1202-PK101, TGR-1202-PK102, TGR-1202-908, TGR-1202-901, TGR-1202-904) and clinical trials (TGR-1202-101, UTX-TGR-205, TGR-1202-102). Based on results of human mass-balance Study TGR-1202-902, umbralisib is the predominant circulating moiety (79%) in plasma.
L1219: *For all moieties measured, is free, bound, or total measured?*
L1220: The total plasma or serum concentrations of umbralisib were measured in the clinical trials. The average binding of umbralisib is about 99.7-99.8%.
L1221: ## *Bioanalytical methods*
L1222: The bioanalytical method validations and performance for measurements of umbralisib concentrations in all clinical trials are summarized in Table 33 (plasma method MET126) and in Table 35 (serum method MET193v1). The plasma method was modified to provide an alternative assay range, i.e., from 1.00 – 2000 ng/mL to 10.0 – 10000 ng/mL. This change was partially validated and is summarized in Table 34 (method MET126 Addendum 1).
L1223: **Table 33: Summary Method Performance for the Determination of Umbralisib Concentrations in Human Plasma (Method MET126)**
L1224: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Analytical method and validation report for the determination of TGR-1202 (base) in human plasma by LC/MS/MS.
Method report MET126 and Addendum 3.
Method description	The method used an analog internal standard, RP-5182. An aliquot of each plasma sample (100 µL) was added to internal standard solution in methanol (750 µL), mixed, and centrifuged. Supernatant was analyzed by LC-MS/MS. Analyte separation was performed with a Synergi Polar RP column with a mobile phase of 25:75 0.1% formic acid in water and acetonitrile run isocratically at a flow rate of 0.4 mL/min. Analytes were detected using an Applied Biosystems Sciex API-3000 triple quadrupole mass spectrometer using turbo ion spray in positive ionization mode.
Materials used for standard calibration curve and concentration	Umbralisib tosylate, lot number: RP-5307/F/1905/45/PTSA.
Stock solution: 1.0 mg/mL in methanol.
Validated assay range	1.08 to 2029.85 ng/mL (and 10.00 to 10060.27 ng/mL [NEBA/STY202]; see Error! Reference source not found..)

L1248: **Table 35: Summary Method Performance for the Determination of Umbralisib Concentrations in Human Serum (Method MET193)**
L1249: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Analytical method and validation report for determination of TGR-1202 in human serum by LC/MS/MS.
Method report MET193 and Addendum 1.
Method description	The method used an analogue internal standard, RP-5182. An aliquot of each serum sample (100 µL) was added to internal standard solution in methanol (750 µL), mixed, and centrifuged. Supernatant was analyzed by LC-MS/MS. Analyte separation was performed with a Phenomenex Synergi Polar RP column with a mobile phase of 25:75 0.1% formic acid in water and acetonitrile run isocratically at a flow rate of 0.4 mL/min. Analytes were detected using an Applied Biosystems Sciex API-3000 triple quadrupole mass spectrometer using turbo ion spray in positive ionization mode.

L1267: ## 19.4.2. Pharmacometrics Review
L1268: A population pharmacokinetics (PPK) model was developed by the Applicant to characterize the PK of umbralisib in patients with select hematologic malignancies, previously treated indolent NHL, MCL, or DLBCL and healthy subjects. Subjects intrinsic and extrinsic factors which influence the PK and PK variability of umbralisib were identified. Individual umbralisib exposure metrics were generated for subsequent exposure-response (E-R) analyses for efficacy and safety. In this review, the FDA Pharmacometrics Reviewer validated the Applicant's PPK model and E-R analyses for both efficacy and safety.
L1269: ## 1. Population PK analyses
L1270: Three clinical trials were included in the PPK analyses: Study 101, 205 and 1102. Total of 2014 PK samples collected from 247 subjects across the three studies were included in the final population PK analysis. Concentration range for the analysis was from 1.08 to 16061.5 ng/mL. In Study 101, the subjects with select hematologic malignancies were administered with [REDACTED] (b)(4) umbralisib with the dose range of 50 to 1800 mg QD under fasted or fed condition. In Study 205, the subjects with previously treated indolent NHL, MCL or DLBCL were administered with [REDACTED] (b)(4) umbralisib at the dose level of 800 mg QD under fed condition. In Study 1102, the healthy subjects were administered with [REDACTED] (b)(4) or [REDACTED] (b)(4) umbralisib at the dose level of 200 mg QD under fasted condition.
L1271: The intrinsic and extrinsic factors of healthy subjects versus subjects with hematological malignancies, disease type (MZL vs FL vs others), age, baseline body weight (BW), sex race (White vs non-White), hepatic function, renal function, and coadministration of strong cytochrome P450 (CYP) 3A inhibitors, strong CYP3A inducers, or acid-reducing agents (ARAs) were evaluated.
L1272: The PK of umbralisib following oral administration was adequately described by a three-compartment disposition model with combined first- and zero-order absorption (Figure 5).
L1273: **Figure 5: Structure of the PK Model for Umbralisib**
L1274: Abbreviations: ka=first-order absorption rate constant; D1=duration of zero-order absorption, CL/F=apparent central clearance; PK=pharmacokinetic; Q2/F=apparent intercompartmental clearance 1; Q3/F=apparent intercompartmental clearance 2; Vc/F=apparent central volume; Vp1=apparent peripheral volume 1; Vp2=apparent peripheral volume 2.
L1275: Source: Applicant's population PK and E-R study report
L1276: The equations for the final model are presented as below:
L1277: CL/F \text{ (L/h)} = 15.5 \times e^{\eta_{CL}}
L1278: Vc/F \text{ (L)} = 312 \times e^{\eta_{Vc}} \times (0.205 \text{ if healthy subject})
L1279: F = 1 \\ \times (0.702 \text{ if in Study 1102}) \\ \times (1.41 \text{ if in Study 205}) \\ \times (1.93 \text{ if DP containing } (b) (4) \text{ DS}) \\ \times (1.38 \text{ if fed condition with DP containing } (b) (4) \text{ DS in Study 101})

L1301: Source: Applicant's population PK and E-R study report
L1302: The Applicant evaluated the impact of concomitant acid reducing agents (ARAs) and strong CYP3A4 inhibitors using population PK analysis to support the labeling claim. For ARAs coadministration, the PPK analysis included 20 subjects who had quantifiable umbralisib concentrations during the period of co-administration of ARAs. Out of the 20 subjects, 6 subjects had intense PK and 14 subjects had sparse PK. For strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, 10 subjects were included in the PPK analysis. Out of the 10 subjects, 4 subjects had intense PK
L1303: and 6 subjects had sparse PK. As shown in Table 38, the change in objective function values (OFV) relative to the base model were all statistically non-significant (p&gt;0.01). Thus, the Applicant determined that the use of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and ARAs had no clinically meaningful impact on the PK of umbralisib.

L1314: *The Applicant's PPK analysis is acceptable for describing the PK of umbralisib in the healthy subjects and select hematologic malignancies patients. The CL of umbralisib estimated by the Applicant's PPK model across different studies are comparable (Figure 8).*

L1317: *The evaluations of the impact of mild/moderate renal impairment on the PK of umbralisib using PPK analysis appear to be reasonable. In the Applicant's PPK analysis for renal impairment, the renal function was categorized based on eGFR estimated by Modification of Diet in Renal Disease (MDRD). FDA Reviewer also evaluated the CL in different renal function groups categorized by creatinine clearance estimated by Cockcroft-Gault equation. As shown in Figure 9, the CL of umbralisib across different renal function groups based on creatinine clearance was comparable, further supporting (b)(4) that no dose adjustment would be needed for mild/moderate renal impairment subjects.*

L1321: *In the Applicant's evaluation of the impact of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and acid reducing agents (ARAs), the frequency and dosing time of the co-administrated strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and ARAs were not clearly provided. In addition, out of the 10 subjects who were co-administrated with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, only 4 of them had intense PK, while 6 subjects only had sparse PK of 1-3 PK samples; out of 20 subjects who were co-administrated with ARAs, only 5 of them had intense PK, while 14 subjects only had sparse PK of mostly 1-3 PK samples. Based on the limited PK data and information on the administration time of concomitant medications, the Applicant's proposal of no dose adjustment for concomitant administration of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors or ARAs is not acceptable based on PPK analysis. As the Applicant conducted dedicated study (TGR-1202-904) to evaluate the effect of ARAs, the result from that study should be used to inform the decision of dose adjustment with co-administrated ARAs.*
L1322: *In the Applicant's evaluation of impact of mild hepatic impairment, only 6 patients with mild hepatic impairment were included. Out of the 6 patients, 4 of them had only 1 PK sample. Although the observed dose-normalized umbralisib concentrations from mild hepatic impairment patients were within the range of those from normal hepatic function patients (Figure 10), the available data were limited to conclude the effect of mild hepatic impairment on PK of umbralisib.*

L1330: ## 2.1 E-R Analysis for Efficacy in MZL Population
L1331: In the E-R analysis for efficacy in MZL population, subjects were primarily male (54%), White (73%), aged 34 to 81 years, had body weight of 49 to 165 kg, and had an Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance score of 0 (54%).
L1332: No significant exposure-efficacy relationships for ORR, TTR, DOR, PFS, and %CfB were identified (Figure 11). Thus, no further modeling was performed by the Applicant. From the univariate E-R analysis, the model predicted ORR at 800 mg QD was 56.9% (90% CI: 43.2, 69.8) (Table 41) and
L1333: the model predicted probability of best %CfB in target lesion dimensions was -52.3% (90% CI: -60.1, -44.3) (Table 42).

L1350: ## **3. E-R Analyses for Safety**
L1351: The exposure-safety dataset included 223 subjects (N=83 from Study 101 and N=140 from Study 205). Of the safety-endpoints evaluated, the AESI with an incidence rate \ge 10% were diarrhea, elevated ALT/AST, rash, neutropenia, pneumonia, and anemia. Among these AESI, only diarrhea (any grade) and elevated ALT/AST (any grade and Grade \ge3) were statistically correlated with umbralisib exposure (Figure 12).
L1352: Figure 12: Relationships between exposure and diarrhea or elevated ALT/AST
L1353: Abbreviations: AIC=Akaike information criterion; ALT=alanine aminotransferase; AST=aspartate aminotransferase; Cav C1=Cycle 1, Day 1 average concentration; Cavss=average concentration at steady state. L1355: For any grade diarrhea, following the covariate analysis, a statistically significant effect of sex on the intercept of the relationship was identified and retained in the final model. For Cav-any grade elevated ALT/AST, following the covariate analysis, statistically significant effects of ECOG and MZL on the slope of the relationship were identified and retained in the final model. No E-R relationships were identified for any grade rash, neutropenia, anemia, thrombocytopenia and pneumonia.
L1356: **Reviewer's comment:**
L1357: *The Applicant's E-R analyses for safety are acceptable. The positive E-R relationships for diarrhea and elevated ALT/AST indicated that increasing the dose in MZL/FL population may lead to higher incidence of adverse events.*
L1358: ## 4. Integrated E-R Analyses
L1359: The Applicant conducted simulation to predict the probability of key efficacy and safety endpoint rates for doses ranging from 400 to 1200 mg QD in MZL and FL populations (Table 43). For exposure-efficacy analysis for FL population, detail refers to Pharmacometrics Review in Appendix in FL Assessment Aid. Figure 13 shows the relative change in event rates relative to the 800 mg QD dose.
L1360: For MZL population, similar ORR was projected at different doses, higher rates of diarrhea and ALT/AST elevation were predicted at higher doses. For FL population, there was a substantial reduction in ORR and tumor shrinkage projected at doses lower than 800 mg QD. On the other hand, any grade and Grade \ge 3 diarrhea was projected to modestly increase by &lt;30%, while any grade and Grade \ge 3 elevated ALT/AST were projected to increase by 62% and 32% for the 1000 mg QD dose, and by 131% and 72% for the 1200 mg QD dose.

L1367: ## **Reviewer's comment:**
L1368: Based on the Applicant's simulation, a lower dose in MZL population may provide better safety profile while maintaining efficacy. However, there is no efficacy data available at doses lower than 800 mg QD in the MZL patients to discern that. For FL population, while the reduced efficacy at lower doses, and increased risk of diarrhea and ALT/AST elevation at higher doses appear to support the proposed 800 mg QD regimen, these projections were potentially limited by small number of subjects at doses outside of 800 mg in the E-R analysis dataset, particularly for efficacy.
L1369: In conclusion, the Applicant developed a PPK model that reasonably described the PK behavior of umbralisib in MZL and FL patients. The PK of umbralisib following oral administration was adequately described by a three-compartment model combined with first- and zero-order absorption after a lag time and first-order elimination. Flat E-R relationships were observed for MZL population while positive E-R relationships were identified for ORR, TTR and %CfB in target

L1375: ## 19.4.3. **Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic (PBPK) Modeling Analysis**
L1376: ## **Executive Summary**
L1377: The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the applicant's physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analyses to evaluate
L1378: - the inhibitory potential of umbralisib on the pharmacokinetics (PK) of substrates of CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19
- the inhibitory and induction potential of umbralisib on the PK of midazolam, a sensitive CYP3A substrate
L1379: The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the PBPK analyses report (R_TGT/1/B) submitted with NDA 213176, responses to FDA's information requests (IRs) submitted on July 24th and 31st, 2020, and the modeling supporting files, and concluded that the effects of umbralisib on CYP3A, CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 could not be confidently predicted using the PBPK modeling approach. The potential of drug-drug interactions (DDIs) of umbralisib with substrates of these enzymes cannot be ruled out.
L1380: ## **Background**
L1381: The proposed umbralisib dosing regimen is 800 mg once daily with food. The maximal dose examined is 1200 mg once daily with food. Following oral administration of umbralisib (b) (4) once daily under the fed condition, umbralisib AUC and Cmax increased approximately dose proportionally across the dose range of 200-mg to 1000-mg on cycle 1 day1 (C1D1) and C2D1, and steady-state was achieved by C3D1. Umbralisib had a long half-life (166 h) and its accumulation ratio ranged from 5.2 to 11.3 for AUC0-τ and 2.8 to 5.9 for Cmax across the doses from 200- to 1200-mg.
L1382: Umbralisib was determined in vitro to be an inhibitor and inducer of CYP3A, and an inhibitor of CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19. The applicant intended to use a PBPK modeling approach to evaluate the potential of drug interactions of umbralisib with the substrates of these CYP enzymes. Refer to the Clinical Pharmacology review section for detail information on umbralisib regarding its ADME properties, in vitro and clinical studies used in PBPK modeling.
L1383: ## **Methods**
L1384: All simulations were performed using the PK/PD Profiles mode in the Simcyp® Simulator (Version 18 Certara, Sheffield, UK). A scheme of the PBPK simulation strategy is shown in the figure below, which summarizes the studies used for model development and verification, and model applications in DDI predictions with CYP substrates. The model structure, model optimization and final model parameters for the umbralisib PBPK model are summarized in the table below. For prediction of DDIs with umbralisib as a perpetrator, Simcyp library substrate files of midazolam, repaglinide, tolbutamide, S-mephentytoin were used without any modifications. All simulations were completed using the sim-Healthy Volunteer population aged 20-50 years with 50% female subjects under the fed condition. All DDI simulations were conducted after 168 days of hypothetical QD umbralisib administration to ensure a maximum systemic umbralisib exposure for DDI prediction.

L1392: ## Results
L1393: ## 1. Can the PBPK model adequately describe the PK profiles of umbralisib?
L1394: Yes, the PBPK model of umbralisib described umbralisib PK following administration of multiple doses of umbralisib in cancer patients (Figures 15 - 16 and Table 45).

L1403: ## Additional comments:
L1404: The multiple-dose PK data from the 800-mg umbralisib cohort (n=3) in Study TGR-1202-101 were used to build and optimize the umbralisib PBPK model during the model development. (b) (4) drug product was given under the fed condition in this study. Figures 15 - 16 and Table 45 show that the model could reasonably well recover umbralisib plasma concentrations in cancer patients, and the estimated exposures (Cmax and AUC0-24h) were mostly within 25% of the values observed or estimated from population PK (PopPK) analysis.
L1405: It should be noted that the applicant developed the PBPK model of umbralisib to predict DDI in cancer patients. However, all simulations were performed using the default sim-Healthy Volunteer population model. To apply the simulation results, there are two underlying assumptions. One is that umbralisib PK in healthy subjects is similar to that in cancer patients. This assumption may be supported by umbralisib PK at 200-mg dose in healthy subjects (TRG-1202-PK101) and cancer patients (TGR-1202-101) as shown in Figure 17 below. The second assumption is that there is no difference in enzyme abundance or activity of CYP3A and CYP2Cs between healthy subjects and cancer patients. This is the case for sim-Healthy Volunteer population and sim-Cancer population models. However, there are evidences that CYP enzyme abundance or activity may be altered in cancer patients (Coutant DE 2015; Schwenger H 2018). Therefore, the simulation results should be interpreted with caution.
L1406: Figure 17: PK parameters following oral administration of a single dose of 200-mg umbralisib
(b) (4) under the fasted condition
L1407: Source: Figure 4-4, the population PK analysis (TGTX-PMX-UMBRAISIB-1100-001)
L1408: ## 2. Can PBPK analyses predict the effects of umbralisib on the pharmacokinetics of CYP substrates?
L1409: No, the PBPK analyses are inadequate to predict the complex inhibitory and induction effects on CYP3A, and the effects of umbralisib on the PK of the substrates of CYP2C8 (repaglinide), CYP2C9 (tolbutamide) and CYP2C19 (S-mephentytoin) due to the uncertainties about in vitro to in vivo extrapolation. (b) (4)
L1410: (b) (4)
L1411: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L1412: Source: the reviewer's analysis
L1413: ## Conclusions
L1414: The effects of umbralisib on CYP3A, CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 cannot be confidently predicted using the PBPK modeling approach. The potential of drug-drug interactions of umbralisib with substrates of these enzymes cannot be ruled out.

L1460: ## 1 Executive Summary
L1461: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L1462: Umbralisib is a phosphoinositide-3-kinase-delta (PI3K\delta) inhibitor in development for the treatment of patients with hematologic malignancies. While both the gamma and the delta isoforms of PI3K are restricted to cells of hematopoietic origin, the delta isoform has the highest expression on leukocytes. Umbralisib also inhibits Casein Kinase 1 epsilon (CK1\epsilon) which regulates the non-canonical Wnt/planar cell polarity pathway. This pathway is involved in lymphoid malignancy pathogenesis via regulation of cell survival, proliferation, chemotaxis, and migration. Regulation of the Wnt/\beta-catenin pathway also modulates T-cell transcription factor-1, which affects the development of regulatory T cells (Tregs). The Applicant purports that this support of regulatory T-cells is the mechanism by which T-regs clones are supported, and the development of immune-related toxicity may be potentially mitigated.
L1463: On 15 June 2020, TG Therapeutics submitted Part 2 of their rolling NDA 213176 submission under 21 CFR 314.50 and Section 505(b)(1) of the Federal Food, Drug and Cosmetic Act, seeking accelerated approval of umbralisib at the recommended dose of 800 mg by mouth daily for the following indication:
L1464: - Treatment of adult patients with follicular lymphoma (FL) who have received at least (b) (4) prior systemic therapies.
L1465: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L1466: The review team recommends accelerated approval of umbralisib for:
L1467: - Treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma (FL) who have received at least three prior lines of systemic therapy.
L1468: The recommended dose is 800 mg orally once daily.
L1469: This recommendation is based on efficacy findings from a single-arm cohort of Study UTX-TGR-205, an open-label, multi-center, single-arm, multi-cohort trial which included 131 patients with relapsed or refractory FL following at least two prior systemic therapies, including an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent. The trial excluded patients with Grade 3b FL, large cell transformation, prior allogeneic transplant, history of CNS lymphoma, and prior exposure to a PI3K inhibitor. Patients received umbralisib 800 mg orally once daily until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The primary endpoint was overall response rate (ORR) assessed by an independent review committee (IRC) based on modified 2007 International Working Group criteria. Secondary endpoints included duration of response and progression-free survival.
L1470: For efficacy, the Applicant provided data for 117 patients with FL. The median age was 65 years (range: 29 to 87 years), 38% were female, 80% were White, 4% were Black, 73% had Stage III-IV disease, 38% had bulky disease and 97% had a baseline ECOG performance status of 0 to 1. The median number of prior systemic therapies was 3 (range 1 to 10) and 57% of the patients had received three or more regimens and 36% were refractory to their last therapy. The median follow-up time was 20.1 months (range: 13.5 to 29.6 months). The overall response rate per IRC was 42.7% (95% confidence interval [CI]: 33.6, 52.2), with 3.4% achieving a complete response. The median duration of response was 11.1 months (95% CI: 8.3, 16.4).
L1471: The review team has recommended accelerated approval of umbralisib for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory FL who have received at least three prior lines of systemic therapy. This contrasts with the UTX-TGR-205 eligibility criteria and the Applicant's proposed indication for those who have received at least two prior systemic therapies. The ORR of 42.7% with a lower bound of the confidence interval of 33.6% from a single-arm trial provides evidence of uncertainty regarding the clinical benefit of umbralisib in these patients. Further, to support accelerated approval, the efficacy data is considered in the context of available therapy for patients with FL in the 3rd line setting and beyond. Available therapy includes anti-CD20 therapy (i.e., rituximab and obinutuzumab) as a single agent and in combination, bendamustine monotherapy and in combination, and immunotherapy combinations such as rituximab plus lenalidomide (Table 4). In the 3rd line setting and beyond, consistent with the patient population enrolled in Study UTX-TGR-205, the overall response rates for these approved and available therapies, including single-agent and combinations, range from 57% to 80% with associated durability (Table 6). Thus, in the context of available therapy, there is residual uncertainty regarding the benefit of umbralisib in patients with relapsed or refractory FL who have received at least 2 prior systemic therapies. Based on the ORR of 42.7%, the lower bound of the confidence interval, and the higher response rates of available therapies in the intended patient population, the recommended indication is based on the characteristics of the FL patient population enrolled in UTX-TGR-205, specifically the
L1472: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L1473: median of 3 prior lines of therapy, for which the data support the determination that umbralisib has clinically meaningful activity and a favorable benefit-risk evaluation. Therefore, the recommend indication is for adult patients with relapsed or refractory FL who have received at least three prior lines of systemic therapy.
L1474: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment (BRA)
L1475: ## **Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment**
L1476: Umbralisib is a specific and orally bioavailable dual kinase inhibitor of phosphoinositide-3-kinase-delta (PI3Kδ) and casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1ε).
L1477: ## **Efficacy:**
L1478: Efficacy of umbralisib in adult patients with relapsed or refractory (R/R) follicular lymphoma (FL) is based on the results from a single-arm cohort of Study UTX-TGR-205, an open-label, multi-center, single-arm, multi-cohort trial which included 131 patients with FL. Patients received umbralisib 800 mg twice daily continuously until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The primary endpoint was overall response rate (ORR) assessed by an independent review committee (IRC) based on modified 2007 International Working Group criteria. Among the 117 patients evaluable for efficacy, the median follow-up time was 20.1 months (range: 13.5 to 29.6 months). The overall response rate per IRC was 42.7% (95% CI: 33.6, 52.2), with 3.4% achieving a complete response. The median duration of response was 11.1 months (95% CI: 8.3, 16.4).
L1479: ## **Safety:**
L1480: The safety profile of umbralisib was supported by analysis of the 131 patients with follicular lymphoma from Study UTX-TGR-205 and a combined safety database of 371 patients with hematologic malignancies [marginal zone lymphoma (22%), follicular lymphoma (40%), diffuse large B-cell lymphoma or mantle cell lymphoma (20%), and other hematologic malignancies (18%)] who received single-agent umbralisib therapy.
L1481: In 131 patients with relapsed or refractory FL, the median duration of treatment was 7.3 months (range 1 day to 22.5 months) and the most common adverse events (≥20% of patients) were diarrhea-colitis, nausea, fatigue, musculoskeletal pain, vomiting, upper respiratory tract infection and abdominal pain. Treatment-emergent laboratory abnormalities of any grade occurring in ≥ 20% of patients were increased creatinine (75%), neutropenia (32%), anemia (28%), increased ALT/AST (27%) and thrombocytopenia (24%). Serious adverse events occurred in 22% of patients and no fatal adverse events occurred. Dose discontinuations due to an adverse event was reported in 12% of patients, most often due to diarrhea-colitis.
L1482: The primary safety issues identified with umbralisib include serious, including fatal, infections and cutaneous reactions, and serious diarrhea or colitis, hepatotoxicity, and neutropenia. In the 371 patients with hematologic malignancies, 51% of patients experienced a grade 3 or 4 toxicity, the most common including neutropenia (17%), diarrhea-colitis (8%), and increased AST/ALT (6%). Fifteen percent of patients discontinued umbralisib due to an adverse event, most often due to diarrhea-colitis and increased AST/ALT.
L1483: ## **Benefit-Risk:**
L1484: Umbralisib has an overall favorable benefit-risk in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma; however, the benefit-risk determination was considered in the context of available therapy to support an accelerated approval.
L1485: The eligibility criteria for Study UTX-TGR-205 required patients with relapsed or refractory FL to have received at least two prior systemic therapies. The patients with FL enrolled had a median number of 3 prior therapies, 36% were refractory to their last therapy, and 31% were refractory to rituximab. The ORR of 42.7% (95% CI: 33.6%, 52.2%) warrants additional consideration in the context of available therapy in the 3rd line setting and beyond. Available therapy includes anti-CD20 therapy (i.e., rituximab and obinutuzumab) as a single agent and in combination, bendamustine monotherapy and in combination, and immunotherapy combinations such as rituximab plus lenalidomide. In the 3rd line setting and beyond, the ORRs for these therapies, including single-agent and combinations, ranges from 57% to 80% with associated durability. Taking into consideration the ORR of 42.7% with a lower bound of the confidence interval of 33.6%, and available therapies in a comparable treatment setting, the review team determined that the residual uncertainty regarding the benefit of umbralisib in these patients warrants restriction of the indication to patients with relapsed or refractory FL who have received 3 prior lines of systemic therapy, reflective of the patient population enrolled in Study UTX-TGR-205 and for which the data support the determination that umbralisib has clinically meaningful activity and a favorable benefit-risk evaluation.
L1486: Therefore, the benefit-risk of umbralisib is deemed favorable to support accelerated approval under 21 CFR 314.510 Subpart H for the following indication:
L1487: - Treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma who have received at least three prior lines of systemic therapy.
L1488: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	Follicular Lymphoma (FL) is considered incurable, and relapse is nearly universal.
With successive treatment regimens for relapsed or refractory disease, response rates and response duration tend to diminish. Patients with symptoms continue to receive treatment repeatedly until fatal resistant disease occurs.
Relapsed and refractory follicular lymphoma is a heterogenous disease and may have an indolent course.
Early relapse from first treatment, refractory disease and transformed lymphoma are associated with a poor prognosis.
Relapsed and refractory follicular lymphoma is a serious and life-threatening disease.
Current Treatment Options	Treatment options for relapsed and refractory FL include chemotherapy, immunochemotherapy, and anti-CD20 monoclonal antibodies.
Numerous patients with relapsed or refractory FL cannot tolerate intensive chemotherapy due to age or comorbidities.
Approved chemoimmunotherapy and immunotherapy combinations have response rates of 55%-80% in the 3rd line setting and beyond, including 57%-79% in the 3rd and 4th line setting, which includes patients with rituximab refractory disease.
New treatments are needed for patients with relapsed or refractory FL, including chemotherapy-free treatments.
Treatment options should be considered in the context of prior therapies and patient comorbidities
L1489: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Benefit	Umbralisib was administered at 800 mg orally once daily
Study UTX-TGR-205 was an open-label, multi-center, single-arm, multi-cohort trial which included 131 patients with relapsed or refractory FL.
Overall response rate per independent review committee was 42.7% (95% CI: 33.6%, 52.2%) with a median duration of response of 11.1 months based on data from 117 patients with FL who were evaluable for efficacy.
Based on ORR with durability, umbralisib has clinically meaningful activity in patients with relapsed or refractory FL who have received at least 3 prior systemic therapies.
Risk and Risk Management	Of 131 patients with follicular lymphoma treated with umbralisib 800 mg daily:The median duration of treatment was 7.3 months (range 1, 22.5 months)
The most common adverse events (\ge 20\% of patients) were diarrhea-colitis, nausea, fatigue, musculoskeletal pain, vomiting, upper respiratory tract infection and abdominal pain.
Laboratory abnormalities of any grade occurring in \ge 20\% of patients were increase in creatinine (75%), neutropenia (32%), anemia (28%), increase in ALT/AST (27%) and thrombocytopenia (24%).
Serious adverse events occurred in 22% of patients and no fatal adverse events occurred.
Dose discontinuations due to any adverse events were reported in 12% of patients with FL, most often due to diarrhea-colitis.

The safety profile of umbralisib is acceptable in the intended patient population.
The USPI includes warnings and precautions for infections, neutropenia, diarrhea or colitis, hepatotoxicity, and severe cutaneous reactions.

L1501: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L1502: ## 2 Therapeutic Context
L1503: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L1504: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1505: Follicular lymphoma (FL) is the second most common non-Hodgkin lymphoma (NHL) and the most common (70%) indolent non-Hodgkin lymphoma (iNHL), with an estimated 15,000 new United States (US) cases annually (Freedman 2018; National Cancer Institute Published 2019). Its prevalence is strongly associated with age, with a median age at diagnosis of 63 years and an incidence of 2.8 per 100,000 person-years for people under 65 years of age and 14.7 per 100,000 person-years for people 65 years and over (Carbone et al. 2019). The majority of patients with FL has advanced-stage disease at diagnosis. Based on Surveillance, Epidemiology, and End Results statistics from 2009 to 2015, 88.4% of patients survive 5 years or more after diagnosis. Transformed follicular center B cells comprise mainly centrocytes with variable amounts of centroblasts, the frequency of which corresponds to the grade and the aggressiveness of the disease (grade 1, 2, or 3, depending on centroblast percentage) (Ma 2017). Approximately 85% of patients with FL have a translocation of chromosomes 14 and 18, resulting in the overexpression of BCL-2, which blocks apoptosis, and in additional gene mutations altering normal T-cell function within the malignant microenvironment that contribute to the pathobiology of the disease (Ghielmini 2010) and to a high degree of mutational heterogeneity in FL both from patient to patient and intra patient tumor diversity (Carbone et al. 2019).
L1506: The underlying biology of FL contributes to its disseminated presentation, varied prognosis, recurrent relapse, and progressive risk of transformation to high grade lymphoma. Even though patients are initially asymptomatic, a majority have advanced-stage disease at diagnosis, with 70% displaying bone marrow involvement (Freedman 2018). Asymptomatic patients are typically managed with observation until they meet the GELF criteria, which provide guidance on whether FL symptoms warrant treatment (Brice et al. 1997). FLIPI indices attempt to stratify patients into risk categories predictive of overall survival; however, the strongest determinant of survival is progression-free survival (PFS) at 2 years following first-line treatment with chemoimmunotherapy (Freedman 2018, Maurer et al. 2016). The introduction of rituximab has been a driving factor behind the improvement in FL survival rates (National Cancer Institute Published 2019).
L1507: Despite these therapeutic advances, FL remains incurable, with patients experiencing multiple relapses until ultimately succumbing to the disease. Furthermore, response rates progressively
L1508: diminish at each subsequent line of therapy, and the risk of developing transformed disease increases over time, with an incidence rate of 28% after 10 years (Montoto et al. 2007). FL patients who experience disease transformation have a substantially shorter median survival of 1.2 years from transformation.
L1509: The FDA's Assessment:
The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1510: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L1511: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1512: Currently, no standard-of-care treatment exists for FL following 2 prior therapies. In the relapsed or refractory setting, treatments vary depending on the clinical presentation, age, prior therapies, and overall health. Consensus guidelines (NCCN 2020) offer a variety of options for first-line therapy of FL with chemo-immunotherapy regimens including bendamustine+rituximab or rituximab, cyclophosphamide, doxorubicin, vincristine, and prednisone (R-CHOP), with milder regimens suggested for patients who are elderly or infirm, including rituximab monotherapy. Second-line and subsequent therapeutic options include repeat chemo-immunotherapy, lenalidomide+rituximab, ibrutinib, radioimmunotherapy, phosphoinositide 3-kinase (PI3K) inhibitors, or rituximab monotherapy. Treatment options are limited for patients who have received 2 or more prior therapies, with no Food and Drug Administration (FDA)-approved options specifically for this patient population.
L1513: A summary of FDA approved treatment options in the relapsed or refractory FL setting is shown in Table 1.
L1514: **Table 1: Summary of FDA Approved Treatment Options in the Relapsed or Refractory FL Setting**

L1521: Source: Drugs@FDA
L1522: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L1523: While advances in treatment options have produced increases in overall survival, FL remains an incurable, life-threatening disease plagued by recurrent remissions. Furthermore, responses shorten with each subsequent line of therapy, highlighting the need for additional treatment options.
L1524: For relapsed/refractory FL, after 2 lines of systemic therapies, there are no fully approved treatment options; however, PI3K inhibitors idelalisib, copanlisib, and duvelisib have been granted accelerated approval with each carrying toxicities and dosing considerations which have limited their use. This paucity of therapeutic options represents a high unmet medical need for this serious and life-threatening condition.
L1525: FL is considered incurable and most patients eventually relapse or progress (Rivas-Delgado et al. 2019). Additionally, the response duration shortens after each relapse even in patients treated with rituximab combinations as the first-line option. Limited non-chemotherapeutic options exist for patients with relapsed or refractory disease following an anti-cluster of differentiation 20 (CD20) monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent. Therefore, new treatments with alternative mechanisms are needed. There remains an unmet need for new drugs that will provide clinically meaningful efficacy with differentiated toxicity profiles.
L1526: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1527: The FDA disagrees with the assertion that there are no FDA approved therapies for patients with FL who have received at least 2 prior therapies. Rituximab, bendamustine, bendamustine plus obinutuzumab, and lenalidomide plus a rituximab-product all have regular approval for patients with relapsed or refractory FL, which is inclusive of those patients with FL who have received at least 2 prior therapies. In addition, Table 1 above incorrectly indicates "Regular approval" for Idelalisib. In 2014, The FDA granted accelerated approval to Idelalisib for treatment of patients with relapsed follicular B-cell NHL who have received at least two prior systemic therapies. Furthermore, the table does not reflect the other approved therapies for patients with FL. The table below provides additional information:

L1574: ## 4 Significant Issues from Other Review Disciplines Pertinent to Clinical Conclusions on Efficacy and Safety
L1575: ## 4.1. Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)
L1576: Clinical site inspections were requested for two clinical sites in the U.S. with the highest accrual for Study UTX-TGR-205, along with inspection of the Applicant, TG Therapeutics. The clinical sites included 1) Site HOB - MD Anderson (Felipe Samaniego, MD; Houston, TX), which enrolled 24 patients and 2) Site CLA - Levine Cancer Institute (Nilanjan Ghosh, MD; Charlotte, NC), which enrolled 19 patients. Based on the clinical site inspections, the study data from the clinical sites were considered reliable. Additionally, the Applicant was inspected concerning its oversight of Study UTX-TGR-205. Based on the inspection, the Applicant's oversight of the clinical trial and the quality of conducting the study were determined to be adequate. The audited study data submitted to the Agency was deemed acceptable in support of the NDA application, in compliance with Good Clinical Practice.
L1577: ## 4.2. Product Quality
L1578: Umbralisib tosylate is a small chiral BCS class II molecule that is produced as a single enantiomer that is manufactured by Alembic Pharmaceuticals Limited, India, as a single enantiomer in a linear, two-stage synthesis. The drug product is presented as a 200 mg immediate-release tablet for oral administration. It is a green film-coated, oval-shaped tablet, debossed with "L474" on one side and plain on the other.
L1579: The recommended starting dose for the drug product is 800 mg orally, once daily until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L1580: Based on the information provided by the Applicant, the Office of Product Quality (OPQ) has determined that the data is adequately supportive and the potential risks to patient safety, product efficacy, and product quality are mitigated appropriately. Therefore, OPQ recommends approval of umbralisib and grants a (b)(4) month retest for the drug substance and a 36-month shelf-life for the drug product when stored in HDPE bottles at 20°C and 25°C (68°F to 77°F).
L1581: ## 4.3. Clinical Microbiology
L1582: The data to support the microbiology assessment were adequate and there were no issues from a microbiology standpoint.
L1583: ## 4.4. Devices and Companion Diagnostic Issues
L1584: Not applicable.
L1585: ## 5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L1586: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L1587: Umbralisib (also known as RP-5264, TGR-1202, [REDACTED](b) (4) and RP-5307) is a small molecule inhibitor of phosphoinositide-3-kinase delta (PI3K\delta) and casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1\epsilon). The targets mediate tumor-progressive signaling in a variety of hematologic malignancies.
L1588: Umbralisib binds to human PI3K\delta and CK1\epsilon with dissociation constants (Kd) of 6.2 nM and 180 nM, respectively. A screen of 442 kinases incubated with 1 \muM of umbralisib showed that the phosphorylated Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1 containing the mutation F317I (ABL1 (F317I)-phosphorylated) kinase was inhibited at levels comparable to CK1\epsilon. In vitro growth-inhibitory activity was assessed in a panel of 13 human leukemia and lymphoma cell lines. Growth inhibitory (GI50) values ranged from 0.6 to 66 \muM after the cells were treated for 72 or 96 hours with umbralisib. The test article also inhibited cytokine-mediated adhesion and migration in vitro.
L1589: The in vivo antitumor activity of umbralisib was evaluated in a MOLT-4 xenograft model. MOLT-4 tumor-bearing mice were dosed orally with umbralisib at 150 mg/kg/day for 25 days, with the measurement of tumor growth continuing until 41 days after dosing initiation. The test article dosing group had tumors growing at a slower rate than the control group tumors.
L1590: Safety pharmacology studies assessed the effects of umbralisib on cardiovascular, central nervous system (CNS), and respiratory function. Umbralisib had no toxicologically-significant effects on cardiovascular function in a cardiovascular study in dogs, and had no effects on neurobehavioral function or temperature in mice. Respiratory function was affected by umbralisib with decreases in tidal volume as much as 24%, respiration rate as much as 38%, and minute volume as much as 46% observed in respiratory studies conducted in mice administered a single umbralisib dose of 50 to 500 mg/kg.
L1591: The oral bioavailability of umbralisib in mice and dogs were 47% and 34% respectively for the 2 species. A mass balance study in male mice demonstrated that 89% and 5% of umbralisib is excreted in feces and urine, respectively. Umbralisib metabolism was evaluated in vitro with liver microsomes. After 60 minutes of incubation with mouse, dog, and human liver microsomes, 75%, 77%, and 86% respectively of umbralisib remained.
L1592: The repeat-dose studies in mice and dogs referenced above were conducted to assess the general toxicity of umbralisib. The drug was administered once daily for 26 weeks in mice and 39 weeks in dogs, with the doses and durations informed by the experience of the 13-week repeat-dose studies in both species. The studies were conducted using the oral route of administration, which is consistent with the intended clinical route of administration. There were several unscheduled deaths in the mouse study, while dose reductions and dosing holidays were applied to the dog study. Drug-related findings included decreased white blood cells (WBCs) and differentials, increased liver weights and alkaline phosphatase (ALP) levels, and lymphoid depletion.
L1593: In the 26-week mouse study, umbralisib was administered by oral gavage at 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day with 4 weeks of recovery. Umbralisib-induced mortality was observed in the study with half of all observed mortalities and unscheduled euthanasia's occurring in the 300 mg/kg dose group. The exact causes of death in these animals were undetermined. In the surviving animals, toxicities included reduced lymphocyte levels, increased liver weights and ALP levels, and inflammatory findings. In the 39-week dog study, dogs were initially dosed orally with umbralisib at 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg/day, but doses were reduced to 8, 20, and 50 mg/kg/day due to notable weight loss as the study proceeded. The high-dose group (100/50 mg/kg/day) also had a dosing holiday due to continued weight loss. Reduced RBCs, hemoglobin, hematocrit, and reticulocyte levels were observed in all umbralisib-dosed conditions. Microscopic findings were limited to decreased cellularity in the lymph nodes and the thymus. Additional findings of decreased weight and the presence of debris in the lumen, and tubular degeneration and atrophy in the testes were noted in the 13-week repeat-dose study in dogs.
L1594: A combined male and female fertility study was conducted in mice with umbralisib administered once daily at doses of 50, 150, or 300 mg/kg/day, starting 28 days prior to pairing and through mating in males and starting 14 days prior to pairing through Gestation Day (GD) 7 in females. Umbralisib-related findings included reduced testis and epididymis weights, and reduced sperm count and motility in the mid- and high-dose groups. Findings in female mice included increased post-implantation loss and increased resorption. Exposure at 150 mg/kg/day is approximately 1.3 times the recommended human clinical dose based on the toxicokinetic (AUC) values.
L1595: In an embryo-fetal development study, daily oral administration of umbralisib at 100, 200, or 400 mg/kg/day to pregnant mice on GD 6-15 resulted in increased litter losses, visceral findings and fetal skeletal abnormalities in all dose groups. Malformations noted were increased frequency of cleft palates in the mid- and high-dose groups and low incidence nasopharyngeal fistula (one fetus) at high dose. The 100, 200, and 400 mg/kg groups had exposures that were 0.9, 1.4, and 2.4 times the exposures at the recommended clinical dose based on AUC. In an embryo-fetal development study in female rabbits, daily administration of umbralisib at 30,
L1596: 100, or 300 mg/kg/day on GD 6-19 resulted in maternal toxicity characterized by a 50% reduction in food intake and an 8% reduction in body weight by the end of the dosing period. Fetal weights were lower in the high-dose group. Elevated fetal abnormalities were noted in the mid- and high-dose groups compared to the concurrent controls, although the levels were considered within the normal range for this strain of rabbit. The 30, 100, or 300 mg/kg/day doses resulted in exposures that were 0.03, 0.08, and 0.03 times the exposures at the recommended clinical dose based on AUC. There was high inter-animal variability in the toxicokinetic values in this study within each dose group, reduced exposures at high dose, and lack of dose-dependence in exposures observed in rabbits.
L1597: Umbralisib was not mutagenic in the in vitro bacterial reverse mutation test or clastogenic in either the in vitro chromosomal aberrations assay or in the in vivo bone marrow micronucleus assay in mice. No carcinogenicity studies have been conducted or are warranted to support the marketing of umbralisib for the current indications.
L1598: The nonclinical pharmacology and toxicology data submitted to this NDA are adequate to support the approval of umbralisib for the proposed indications.

L1603: ## 5.3. Pharmacology
L1604: ## Primary pharmacology
L1605: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1606: In vitro and in vivo pharmacology studies conducted with umbralisib assessed the mechanism of action and the pharmacological activity. The binding affinity for CK1\epsilon and PI3K was determined with a competitive binding assay based on phage display (Study # TGT027-01-S-00001). Umbralisib was incubated with CK1\epsilon and PI3K variants \alpha, \beta, \delta, and \gamma for an hour at room temperature prior to an assessment of binding affinity (Kd). The resulting Kds were 180 nM for CK1\epsilon and 6.2 for PI3K\delta. The Kds for the other PI3K variants were in the \muM range.
L1607: ## Inhibitory activity of umbralisib
L1608: Umbralisib Kd (nM)
Kinase	CK1\epsilon	PI3K\alpha	PI3K\beta	PI3K\delta	PI3K\gamma
L1609: 180	>10000	>10000	6.2	1400
L1610: The selectivity of umbralisib was determined by evaluating its binding to a panel of 442 kinases that included PI3K variants and CK1\epsilon (Study # INO006-01-P-00001). Using the same platform that determined the Kd for umbralisib, the test article was incubated with the panel of kinases at 1 \muM for an hour at room temperature prior an assessment of binding inhibition normalized to control conditions. Beyond the intended targets, phosphorylated Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1 containing the mutation F317I (ABL1 (F317I)-phosphorylated) was the only kinase in the panel that was inhibited by the test article at levels beyond the 65% threshold. With an inhibition level of 72%, ABL1 was inhibited at levels comparable to CK1\epsilon, which had an inhibition level of 73% in the same assay.
L1611: The in vitro inhibitory effect of umbralisib on malignant cell proliferation was evaluated with a panel of 13 leukemia and lymphoma cell lines (RPMI-8226, U266B1, MM1.S, MM1.R, DLBCL, Toledo, Daudi, Raji, CCRF-CEM, MOLT-4, U937, THP-1, and Jurkat. Study # IVT-5264-LCA-01). Umbralisib was incubated with the cell lines for 72 or 96 hours at 37°C. Growth was determined with a 3-(4,5-Dimethylthiazol-2-yl)-2,5-diphenyltetrazolium bromide (MTT) assay and normalized to untreated conditions. Growth inhibitory (GI_{50}) values ranged from 14.9 \muM (Daudi, a Burkitt's lymphoma cell line) to 0.58 \muM (Toledo, a DLBCL/non-Hodgkin's lymphoma cell line).

L1615: To determine if umbralisib inhibits cell adhesion (Study # VT-5264-VCAD-29), Daudi cells were treated with the test article at 1 \muM for an hour prior to a brief plating on VCAM- and CXCL12- or CCL19-coated plates with centrifugation at room temperature. The plates were washed and the remaining cells were fixed and stained prior to plate reading for absorbance at 570 nm. Umbralisib inhibited CXCL12-mediated adhesion by 50% and CCL19-mediated adhesion by 70%.
L1616: Umbralisib inhibition of cell migration was also evaluated with Daudi cells (Study # IVT-5264- VCMIG-28). The cells were added to VCAM-coated migration chamber inserts which were then added to chambers containing CXCL12, CXCL13, or CCL19, with umbralisib at 1 \muM for 5 hours at room temperature. Cells that migrated to the underside of the migration chamber insert were stained prior to plate reading for absorbance at 570 nm. Umbralisib inhibited CXCL12- and CCL19-mediated migration by 70% and CXCL13-mediated migration by 80%.
L1617: Umbralisib's in vivo antitumor activity was evaluated in a Molt-4 xenograft model (Study # Incozen-CP-P006). The Molt-4 cell line was established subcutaneously in female NOD/SCID mice prior to oral dosing with umbralisib at 150 mg/kg for 25 days. Tumors were measured 3 times per week and the mice were weighed weekly. The tumor volumes were determined and normalized to the untreated condition. A group of mice were dosed with Ara-C as a dosing control with known antitumor effects on the model. The animals were euthanized on day 41. Umbralisib-dosed animals had reduced tumor growth rates compared to untreated animals.
L1618: ## **Antitumor activity of umbralisib (ICZ-515; excerpted from Applicant's submission)**
L1619: ## **Secondary Pharmacology**
L1620: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1621: Umbralisib demonstrated selectivity against a panel of 442 kinases in a KINOMEscan™ competition binding assay. At a fixed concentration of 1 \mu\text{M}, umbralisib inhibited ligand binding of only 3 kinases at greater than the 65% inhibition cutoff: PI3K\delta (100% inhibition), casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1\epsilon) (73% inhibition), and phosphorylated Abelson murine leukemia viral oncogene homolog 1 (ABL1)(F317I)-phosphorylated (72% inhibition).
L1622: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1623: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that umbralisib at a concentration of 1 \mu\text{M} inhibited 3 out of 442 kinases at levels greater than 65%. One of the 3 kinases, ABL1(F317I), is not an intended target and was inhibited at levels comparable to CK1\epsilon at the concentration tested.
L1624: ## **Safety Pharmacology**
L1625: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1626: In a cell-based human ether-à-go-go-related gene (hERG) assay, the \text{IC}_{50} for umbralisib was found to exceed 100 \mu\text{M} (highest tested concentration), a concentration which is beyond the relevant human concentrations of umbralisib in the circulation. In electrocardiogram (ECG) evaluations in dogs, oral doses of umbralisib up to 250 mg/kg (equivalent to 5,000 \text{mg}/\text{m}^2 or 9,722 mg in a 70 kg human) did not result in any changes in cardiovascular parameters. In a
L1627: central nervous system (CNS) evaluation in mice, after a single dose of umbralisib at doses up to 300 mg/kg (equivalent to 900 mg/m2 or 1707 mg in a 70 kg human), there were no changes in functional observational battery observations. In mice, after a single dose of umbralisib at doses up to 500 mg/kg (equivalent to 1,500 mg/m2 or 2917 mg in a 70 kg human), there were dose-dependent decreases in tidal volume, respiration rate, and minute volume, which were considered minimal and non-adverse.
L1628: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1629: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary for safety pharmacology. Additional details regarding the safety pharmacology assessments are provided below.
L1630: ## **A. Central Nervous System**
L1631: CD-1 mice that were part of the 26-week GLP toxicology study were subjected to a CNS functional observation battery (FOB). The animals were dosed with umbralisib daily with 30, 100, or 300 mg/kg. Overall, umbralisib had no significant effects on neurobehavioral function at doses up to 300 mg/kg.
L1632: ## **B. Respiratory**
L1633: In a study evaluating the effects on respiratory function (Study # 037274), CD-1 mice were administered a single oral dose of umbralisib (50, 150, or 500 mg/kg) or vehicle (0.5% methylcellulose and 10% PS80 in water). Respiratory measurements including tidal volume, respiratory rate, and derived minute volume, were taken at 0.25 through 8 hours after dosing. Compared to control conditions, umbralisib caused a 24% decrease in tidal volume at the high dose by the end of the testing period and transient decreases in respiratory rate (lowest of 36% and 38% at 1 hour for the 50 and 150 mg/kg groups, and 24% at 1.75 hours for the 500 mg/kg) and tidal volume at all doses tested (lowest of 11% at 2.25 hours for the 50 mg/kg group, and 15% at 4.25 hours for the 150 mg/kg group). Minute volumes in the umbralisib-treated conditions were at their relative lowest between 1 and 2 hours after dosing, with maximum differences of 43% and 47% for the 50 and 150 mg/kg groups at 1 hour, and 39% for the 500 mg/kg group at 1.75 hours.
L1634: ## **C. Cardiovascular**
L1635: An in vitro hERG channel inhibition study (Study # CYP0358-R10) with hERG-expressing cells incubated with umbralisib at concentrations up 100 µM showed that the test article had an IC50 greater than the highest dose tested. In vivo cardiovascular assessments were conducted as part of a 13-week repeat dose general toxicology study in dogs. ECG examinations showed that umbralisib at doses 30, 100, or 250 mg/kg did not result in any umbralisib-related effects on CV system parameters.
L1636: ## 5.4. ADME/PK
L1637: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1638: After a single oral dose, umbralisib absorption was faster in mice, with a time to maximum concentration (T_{max}) of 0.5 hours, compared to 2, 7, and 3 hours in rats, dogs, and monkeys, respectively. The absolute oral bioavailability, ranked from highest to lowest, was &gt;100% in rats, 51.4% in dogs, 46.8% in mice, and 30.3% in monkeys. Following repeat oral administration to mice and dogs, the systemic exposures generally increased in a less-than-dose proportional manner over a dose range of 30 to 750 mg/kg/day in mice and 30 to 500 mg/kg/day in dogs. Little to no drug accumulation occurred in mice or rats, but 2- to 13-fold drug accumulation was apparent in dogs. No consistent sex-related effects were apparent in any animal species tested. Plasma protein binding of umbralisib ranged from 99.7% to 99.8% in animals and humans. In mice, umbralisib was widely distributed, with the highest concentrations in the gastrointestinal, metabolic/excretory, fatty, secretory, and endocrine tissues and the lowest concentration in brain, demonstrating penetration of umbralisib occurred in richly perfused organs, while passage to the CNS was limited. There was no meaningful ocular distribution. Fecal elimination was the primary excretion pathway.
L1639: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1640: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's statement. See the review below for additional details regarding the PK studies.

L1645: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L1646: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L1647: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1648: Umbralisib was well tolerated after a single dose, with a maximum tolerated dose (MTD) of &lt;1000 mg/kg/day in mice and &gt;2000 mg/kg/day in rats. The tolerability, potential toxicities, target organs, and toxicokinetics of umbralisib were evaluated in repeat-dose toxicity studies up to 26 weeks in mice and 39 weeks in dogs. Umbralisib was generally well tolerated through acute, subchronic, and chronic dosing; test article-related mortality occurred at \ge 100 mg/kg/day in mice (equivalent to 300 mg/m^2 or 584 mg in a 70 kg human), and adverse clinical findings necessitating dose reductions occurred at \ge 10 mg/kg/day in dogs (equivalent to 200 mg/m^2 or 389 mg/kg in a 70 kg human) when administered chronically.
L1649: The liver was identified as the main target organ in mice and the gastrointestinal tract in dogs. Increased liver weights were observed in all studies in mice for the high-dose groups, but these findings did not correlate with increases in liver enzymes, and liver histopathology findings were minimal to mild. Another major finding was damage to the non-glandular stomach mucosa after subchronic and chronic administration, but the clinical relevance of these findings in humans cannot be determined, as humans do not have a non-glandular stomach. Other findings in mice included decreases in leukocytes and lymphocytes and increases in cholesterol that were considered minimal, non-adverse, or related to the pharmacologic action of umbralisib. All findings in mice were reversible upon treatment cessation. None of these findings were observed in dogs upon treatment with umbralisib. In dogs, diarrhea, body weight reductions, and decreased food consumption were noted in all studies and increased in severity and frequency in a dose-dependent manner. Dogs with body weight loss and decreased food consumption were offered canned food to increase appetite and food consumption. Dose holidays and dose reductions were necessitated during chronic administration due to low body condition scores. There were no gross pathology or histopathology findings that correlated to the clinical signs. Clinical pathology showed minor changes in metabolic parameters that were considered secondary to the clinical findings. There were no other adverse findings upon umbralisib treatment in dogs.
L1650: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1651: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment; see additional information provided below for the 26-week and 39-week repeat-dose toxicity studies in mice and dogs, respectively.
L1652: **Study title/Study number: A 26-week Study of TGR-1202 by Oral Gavage in Mice with a 28-day Recovery Period/20150163**
L1653: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L1654: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1655: - There were 20 unscheduled deaths, with all but 1 occurring in the umbralisib-dosed groups and half occurring in the high-dose group. While umbralisib induced mortality occurred in a dose-dependent manner, the deaths were due to undetermined causes.
- Principle hematology findings included reduced lymphocyte levels in mid-dose and high-dose male and female animals.
- Hepatotoxicity: Liver weights, ALP levels, and microscopic signals (minimal necrosis, inflammation, and hypertrophy) were increased in umbralisib-dosed animals.

L1660: ## **Observations and Results: changes from control**
L1661: Parameters	Major findings
Mortality	
There were 20 unscheduled deaths during the study.
Control: One control group female mouse was found dead on day 177.
LD: A total of 5 animals died or were euthanized with 3 of the 5 animals dying due to housing-related incidents unrelated to umbralisib. The other 2 were females euthanized on day 72. These animals exhibited ulcerative dermatitis, which was determined as the cause of death.
MD: A total of 4 animals died. Two of the 4 animals died due to housing-related incidents unrelated to umbralisib. The other 2 were females found dead on days 50 and 164. These animals had no macroscopic findings and microscopic findings, including inflammatory signals in the liver and lymph nodes, were not considered causal to their deaths by the Applicant's pathologist.
HD: 10 animals died prematurely. One animal died due to housing-related incidents unrelated to umbralisib. Two animals, a male and a

L1667: ## **Study title/Study number: A 39-week Study of TGR-1202 by Oral Capsule in Dogs with a 28-day Recovery Period/20150164**
L1668: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1669: - Due to weight loss and thin appearance, most notably in the mid- and high-dose animals, all umbralisib-dosed groups had dose modifications. High-dose animals received 2 dose modifications, a dosing holiday, and early euthanization on day 183 (week 26).
- Hematology signals included reduced RBC, hemoglobin, hematocrit, and reticulocytes in all umbralisib-dosed conditions.
- Microscopic findings included decreased cellularity in the lymph nodes and thymus.
L1670: Conducting laboratory and location: (b) (4)
L1671: GLP compliance: Yes
L1672: ## **Methods**
L1673: Dose and frequency of dosing:	0 (control), 10, 30, or 100 mg/kg/day (umbralisib batch # TGR1202/II/A422/10). Administered once daily. LD, MD, and HD groups were reduced to 8, 20, and 50 mg/kg/day, respectively, on day 127 (week 18). HD recovery group dogs were dosed with 20 mg/kg/day from day 183 (week 26) to day 273 (week 39).

L1680: ## Additional toxicology studies
L1681: Below is a summary of the 13 week toxicology study in dogs, titled "TGR-1202: 13-Week Oral Toxicity Study in Dogs with a 30-Day Dose-Free Recovery" (Study 034717):
L1682: Beagle dogs (3/sex/group) were administered umbralisib orally at doses of 0, 30, 100, or 250 mg/kg/day for 13 weeks. There were no test article-related mortalities. Treatment with umbralisib resulted in:
L1683: - GI toxicities as presented with soft, mucoid, and/or liquid feces, and emesis.
- Hematological changes included reductions in RBC parameters (hemoglobin, hematocrit, and mean corpuscular hemoglobin concentration).
- Notable but reversible changes in clinical chemistry parameters were most evident in the HD group, with reductions in total protein, albumin, globins, electrolytes, cholesterol, and triglycerides. These changes may be the secondary effect of the clinical changes (weight loss, emesis, reduced food intake).
- Lower thymus and spleen weights with microscopic observations of thymic atrophy and lymphoid depletion of lymph nodes
- Lowered weights in the testes and epididymides. MD and HD males displayed testicular changes that included degeneration, multinucleated giant cells, vacuolation, and sloughed cells in the tubular lumen within the seminiferous epithelium.
L1684: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L1685: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1686: Umbralisib was negative for mutagenic potential in an in vitro Ames assay at concentrations up to 5000 µg/plate and in an in vitro micronucleus assay. In vivo mutagenicity was also assessed in vivo after dosing CD-1 mice with 2 oral gavage doses of umbralisib up to 1000 mg/kg administered once daily. No increases in the scored micronuclei of reticulocytes in treated mice compared to control mice were detected under the study conditions.
L1687: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1688: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Additional details regarding the studies are provided in the review below.
L1689: ## **In Vitro Reverse Mutation Assay in Bacterial Cells (Ames)**
L1690: **Study title/ number:** Bacterial Reverse Mutation Assay/ AF33EJ.502ICH.(b) (4)
L1691: ## **Key Study Finding:**
L1692: - Umbralisib did not increase the number of revertant colonies in tester strains with or without metabolic activation.
- Precipitate was observed at ≥1000 µg per plate and toxicity resulting in a reduction in revertant count was observed at 5000 µg per plate.
L1693: GLP compliance: Yes.
L1694: Test system: *Salmonella typhimurium* strains TA98, TA100, TA1535, and TA1537, and *Escherichia Coli* strain WP2 *uvrA* in the presence or absence of rat liver S9 extract (at concentrations up to 5000 µg/plate).
Study is valid: Yes.

AF33EJ.361ICH.(b) (4)
L1697: ## **Key Study Finding:**
L1698: - Umbralisib did not induce chromosome aberrations in TK6 cells with or without metabolic activation.
L1699: GLP compliance: Yes.

Study is valid: Yes.
L1700: ## In Vivo Clastogenicity Assay in Rodent (Micronucleus Assay)

L1702: ## **Key Study Finding:**
L1703: - Umbralisib did not increase the frequency of micronucleated polychromatic erythrocytes in mice after oral administration.
L1704: GLP compliance: Yes.
