L1705: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L1706: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1707: In accordance with the International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) guideline ICH S9 (2009),
L1708: 59
L1709: *Version date: July 24, 2019 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)*
L1710: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L1711: no carcinogenicity studies have been conducted because umbralisib is intended for use in patients with advanced cancer.
L1712: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1713: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L1714: ## **5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology**
L1715: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1716: The potential for umbralisib exposure to impact male and female fertility parameters and pregnancy parameters was evaluated in mice at doses up to 300 mg/kg/day. In male mice, umbralisib administration at 300 mg/kg/day (equivalent to 900 mg/m2 or 1707 mg in a 70-kg human) for 4 weeks resulted in reduced testis weights accompanied by reduced sperm count and motility. The magnitude of these changes was small and did not impact overall male reproductive health.
L1717: The potential for umbralisib exposure to impact maternal health, pregnancy parameters, and embryonic development was evaluated in embryo-fetal development studies in mice and rabbits. In mice, doses \ge200 mg/kg (equivalent to \ge600 mg/m2 or \ge1138 mg in a 70 kg human) administered during the period of organogenesis resulted in no effects on maternal health but impacted pregnancy parameters (resorptions and post-implantation losses) and the developing fetus (increased incidence of cleft palate, nasopharyngeal fistula, and minor skeletal abnormalities). Treatment-related findings included an increase in the number of litters that exhibited 100% resorptions and post implantation resorption; and external (cleft palate), visceral (nasopharyngeal fistula), and skeletal fetal abnormalities. In rabbits, doses of 300 mg/kg (equivalent to 3600 mg/m2 or 6774 mg in a 70 kg human) administered during the period of organogenesis resulted in maternal toxicity of reduced body weights and food consumption with no other reported findings on pregnancy and fetal development in rabbits.
L1718: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1719: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The FDA's review of the fertility and early embryonic development study in mice and embryo-fetal development studies in mice and rabbits is provided below.
L1720: ## **Fertility and Early Embryonic Development**
L1721: ## **Study title/ number: TGR-1202: Study of Fertility and Early Embryonic Development in the CD-1 Mouse by Oral Gavage Administration/YC15PK**
L1722: - There were notable reductions in testis and epididymis weights in all umbralisib-dosed groups, reduced sperm counts in the 150 and 300 mg/kg/day groups, and reduced
L1723: sperm motility in the 300 mg/kg/day group. These reductions were within historic ranges.
L1724: - Increased resorption and post-implantation loss were observed in the mid-dose group.
- While the fertility index is comparable to, or is higher than, the values in the concurrent control group, the low number of pregnancies in the concurrent control group makes it difficult to make a conclusion on the fertility index for umbralisib-treated animals
- The doses of 50, 150, and 300 mg/kg/day doses resulted in exposures that were 0.7, 1.3, and 1.9 times the clinical exposure at the recommended dose of 800 mg/day.
L1725: (b) (4)
L1726: Conducting laboratory and location:
L1727: GLP compliance: Yes.
L1728: ## Methods
L1729: Dose and frequency of dosing: 0, 150, 300 mg/kg/day
Males: From 4 weeks (28 days) prior to mating, through mating until sacrifice.
Females: From 2 weeks (14 days) prior to mating through gestation day (GD) 7. Sacrificed on GD 13.

Route of administration: Oral gavage.
Formulation/Vehicle: 10 % (v/v) polysorbate 80 in 0.5 % (w/v) methylcellulose (400 cp), in deionized water

Species/Strain: Mice/CD-1
Number/Sex/Group: 22
Satellite groups: 4 spare animals/sex
Study design: Mice were paired 1:1 for 14 days or until evidence of mating (presence of a copulatory plug or sperm in the vaginal smear).
L1730: Deviation from study protocol affecting interpretation of results: No
L1731: ## **Observations and Results**
L1732: Parameters	Major findings
Mortality	Two males, both from the 300 mg/kg/day group, died prior to the scheduled euthanasia day. One mouse was found dead on dosing day 62. This animal had no apparent clinical or macroscopic findings. Another mouse was moribund and euthanized on dosing day 33. This animal had decreased activity, was cold to the touch, had irregular breathing, a distended abdomen, and partially closed eyelids. The animal's body weight had decreased and there were macroscopic findings observed in the GI tract that included distention, gas, and a yellow fluid. The causes of death were undetermined in these animals.
Clinical signs	Unremarkable

L1767: ## **Study title/ number: An Oral (Gavage) Embryo-Fetal Development Study in Rabbits/TGR-1202**
L1768: ## **Key Study Findings**
L1769: - Umbralisib administered at the highest dose of 300 mg/kg/day resulted in maternal toxicity characterized by a 50% reduction in food intake and an 8% reduction in body weight by the end of the dosing period. Both findings reversed by the end of the dosing-free period prior to euthanasia.
- Fetal weights at the high-dose were significantly lower than controls.
- Increased fetal skeletal abnormalities compared to the concurrent controls were noted principally in the 100 mg/kg/day (0.08 time the patient exposure) and 300 mg/kg/day dosing groups. These findings were considered within the common range for the strain of rabbits.

L1789: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L1790: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L1791: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1792: Umbralisib is a small molecule inhibitor of phosphoinositide-3-kinase delta (PI3K\delta) and casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1\epsilon). The Applicant is seeking approval of umbralisib for the treatment of patients with follicular lymphoma (FL) who have received at least (b)(4) prior systemic therapies. The proposed dosing regimen is 800 mg QD taken orally with food.
L1793: The Clinical Pharmacology section of the NDA is supported by single and multiple dose pharmacokinetics (PK) characterization, population PK and exposure-response analyses, QT assessment, human mass balance, relative bioavailability, and in vivo food effect studies, in vitro metabolic and transport studies, and physiologically-based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) modeling.
L1794: The clinical pharmacology review focused on the appropriateness of the proposed dosing regimen and dose recommendations for patients with hepatic impairment, and drug-drug interactions.
L1795: ## Recommendations:
L1796: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information submitted in NDA213176 (b)(4). This NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized below in the table below.
L1797: **Table 7: Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues by FDA**
L1798: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and Supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from pivotal study UTX-TGR-205. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by the overall efficacy of umbralisib with an objective response rate (ORR) of 42.7% (95% CI: 33.6, 52.2) in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma.
General dosing instructions	The proposed umbralisib dosing regimen of 800 mg given orally with food is acceptable for approval from a clinical pharmacology perspective:Favorable anti-tumor activity: This dosing regimen demonstrated an ORR of 42.7% in patients with FL. Across the dose range of 400 to 1200 mg QD, \ge 800 mg QD dosage appeared to show > 50% ORR and increased in percent change from baseline (%CfB) of target lesion dimensions in more than 50% of the FL
L1799: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
population, whereas, at lower dosage (<800 mg), there may be reduced activity.
Positive exposure-response relationships for safety: Higher exposure resulted in higher incidence of adverse events, particularly for diarrhea and elevated ALT and AST levels.

Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	No dose adjustment is recommended for patients with mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le upper limit of normal [ULN] with AST > ULN or total bilirubin >1 to 1.5 \times ULN with any AST). There is no data available for patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment (>1.5 \times ULN and any AST).
No dose adjustment is recommended for patients with mild or moderate renal impairment (CLcr 30-89 mL/min). There is no data available for patients with severe renal impairment (CLcr 15-29 mL/min).
Based on PopPK analyses, no dose adjustment is required based on age (18 to 87 years old), sex, race (White and Black), and body weight (44 to 165 kg).

Drug-drug interactions	No clinically significant differences in umbralisib pharmacokinetics were observed when used concomitantly with omeprazole, a proton pump inhibitor.
The available data do not support the Applicant's claim that co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact the PK of P-gp sensitive substrates.
The available data do not support the Applicant's claim that co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact the PK of cytochrome (CYP) sensitive substrates.
The available data do not support the Applicant's claim that co-administration of strong CYP3A inhibitors is not expected to significantly impact the PK of umbralisib.
There is no data to evaluate the effect of CYP2C9 modulators on the PK of umbralisib.

Labeling	
Overall, the proposed labeling recommendations are acceptable contingent upon the Applicant's agreement with the FDA revisions to the label. Clinical pharmacology labeling recommendations are detailed in Section 2.1, 8.6, 8.7, 12.2, and 12.3.
L1800: ## ***Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments***
L1801: PMR or
PMC	Key Issue(s)
to be
Addressed	Rationale	Key Considerations for
Design Features
PMR	Umbralisib dose in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment	Hepatic metabolism plays a major role in the elimination of umbralisib. The recommended dose of umbralisib in patients with moderate and severe hepatic impairment (based on NCI-ODWG criteria) has not been established. The clinical study will determine whether dose adjustment is needed in this specific population.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to determine an appropriate safe dose of umbralisib in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment (based on NCI criteria).
PMR	The effect of strong CYP3A and moderate CYP2C9 inhibitors on the exposure of umbralisib	CYP3A and CYP2C9 plays a role in the elimination of umbralisib. The recommended dose of umbralisib when used concomitantly with a strong CYP3A or moderate CYP2C9 inhibitor has not been established. The clinical study will determine whether dose adjustment is needed in these patients.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of CYP3A and CYP2C9 inhibitors on the single dose pharmacokinetics of umbralisib to determine appropriate dosing recommendations.
PMR	The effect of umbralisib on the exposure of CYP and P-gp substrates	In vitro, umbralisib has the potential to inhibit CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4 and P-gp. However, repeated dosing of umbralisib on the PK of CYP and P-gp sensitive substrates in clinical trials is not fully characterized. The clinical study will evaluate the effect of repeated dosing of umbralisib on these CYP and P-gp sensitive substrates and address the potential for excessive drug toxicity.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of repeat doses of umbralisib on the single dose pharmacokinetics of sensitive CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A and P-gp substrates to address the potential for excessive drug toxicity.

PMC	Key Issue(s)
to be
Addressed	Rationale	Key Considerations for

Additional ECG data are needed to assess the risk of QTc prolongation with umbralisib. L1803: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L1804: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L1805: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1806: The clinical pharmacology of umbralisib has been well characterized. The data in the application to support the use of umbralisib 800 mg once daily (QD) in subjects with FL comprise in vitro experiments using human biomaterials; clinical pharmacology studies with umbralisib in healthy subjects and subjects with cancer including single and repeat-dosing pharmacokinetic (PK) studies, biopharmaceutic studies (food effect), intrinsic factors (sex), extrinsic factors
L1807: (drug-drug interactions [DDIs]); and population PK analyses.
L1808: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1809: The FDA does not agree that the clinical pharmacology of umbralisib has been well characterized. The PK of umbralisib in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment, potential drug interactions of umbralisib as a perpetrator of CYP enzymes (CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4) and P-gp and a victim (CYP3A and CYP2C9 inhibitors and inducers), and the effect of umbralisib on the QT interval are not well characterized. Additional studies are needed (see Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments, Section 13).
L1810: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L1811: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L1812: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1813: During the course of the development of umbralisib, absorption, distribution, metabolism, excretion, and DDI properties were comprehensively evaluated. The appropriateness of umbralisib for the FL indication was evaluated in the exposure-response (E-R) analyses for efficacy and safety across five dose levels: 400, 600, 800, 1000, and 1200 mg. The pooled population pharmacokinetics (PPK)/E-R analyses robustly evaluated biologically inter-dependent endpoints of efficacy and safety. Overall, the dose-response results demonstrate an acceptable balance between benefits and risk at the recommended dose of 800 mg and justify the use of this dose regimen in subjects with FL.
L1814: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1815: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the recommended dosing regimen of 800 mg QD with food for patients with FL is reasonable. The Applicant compared the key efficacy and safety endpoints for a wide dose range of 400 to 1200 mg QD and identified that (1) for the 800 mg QD dose regimen, \ge 50\% of the FL population showed an ORR greater than 50% and an increased in %CfB of target lesion dimensions; (2) &lt; 800 mg, there may be reduced activity in the FL population. At doses &gt; 800 mg, there may be an increased risk of any grade and Grade \ge 3 elevated ALT/AST. After evaluating the benefit and risks based on the dose/exposure-response relationships for efficacy and safety, the proposed 800 mg QD for the treatment of FL populations is reasonable.
L1816: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L1817: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1818: No therapeutic individualization is needed in the proposed indication based on demographic factors (age, sex, race, body weight), DDIs (use of acid-reducing agent [ARA], concomitant use of strong 3A4 inhibitor, P-glycoprotein [P-gp] substrates), or in special populations of mild hepatic impairment or mild and moderate renal impairment. The impact of umbralisib on moderate and severe hepatic impairment and severe renal impairment have not been investigated.
L1819: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1820: Based on the population PK (PPK) analysis, FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is needed for demographic factors (age, sex, race and body weight) and patients with mild to moderate renal impairment. However, the FDA does not agree with the Applicant's position regarding the dose recommendations for concomitant use of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors based on the PPK analysis.
L1821: In the Applicant's evaluation of the impact of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, the frequency and dosing time of the co-administered strong CYP3A4 inhibitors were not available. In addition, in a pooled PK analysis (Study TGR-1202-101 and Study UTX-TGR-205) of 10 patients who were co-administered strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, only 4 patients had intense PK data available, while 6 patients had sparse PK of 1-3 PK samples. Based on the limited PK data and information on the administration time of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, the Applicant's current conclusion that no dose adjustment would be needed for concomitant administration of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors is not acceptable.
L1822: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is needed for patients with mild hepatic impairment. In the Applicant's PPK evaluation of the impact of mild hepatic impairment, PK data were only available for 6 patients with mild hepatic impairment (classified by NCI-ODWG criteria). Out of the 6 patients, 4 patients had only one PK datapoint. The available data were insufficient to use the PPK approach to assess the effect of mild hepatic impairment on the PK of umbralisib; however, the observed dose-normalized umbralisib concentrations from patients with mild hepatic impairment were within the range of those from patients with normal hepatic function (Figure 1), supporting no dose adjustment in patients with mild hepatic impairment. There are no data for patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment.
L1823: **Figure 1: Comparison of umbralisib observed concentrations between patients with normal hepatic function and mild hepatic impairment**

L1825: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L1826: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1827: None.
L1828: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1829: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that there are no outstanding issues. The PK characterization of umbralisib in patients with hepatic impairment, potential drug interactions of umbralisib as a perpetrator of CYP enzymes (CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4) and P-gp and a victim (CYP3A and CYP2C9 inhibitors and inducers), and the effect of umbralisib on the QT interval are not well characterized. Refer to the Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments, Section 13.
L1830: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L1831: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L1832: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1833: Based on comprehensive clinical pharmacology evaluations of umbralisib, the following characteristics of umbralisib are observed.
L1834: ## **Absorption and Pharmacokinetics:**
L1835: - From Study TGR-1202-101, the observed median T_{max} at 800 mg was 2.1 and 3.9 hours on Days 1 and 29 of dosing, respectively.
- Linear PK was observed over a dose range of 200 to 1000 mg by non compartmental analysis and 200 to 1800 mg by PPK analysis, although the number of subjects at 1800 mg was limited (n=2)
- Following repeat dosing at 800 mg, the observed accumulation ratio for area under the plasma/serum concentration-time curve (AUC) and maximum concentration (C_{max}) were 5.2- and 2.8-fold, respectively; the PPK model-estimated median steady state accumulation ratio for AUC was 6.4-fold (95% confidence interval [CI]: 3.7, 10.9). Ninety percent of the AUC at steady state was achieved after 35.5 doses (95% CI: 20, 70) of 800 mg QD.
- The PPK model-estimated relative oral bioavailability of drug product (DP) containing [REDACTED](b) (4) drug substance (DS) was 193% compared to DP containing [REDACTED](b) (4) DS.
L1836: ## Distribution:
L1837: - The geometric mean (percentage coefficient of variation [CV%]) apparent volume of distribution at steady state in healthy subjects was 1900 L/h (38%). This value is about 30 times greater than the volume of total body water in humans (Davies and Morris 1993), suggesting wide tissue distribution of umbralisib. This observation is expected for a Biopharmaceutics Classification System Class 2 compound, such as umbralisib.
- Umbralisib plasma protein binding was high in humans at concentrations of 2 (99.7%) and 5 (99.8%) \mu\text{mol/L}. The PPK model-estimated C_{max} at steady state and minimum plasma concentration at steady state (C_{min,ss}) were 11 and 8 \mu\text{mol/L} (6.3 and 4.7 \mu\text{g/mL}), respectively.
L1838: ## Elimination:
L1839: ## Metabolism:
L1840: - In plasma, umbralisib was minimally metabolized. In a radiolabeled mass balance study, unchanged umbralisib was the predominant radioactive component found in plasma (79.2% of total radioactivity area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero to 48 hours post-dose [AUC_{0-48}]) with 2 minor metabolites identified. Each plasma metabolite represented &lt;5% of total radioactive AUC_{0-48}.
- The proposed metabolites were formed by O-de-alkylation, mono-oxidation, methylation, oxidative defluorination, hydrogenation, glucuronidation, or
L1841: combinations thereof.
L1842: ## *Excretion:*
L1843: - Hepatobiliary elimination was the major excretion pathway for umbralisib, with fecal excretion accounting for 81% and urine excretion accounting for 3% of the total radioactive dose administered orally.
- In Study TGR-1202-904, the geometric mean (CV%) umbralisib apparent clearance was 8.72 (31%) L, with an apparent terminal phase half-life of 151 (33%) hours.
L1844: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1845: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment on general pharmacology and pharmacokinetic characteristics of umbralisib.
L1846: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that umbralisib is minimally metabolized. Based on data from the human mass balance study (TGR-1202-902), the majority of radioactivities in feces and urine were observed to be metabolites. Thus, umbralisib is mainly eliminated through metabolic pathways.
L1847: Differences in PK exposure was observed between healthy subjects and patients. Based on the PPK analysis, healthy subjects (n = 24) appeared to have 30% lower relative bioavailability (F) in Study 1102 compared to subjects with hematological malignancies in Study 101 (n=83). Central volume of distribution (Vc/F) in healthy subjects (n=24) appeared to be 79.5% lower than those in patients (n = 223) with hematological malignancies. The impact of food effect and formulations (b)(4) on relative bioavailability was taken into account in the PPK analysis and could not fully explain the exposure difference observed between Study 101 and Study 205. The rationale for the difference is currently unknown.
L1848: The general overview of umbralisib ADME and clinical PK information as assessed by FDA are presented in the table below.
L1849: **Table 8: Highlights of Clinical Pharmacology for umbralisib**
L1850: Physiochemical properties
L1851: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L1852: Mechanism of Action	Umbralisib is a small molecule inhibitor of phosphoinositide-3-kinase delta (PI3Kδ) and casein kinase 1 epsilon (CK1ε). The mechanism of action (MOA) involves competitive binding of umbralisib to PI3Kδ and CK1ε, inhibiting cell proliferation and inducing apoptosis of lymphoma and leukemic cell lines and primary tumor cells.
Active Moieties	Umbralisib was observed to be the major active species in plasma (79.2%).
QT/QTc Prolongation	Umbralisib exhibits no large mean increases in the QTc interval (i.e., >20 msec). However, the effect of umbralisib on the QT interval is not fully characterized as umbralisib exposure observed in TGR-1202-102 for QTc assessment is 37% lower than that reported by the PPK model. The effect of food and formulation differences in clinical trials and bioanalytical assays were not considered factors for this PK differences.
General Information
Bioanalysis	
Two validated PK assays were used to measure umbralisib concentrations in human plasma (MET126) and serum (MET193). A cross-validation study of plasma and serum (MET126 Addendum 1) indicated 12.5% bias between the methods, which is not a significant factor. The majority of PK data were obtained from plasma PK samples (91.6%).
A summary of the method validation reports is included in Appendix 19.4.1.

Healthy Volunteers vs. Patients	Based on population PK analysis, F and Vc/F in healthy subjects were 30% and 79.5% lower when compared to those in patients with hematological malignancies. The reason for this difference is currently unknown.
Drug exposure at steady state following the therapeutic dosing regimen	In adult patients with hematological malignancies who received umbralisib 800 mg QD with food, geometric steady-state (CV%) C_{max} and AUC_{tau} of umbralisib were 7.3 (39%) µg/mL and 141 (46%) µg*h/mL, respectively, based on the PPK model. Noncompartmental pooled PK analysis was not conducted as there were a limited number of patients (n=3) with intense PK sampling available in Phase 1 portion of Study TGR-1202-101.
Minimal effective dose or exposure	Not determined.
L1853: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L1854: Maximal tolerated dose or exposure	In Study TGR-1202-101, the MTD of umbralisib as a single-agent treatment in patients with hematological malignancies was not reached at doses up to 1800 mg due to PK saturation in absorption.
Dose Proportionality	Umbralisib (b)(4) appears to show dose-proportionality for both C_{max} and AUC in the dose range of 200 to 1000 mg following a single dose and at steady state.
Accumulation	The median accumulation ratios (R_{acc}) following umbralisib 800 mg QD with food was approximately 6.4-fold for AUC_{tau} and 3.8-fold for C_{max} based on PPK analysis.
Variability	Based on PPK analysis, the inter-subject variability (CV%) of apparent clearance (CL/F) and central volume of distribution (V_c/F) were 51.9% and 185%, respectively.
Absorption	
Bioavailability	Absolute bioavailability study was not conducted.
T_{max}	The median time to reach peak plasma concentration (T_{max}) of umbralisib following 800 mg QD with food was about 3.9 hours.
L1855: Relative Bioavailability(b) (4)	
Study TGR-1202-PK102 was an open-label, randomized, 2-way crossover study in 26 healthy subjects to evaluate the PK of umbralalisib with [REDACTED](b) (4) tablets. The results showed that the exposure of umbralalisib was increased 60% and 2.2-fold for AUC and C_{max}, respectively, following a single dose of 200 mg [REDACTED](b) (4) tablet and a single dose of 200 mg [REDACTED](b) (4) tablet in healthy subjects under fasting condition (Table 10).

L1856: Proton Pump Inhibitor (PPI) effect (omeprazole)	
Study TGR-1202-904 was an open-label, single-dose, crossover study to determine whether repeated doses of omeprazole (40 mg QD for 4 consecutive days), a proton pump inhibitor (PPI), has an effect on the PK of umbralisib in 18 healthy subjects who received a single dose of 800 mg (b)(4) tablet under fed conditions. Results showed that there was no clinically meaningful PK changes on the exposure of umbralisib, following a single dose of 800 mg (b)(4) tablet in healthy subjects with food (Table 13).

Distribution	
Volume of distribution	
Based on the PPK analysis, the geometric mean apparent volume of distribution of the central compartment (Vc/F) of umbralisib was estimated to be 312 L in patients with hematological malignancies.

Plasma protein binding	
In vitro, protein binding of umbralisib was 99.7-99.8%.

Blood to plasma ratio	
The mean blood-to-plasma ratio was 0.61-0.64 for umbralisib.

Elimination	
Half-life	
Following a single 800 mg dose in healthy subjects, the mean apparent elimination half-life (t1/2) of umbralisib was approximately 110 hours based on noncompartmental analysis.
In patients with hematological malignancies who received 800 mg QD, the geometric mean effective t1/2 of umbralisib was approximately 91 hours (42% CV) based on PPK analysis.
L1857: Clearance	Based on the PPK analysis, the geometric mean apparent clearance of umbralisib was estimated to be 15.5 L/h in patients with hematological malignancies.
Metabolism
Primary metabolic pathway(s)	In vitro, umbralisib appears to be metabolized by CYP3A4 and CYP2C9 and to a lesser extent CYP1A2. The metabolic assay [low incubation time (1 hr) of umbralisib with human recombinant P450 enzymes] was not adequate to determine the exact enzyme contribution due to low hepatic extraction of umbralisib.
Inhibitor/Inducer	In vitro,Umbralisib inhibits CYP2C8 (IC_{50} value of 10.6 \mumol/L), CYP2C9 (IC_{50} value of 3.4 \mumol/L), CYP2C19 (IC_{50} value of 13.5 \mumol/L), and CYP3A4 (IC_{50} value of 3.9 to 4.6 \mumol/L), but does not inhibit CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP2D6.
Umbralisib induces CYP3A4.
Umbralisib is not a substrate to P-gp, BCRP, OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, MATE1, and MATE2-K.
Umbralisib inhibits P-gp (IC_{50} value of 0.0653 \mumol/L).

Excretion
Primary excretion pathways (% dose) \pmSD	In the human mass balance Study TGR-1202-902 with mean total radioactivity recovery of 57.0-86.8% over 600 hours period, the mean radioactivity recovery in feces and urine in 6 healthy subjects were 81.0% (16.9% unchanged umbralisib) and 3.2%, respectively.
L1858: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L1859: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L1860: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1861: Yes, at 800 mg QD, a 54% objective response rate (ORR) was observed among subjects with FL for whom PK data was available for E-R analysis, and 58% of subjects with FL achieved &gt;50% decrease in target lesion dimensions. The model-predicted ORR rate was 56.1 (90% CI: 46.2, 65.1). The model-predicted percent change from baseline (%CfB) (90% CI) in target lesion dimensions was -47.3% (-56.9%, -38.4%). A significant (p&lt;0.05) relationship was observed between exposure and ORR, and between exposure and best %CfB in target lesion dimensions in subjects with FL. There were no significant (p&gt;0.05) exposure-efficacy relationships for duration of response (DOR), PFS, or time to response (TTR) in subjects with FL. The PPK model-predicted C_{min,ss} (bound and unbound umbralisib) after 800 mg QD was at least 7-fold higher than the concentration achieving 90% of maximal inhibition of PI3K\delta inhibition for the FL population.
L1862: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1863: The FDA generally agrees with Applicant's position.
L1864: ## 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L1865: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1866: Yes, the proposed dose of 800 mg QD is effective and generally well tolerated with no dosing difference observed in PK for age, sex, race, body weight, use of ARA, concomitant use of strong 3A4 inhibitor, mild hepatic impairment, or mild and moderate renal impairment.
L1867: During umbralisib development, 800 mg under fed conditions was implemented as the clinical dose for Phase II and later studies. The appropriateness of umbralisib for the FL indication was evaluated in the PK-efficacy relationships across several dose/exposure levels, including the clinical dose of 800 mg. This assessment used the final population PK model for all efficacy and safety endpoints. The integrated view of absorption, distribution, metabolism, excretion, and DDI properties of umbralisib and the dose-exposure-response relationships showed consistent trends that support the proposed clinical dose of 800 mg for patients with FL.
L1868: E-R analysis indicated no positive E-R relationship between average steady-state concentration and adverse events of special interest (AESI), with the exception of diarrhea (any grade) and elevated alanine aminotransferase (ALT)/aspartate aminotransferase (AST) (any grade and Grade \ge 3). Exposure-corrected QT interval (QTc) analyses indicated no cardiac safety-related risk at the recommended umbralisib dose of 800 mg or at a supratherapeutic dose of 1200 mg. Other additional covariate analyses supported no dose adjustment for subjects with hepatic or
L1869: renal impairment.
L1870: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1871: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposed dosing regimen for the general patient population. However, the FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that there is no risk of QTc prolongation with umbralisib. The Applicant's exposure-corrected QT interval (QTc) analyses were conducted using steady state exposure (C_{max,ss}) data in Study TGR-1202-102. The geometric mean C_{max} on Cycle 2 Day 1 was 4012 ng/mL (n=20), which was lower than the therapeutic exposure (7266 ng/mL) obtained from PPK analysis. The 1200 mg QD dose group in Study TGR-1202-102 did not provide higher exposure (4172 ng/mL) as compared to the 800 mg QD dose group. Thus, the Applicant's QT assessment was not adequate to extrapolate the risk of QTc prolongation to the higher exposure range. The FDA agrees that there were no large mean increases (i.e., 20 msec) with umbralisib treatment.
L1872: ## **6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?**
L1873: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1874: No. Based on the assessment of intrinsic factors, no dose adjustment or change in regimen is required based on demographics or in special populations.
L1875: **Specific Populations:** No clinically significant differences in the PK of umbralisib were observed based on age (&lt;65 or ≥65 years old), sex, race (White or non-White), body weight (&lt;78.5 or ≥78.5 kg), or mild and moderate renal impairment (estimated glomerular filtration rate [eGFR] 60 to &lt;90 or 30 to &lt;60 mL/min, respectively, estimated by Modification of Diet in Renal Disease equation). The effect of severe renal impairment (eGFR &lt;30 mL/min) and dialysis on umbralisib PK is unknown.
L1876: **Hepatic Impairment:** Umbralisib is minimally metabolized in the liver. No significant difference in the PK of umbralisib was observed in patients with mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin &gt;1 to 1.5 × upper limit of normal and any AST/ALT, by National Cancer Institute - Organ Dysfunction Working Group criteria). The effect of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on umbralisib PK is unknown.
L1877: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1878: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is required based on age (18 to 87 years old), sex, race [83.8% (n=207) White and 5.7% (n=14) Black], body weight (44 to 165 kg) or mild to moderate renal impairment (CL_{cr} 30 to 89 mL/min, estimated by the Cockcroft-Gault equation). In the Applicant's PPK evaluation of the impact of mild hepatic impairment, only 6 patients with mild hepatic impairment were included. Of the 6 patients, 4 patients had only 1 available PK sample for analysis. The available data were insufficient to use the PPK approach to assess the effect of mild hepatic impairment on the PK of umbralisib; however, the observed
L1879: dose-normalized umbralisib concentrations from patients with mild hepatic impairment were within the range of those with normal hepatic function (Figure 1), supporting no adjustment in patients with mild hepatic impairment.
L1880: ## 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L1881: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1882: The clinically relevant food-drug interactions and DDIs with concomitant medications are described in further detail below with their respective recommendation for management.
L1883: **Food:** Umbralisib is recommended to be taken with food as administration of a single dose with a high-fat meal in healthy subjects increased AUC and C_{max} of umbralisib by 61% and 115%, respectively, relative to fasting condition.
L1884: ## **Effect of Other Drugs on Umbralisib:**
L1885: **ARA:** Repeat dosing of omeprazole, an ARA, had no apparent impact on the exposure of a single dose of umbralisib. The area under the plasma/serum concentration-time curve from time zero to the time of the last quantifiable concentration (AUC_{0-t}) and area under the plasma concentration-time curve from time zero to infinity (AUC_{0-inf}) were both increased by 20% and the C_{max} decreased by 8% in the coadministration group, relative to the umbralisib alone group.
L1886: **Cytochrome P450 (CYP)3A4 Inhibitors or Inducers:** PPK analysis revealed that concomitant use of CYP3A4 inhibitors with umbralisib did not impact the PK of umbralisib. Use of strong CYP3A4 inducers (n=9) on the PK of umbralisib is unknown.
L1887: **Transporter Systems:** Umbralisib was not a substrate of organic anion transporter (OAT)1, OAT3, organic cation transporter (OCT)2, organic anion transport protein (OATP)1B1, OATP1B3, multidrug and toxic compound extrusion transporter (MATE)1, MATE2 K, P-gp, and breast cancer resistance protein. Thus, no impact on the PK of umbralisib is expected with concomitant use of these transporter substrates.
L1888: ## **Effect of Umbralisib on Other Drugs:**
L1889: **P-gp Substrates:** Umbralisib increased the absorption rate of fexofenadine (a sensitive P-gp substrate) producing a 42% higher C_{max} and a 1 hour decrease of T_{max} in the coadministration group relative to the fexofenadine alone group. However, the overall extent of fexofenadine exposure was unaffected, with the AUC_{0-t} and AUC_{0-inf} increased by 9% to 10%, respectively, in the coadministration group relative to the fexofenadine alone group. Therefore, co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact the PK of P-gp substrates.
L1890: **CYP3A4 Substrates:** Simultaneous simulation using a physiologically-based PK (PBPK) model suggested umbralisib was a weak (\ge 1.25- to \le 2-fold changes in C_{max} and AUC ratios,
L1891: respectively) inhibitor. Specifically, multiple doses of umbralisib increased AUC and C_{max} of single dose midazolam 1.56- and 1.52-fold, respectively. These simulated exposure increases of midazolam suggested that umbralisib did not induce CYP3A4.
L1892: ***CYP2C8, CYP2C9, and CYP2C19 Substrates:*** Simulation using PBPK modeling suggested that multiple doses of umbralisib did not impact the exposures of single doses of repaglinide (CYP2C8 substrate), tolbutamide (CYP2C9 substrate), or S-mephenytoin (CYP2C19 substrate). Specifically, the predicted C_{max} and AUC ratios with and without simulated coadministration of the probe substrate for each CYP isozyme were &lt;1.25-fold under simulated steady state conditions of umbralisib.
L1893: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1894: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that umbralisib should be taken with food.
L1895: ## ***Effect of omeprazole on PK of umbralisib:***
L1896: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is required for the use of omeprazole.
L1897: ## ***Effect of umbralisib on PK of P-gp substrates:***
L1898: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact the PK of P-gp substrates. In Study TGR-1202-901, the study design is only sufficient to examine the effect of intestinal P-gp inhibition as only a single oral dose of umbralisib (800 mg with food) and fexofenadine (180 mg) were administered to healthy subjects. However, in vitro data suggest that umbralisib inhibits P-gp with an IC_{50} value of 0.064 \mumol/L. Umbralisib has a high accumulation ratio (6.4-fold), a long half-life (effective half-life of 91 hours), and a small fluctuation between C_{max} and C_{min} (0.008 to 0.011 \mumol/mL) at steady state. Under a worst case scenario when steady state of umbralisib is reached, there is a high potential that systemic inhibition of P-gp (liver and kidney) may affect the PK of P-gp sensitive substrates. Thus, the current data do not support the claim that umbralisib does not affect the PK of P-gp sensitive substrates.
L1899: ## ***Effect of strong CYP3A inhibitors on PK of umbralisib:***
L1900: The FDA disagrees with Applicant's position that co-administration of strong CYP3A inhibitors is not expected to significantly impact the PK of umbralisib. In the Applicant's evaluation of the impact of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, the frequency and dosing time of the co-administered strong CYP3A4 inhibitors were not available. In addition, of the 10 patients who were co-administered strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, only 4 patients had intense PK, while 6 patients only had sparse PK data of 1-3 PK samples. Based on the limited PK data and information on the administration time of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, the Applicant's current conclusion that no dose adjustment would be needed for concomitant administration of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors is not acceptable.
L1901: ## *Effect of umbralisib on PK of CYP substrates:*
L1902: The FDA disagrees with Applicant's position that co-administration of umbralisib is not expected to significantly impact CYP substrates. The effect of umbralisib on CYP3A, CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 substrates could not be confidently predicted using the PBPK modeling approach due to uncertainties about the in vitro to in vivo extrapolation. The potential of drug-drug interactions of umbralisib with substrates of these enzymes cannot be ruled out.

L1948: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L1949: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1950: The clinical review of efficacy was primarily based on an analysis of the single-arm cohort of study UTX-TGR-205, an open-label, multi-center, multi-cohort trial (NCT02793583) that included 117 patients with relapsed/refractory FL who were evaluable for efficacy.
L1951: The review of safety is primarily based on the pooled data from 371 patients with hematologic malignancies in three single-arm, open-label trials (Study TGR-1202-101, TGR-1202-202, and UTX-TGR-205) and one open-label extension trial (Study UTX-TGR-501). Of note, data from UTX-TGR-103, described in the table above, was not included in the NDA submission.
L1952: All major efficacy and safety analyses were reproduced or audited. Statistical analyses by the reviewers were performed using SAS/JMP 13.0 (SAS Institute, Inc., Cary, NC) and MedDRA-Based Adverse Event Diagnostics (MAED) 1.8 (Enterprise Performance and Lifecycle System Design).
L1953: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L1954: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L1955: The efficacy of umbralisib in subjects with FL was evaluated in a Phase I study, TGR-1202-101, and the Phase II single-arm study, UTX-TGR-205. The pivotal study to characterize the efficacy of umbralisib in FL is the ongoing Phase IIb Study UTX-TGR-205; efficacy data of subjects with FL are summarized as of the cutoff date of 30 November 2019. Efficacy data from Study TGR-1202-101 are used as supportive information.
L1956: ## 8.1.1. Study UTX-TGR-205
L1957: ## Trial Design
L1958: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1959: ## Basic Study Design
L1960: Study UTX-TGR-205 is an ongoing, Phase IIb, randomized study to evaluate the efficacy and safety of single agent umbralisib and umbralisib in combination with ublituximab with or without bendamustine in subjects with previously treated iNHL, mantle cell lymphoma (MCL), and diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL). Subjects were enrolled into 1 of 4 cohorts (marginal zone lymphoma [MZL], FL and small lymphocytic lymphoma [SLL], MCL, or DLBCL) based on tumor type.
L1961: ## Trial Location
L1962: This study is being conducted at 83 sites in the US and 38 sites outside of the US (10 sites in Poland, 6 sites in the United Kingdom, 5 sites each in Spain and South Korea, 4 sites each in Israel and Italy, 3 sites in Australia, and 1 site in Slovakia).
L1963: ## Choice of Control Group
L1964: On the basis of the disease types and settings being studied, an open-label Phase II design without an active controlled standard of care arm was selected.
L1965: ## Diagnostic Criteria
L1966: Subjects were enrolled with histologically confirmed diagnosis of B-cell NHL with histological subtype FL of Grade 1, 2, or 3a based on criteria established by the World Health Organization 2008 classification of tumors of hematopoietic and lymphoid
L1967: tissues.
L1968: ## Key Inclusion/Exclusion Criteria
L1969: Male or female subjects \ge 18 years of age with FL were required to be relapsed or refractory after \ge 2 prior lines of systemic therapy. Subjects must have received an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent and must have documented evidence for requiring treatment (1 or more of the following: bulky disease [&gt;5 cm], high lactate dehydrogenase, B symptoms, threatened organ function, splenomegaly, cytopenias due to lymphoma, or effusions).
L1970: Subjects with FL also had to have measurable disease, defined as at least 1 measurable disease lesion &gt;1.5 cm in at least 1 diameter by computed tomography (CT), positron emission tomography (PET)-CT, or magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) in an area of no prior radiation therapy, or in an area that was previously irradiated that has documented progression.
L1971: Subjects were excluded if they were receiving cancer therapy (i.e., chemotherapy, radiation therapy, immunotherapy, biologic therapy, hormonal therapy, surgery, and/or tumor embolization) or any investigational drug within 21 days of Day 1 of Cycle 1 or had prior exposure to drugs that specifically inhibit PI3K.
L1972: ## Dose Selection
L1973: Based on the results from a Phase I dose-escalation study of umbralisib (TGR-1202-101) and 2 Phase I PK studies in healthy volunteers (TGR-1202-PK101 and TGR 1202-PK102), a dose of 800 mg QD of umbralisib under fed condition was selected.
L1974: ## Study Treatments
L1975: Umbralisib 800 mg QD was taken orally with food during all cycles in the study. Umbralisib was to be continued daily until progressive disease (PD), unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal from the study for other reasons.
L1976: ## Assignment to Study Treatment
L1977: In Stage 1, eligible FL subjects received single agent umbralisib.
L1978: ## Blinding
L1979: This was an open-label study.
L1980: ## Dose Modification
L1981: For adverse events (AEs) suspected to be related to umbralisib, management guidance and dose delays and/or modifications were provided in the protocol. Supportive care was to be considered for any subject who experiences Grade \ge 2 cytopenias, or Grade \ge 1 non-hematologic toxicities. If a subject experienced a treatment-related AE requiring dose modification during the course of therapy, then study drug administration was to be held, as necessary, until the AE resolves or stabilizes to an acceptable degree.
L1982: ## Administrative Structure
L1983: The best clinical response as well as disease progression was determined by an Independent Central Radiology Group, also referred to as an Independent Review Committee (IRC).
L1984: An independent Data Safety Monitoring Board was established to advise the Sponsor on safety for subjects enrolled in the study. Given that this was an open-label, non-comparator Phase II trial, the Sponsor evaluated preliminary efficacy and safety data periodically to guide continued development of ublituximab and umbralisib.
L1985: ## Procedures and Schedule
L1986: During the study period, all subjects were evaluated for response by CT, PET-CT, and/or MRI at the end of Cycle 3 and then within 14 days prior to Day 1 of Cycles 6, 9, and 12. After Cycle 12, response assessments occurred every 6 cycles or more frequently. Subjects remained on study drug until the occurrence of definitive disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal from the study due to Investigator decision or other reasons. Subjects who discontinued from study drug for reasons other than disease progression were followed for progression.
L1987: ## Treatment Compliance
L1988: Study drug compliance was reviewed with the subject at the beginning of each new treatment cycle and as needed.
L1989: ## Subject Completion
L1990: Subjects continued on study treatment until the occurrence of definitive disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal from the study due to Investigator decision or other reasons. Subjects who discontinued from study treatment (either for toxicity or physician choice) and have not progressed were followed for progression.
L1991: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1992: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description.
L1993: ## Study Endpoints
L1994: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1995: The study endpoints ORR and PFS are all accepted and well-recognized for oncology trials. The primary efficacy outcome, ORR, was defined as the percentage of subjects who achieved complete response (CR) or partial response (PR), according to the Revised Criteria for Malignant Lymphoma (Cheson 2007). The primary analysis for ORR was based on the IRC assessments.
L1996: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1997: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position and has provided additional information below.
L1998: The determination of response and progression was based on the International Working Group (IWG) Revised Response Criteria for Malignant Lymphoma (Cheson et al., 2007) modified so that the six index lesions included both nodal and extranodal lesions as described in the Lugano classification (Cheson et al. 2014) rather than considering nodal and extranodal lesions separately. The best clinical response and disease progression were determined by the independent central radiology group (ICRG). The findings of the ICRG were considered primary for analyses of ORR, PFS, and other tumor control endpoints. Subjects who discontinued from study treatment (either for toxicity or physician choice) and had not progressed continued to be followed for progression.
L1999: In the absence of a control arm and due to the open-label single-arm design of the trial, it is difficult to interpret and is unlikely to provide evidence to support the time-to-event endpoints, PFS and OS. The major limitation is that the measurements in isolation are out of context and comparing them to historical controls is prone to bias. The durability of the ORR will be assessed using the duration of response.
L2000: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L2001: ## The Applicant's Description:
L2002: The Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP) was agreed upon and finalized prior to the conduct of any efficacy analysis.
L2003: ## Analysis Populations
L2004: The intent-to-treat (ITT) Population included all subjects who were enrolled in the FL cohort (Stage 1) and who received at least 1 dose of single agent umbralisib. The ITT
L2005: Population was used for the efficacy analyses of ORR, DOR, PFS, CR, and TTR. To be included in the ITT Population, an FL subject must have
L2006: - been enrolled in the FL/SLL cohort Stage 1 (last enrollment 16 October 2018) to receive single agent umbralisib, and indicated as FL, not SLL, at Screening
- received at least 1 dose of study drug
- had prior systemic therapy with an anti-CD20 antibody and an alkylating agent.
L2007: The Per Protocol (PP) Population included all ITT subjects without major protocol deviations related to efficacy parameters and who had at least 1 post-baseline response assessment.
L2008: ## Interim Analysis
L2009: This was an interim analysis based on Amendment 7, Version 5.1 of the study protocol as of the data cutoff date of 30 November 2019. Results from the following analyses were presented in the interim CSR:
L2010: - Efficacy assessments of single agent umbralisib in FL cohort (Stage 1)
- Safety assessments for single agent umbralisib and by disease type, if appropriate
L2011: ## Efficacy Analysis
L2012: The primary efficacy outcome was ORR defined as the percent of subjects who achieved CR or PR, according to the Revised Criteria for Malignant Lymphoma (Cheson 2007). The primary analysis for ORR was conducted in the ITT population based on the IRC assessments. The estimated response rate and the exact 2-sided 95% CI (Clopper-Pearson method) were presented. Waterfall plots of maximum percentage decrease in sum of the products of longest diameters (SPD) by IRC assessment was presented, also showing FL grades. Additionally, the Investigator assessments were summarized in supportive analyses, and agreement in best overall response between IRC and Investigator assessments was also summarized.
L2013: Subject follow-up time (months) was calculated as follows: (date of efficacy data cutoff - date of first dosing +1)/30.4375.
L2014: The DOR was defined as the interval from the first documentation of CR or PR to the earlier of the first documentation of definitive disease progression or death from any cause. The DOR was calculated based on subjects who achieved a response. For subjects who responded but never progressed in the study, the DOR was censored at the time of
L2015: last tumor assessment using the same censoring algorithm as for PFS. The DOR was summarized by Kaplan-Meier method.
L2016: The TTR was defined as the interval from enrollment to the first documentation of CR or PR. The Kaplan-Meier estimate for TTR was presented for the ITT population only in subjects who achieved CR or PR.
L2017: PFS was defined as the time from the date of enrollment until the date of first documentation of definitive disease progression or date of death from any cause, whichever occurred first. The median duration of PFS and the proportion of subjects who were progression-free at 6, 12, and 18 months were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method. For each estimate, a 95% CI was reported. Additional sensitivity analyses were also performed for PFS.
L2018: Efficacy endpoints were examined for subgroups of the ITT population. The same methodology as for the primary analysis of ORR, PFS, CR, and DOR was used. Forest plots were produced for ORR.
L2019: ## Safety Analysis
L2020: Study treatment exposure was summarized for the ITT and Safety populations. The number and percentage of subjects with treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), Grade \ge 3 TEAEs, drug-related (to umbralisib and to ublituximab) TEAEs, drug-related Grade \ge 3 TEAEs, serious TEAEs, Grade \ge 3 serious TEAEs, drug-related serious TEAEs, TEAEs resulting in discontinuation of study treatment (umbralisib or ublituximab), and TEAEs with fatal outcome were summarized by tumor type, system organ class (SOC), and preferred term(PT). The number and percentage of subjects with AESIs was also summarized by AESI category and PT. Laboratory data for hematology and serum chemistry tests, vital signs, and Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance status were also summarized with shift tables from baseline.
L2021: ## Planned Subgroup Analyses
L2022: Analyses using following subgroups were performed for efficacy endpoints ORR, CR, PFS, and DOR:
L2023: - Gender
- Age group (&lt;65, \ge 65 years)
- Race (Caucasian, Non-Caucasian)
- Geographical region (US, Non-US)
L2024: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L2025: - Baseline ECOG Performance status score (0, \ge 1)
- FL grade at Screening (1, 2, 3a)
- Relapse or refractory to last therapy (Relapse, Refractory)
- Bulky disease (&gt;5 cm)
- Splenomegaly
- Number of prior lines of therapy (&lt;3, \ge 3)
- Previously treated with lenalidomide (Yes, No)
- Previously treated with lenalidomide in combination with anti-CD20 antibody (Yes, No)
- Previously treated with lenalidomide in combination with rituximab (Yes, No)
L2026: ## Changes to the Planned Analyses
L2027: The following changes to the planned subgroup analyses were revised from Version 1.0 of the SAP:
L2028: - Number of prior lines of therapy (&lt;3, \ge 3)
L2029: Reason for change: The study inclusion criteria required at least 2 prior therapies for subjects with FL. The new category (&lt;3, \ge 3) was added; otherwise, the number of subjects in the &lt;2 subgroup would be too small for subgroup analysis.
L2030: - Previously treated with lenalidomide in combination with rituximab (Yes, No) was added as a new subgroup.
L2031: **The FDA's Assessment:** The FDA agrees, in general, with the Applicant's position and has the following comments.
L2032: According to the sample size calculation provided in the SAP, 100 patients in the FL cohort would provide a 90% power at the 5% level of significance to rule out 35% ORR under the null against the alternative hypothesis that ORR is 51.5%. Using a confidence interval of the ORR derived using the Clopper-Pearson exact method, an estimated ORR of 45% (i.e., 45 subjects responding of 100 FL subjects evaluated) would achieve a 1-sided 97.5% lower bound of 35.0%.
L2033: Although there were no planned interim analysis in the SAP, updated efficacy data was submitted on 9/15/2020 during the review of this application. The updated efficacy
L2034: results will be presented in the FDA's assessment section.
L2035: ## Protocol Amendments
L2036: ## The Applicant's Description:
L2037: The study protocol was amended 7 times during the study. None of the implemented changes impacted the integrity of the trial or the interpretation of the results. Important modifications/amendments to the study protocol included the following:
L2038: - Amendment 3 (Version 2.0, 12 April 2017 and Version 2.0 UK 12 April 2017):Live virus vaccines were prohibited within 4 weeks prior to enrollment, during therapy, or for 6 weeks post-treatment.
- Amendment 4 (Version 3.0, 01 October 2017):Inclusion criterion was modified to require subjects with FL or SLL to be relapsed or refractory after \ge 2 prior lines of therapy, and all subjects must have received an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent.
- Inclusion criterion was modified to require creatinine clearance \ge 30 mL/min for subjects with FL, SLL, or MZL and \ge 40 mL/min for subjects with DLBCL.
- Exclusion criterion was modified to allow use of concomitant medications known to cause QT prolongation or torsades de pointes with caution and at the Investigator's discretion.
- The FL/SLL cohort was modified to include a Stage 1 to evaluate single agent umbralisib in up to 150 subjects prior to proceeding to Stage 2, which randomized subjects to either umbralisib alone or in combination with ublituximab.
- More specific instructions were given around dose modifications in the event of diarrhea and/or colitis.
- Futility stopping rules were added for the MZL cohort and the FL/SLL cohort based on Simon's 2-stage design.
- Amendment 5 (Version 4.0, 15 February 2018):Mention of copanlisib was added as an excluded prior therapy.
- Amendment 6 (Version 5.0, 20 August 2018)The primary objectives of the study were revised to reflect ORR as the primary endpoint for all cohorts.
- The key secondary objectives of CR rate for subjects with FL or SLL and PFS for subjects with DLBCL were revised to be secondary objectives.
L2039: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralalisib)
L2040: - Inclusion criterion was amended to require subjects with FL, SLL, or MZL to have documented evidence for requiring treatment.
- Statistical assumptions for the MZL, FL/SLL, and DLBCL cohorts were revised in accordance with recent SAPs.
- Amendment 7 (Version 5.1, 20 September 2018)Updated to include cytomegalovirus surveillance for all subjects
- Revised to allow dose delays of any study medication for durations longer than 28 days to recover from toxicity
- Updated to provide clarification on the liver toxicity dose modification guidelines

L2042: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L2088: ## Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)
L2089: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2090: For the 117 FL subjects in the ITT Population, 30 subjects (25.6%) were Grade 1, 53 subjects (45.3%) were Grade 2, and 32 subjects (27.4%) were Grade3a (Table 16). Most of the subjects were Stage III (30.8% of subjects) or Stage IV (41.9% of subjects). Fourteen subjects (12.0%) previously received lenalidomide and anti-CD20 antibody, of whom 12 subjects (10.3%) received lenalidomide in combination with rituximab (R2).
L2091: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2092: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. In the ITT Population of 117 subjects with FL, 36% of the patients had disease refractory to last therapy and 31.6% had disease refractory to rituximab. More than half of the patients (57.3%) had received median of 3 or more prior lines of systemic therapies.
L2093: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L2094: ## Treatment Compliance
L2095: One subject with FL was discontinued due to non-compliance with study.
L2096: ## Concomitant Medications
L2097: All 131 subjects with FL received at least 1 concomitant medication. Concomitant medications were categorized according to their potential for inhibition/induction of P450 isozymes and inhibition of transporters. In the category of P450 inhibition, the most frequently used drugs (at least 10% of subjects overall) were acyclovir (73.3% of subjects), allopurinol (32.8% of subjects), and omeprazole (16.8% of subjects), all of which are classified as weak inhibitors based on FDA guidance (FDA: Drug development and drug interactions). Overall, P450 inducers were used infrequently (\le 1.1\%). The most frequently used clinical OATP1B1/OATP1B3 inhibitor was clarithromycin (2.3% of subjects), which is also an inhibitor of P-gp. Other frequently used clinical P gp inhibitors included carvedilol (4.6%).
L2098: ## Rescue Medication Use
L2099: Not applicable.
L2100: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2101: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

L2118: For the PP Population, the ORR was 43.4%, with CR in 3.5% of subjects and PR in 39.8% of subjects based on IRC assessment and 40.7%, with CR in 4.4% of subjects and PR in 36.3% of subjects based on Investigator assessment.
L2119: ## *Subgroup Analyses*
L2120: Subgroup analyses of ORR based on IRC assessments for the ITT Population are presented in Figure 3. With respect to FL grade at Screening, the ORR was highest in subjects with Grade 1 FL (50.0%), followed by Grade 2 FL (43.4%) and Grade 3A FL (34.4%). The ORR for subjects with &lt;3 prior lines of therapy was 38.8% compared to 45.6% for subjects with ≥3 prior lines. Among the 18 subjects previously treated with a lenalidomide-containing regimen, a 33.3% ORR was observed compared to 44.4% for subjects without prior lenalidomide therapy. Among 14 subjects with prior R2 treatment, the ORR was 42.9% compared to 42.7% among those without prior R2 treatment.
L2121: **Figure 3: Forest Plot of ORR for Subjects With FL Based on IRC Assessment - Study UTX-TGR-205 (ITT Population)**
L2122: Source: CSR UTX-TGR-205, Figure 14.2.5.1
L2123: Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval; CSR, clinical study report; ECOG, Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group; FL, follicular lymphoma; IRC, Independent Review Committee; ITT, intent-to treat; ORR, overall response rate; PS, performance status; US, United States.
L2124: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2125: The FDA agrees with Applicant's presentation of ORR results and the subgroup analyses results. Some of the subgroups had insufficient number of patients needed to draw meaningful conclusions, e.g., the previously treated with lenalidomide categories have insufficient number of patients in the "Yes" subgroups to draw reasonable conclusions. In addition, these analyses are exploratory with no multiplicity adjustment and no pre-specified hypotheses. The outlier subgroups with wide confidence intervals can be attributed to the small number of patients and responders in that subgroup.
L2126: The ORR results by ethnicity are as follows:
L2127: There were 2 responders out of 6 Hispanic or Latino patients, 42 responders out of 100 non-Hispanic or Latino patients and 6 responders in the 11 patients who had not reported or had missing ethnicity.
L2128: For race, some of the subgroups had insufficient numbers, limiting the conclusions in this study to a broad patient population.
L2129: Based on the efficacy update provided on 9/11/2020 using the data cutoff of 15 July 2020, the ORR changed from 42.7 % (95% CI: 33.6, 52.2) to 45.3% (95% CI: 36.1, 54.8) with an addition of two patients with CR and one patient with PR.

L2132: No data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections.

L2134: No issues were identified with the data quality from the pivotal study which would affect the efficacy results. The submitted datasets are generally consistent, and variables are clearly labeled and/or explained.
L2135: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L2136: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2137: ## *Duration of Response*
L2138: The median DOR for subjects with FL was 11.1 months (95% CI: 8.3, 16.4) based on IRC assessment and 15.7 months (95% CI: 7.6, 18.5) based on Investigator assessment (Table 18). Based on IRC assessment, the DOR rates at 6 and 12 months were 74.4% and 49.2%, respectively.

L2140: Subgroup	IRC Assessment
FL (N=50)	Investigator Assessment

L2156: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2157: The FDA agrees with the estimates provided for the duration of response.
L2158: Based on the efficacy update provided on 9/11/2020 using the data cutoff of 15 July 2020, the ORR changed from 42.7 % (95% CI: 33.6, 52.2) to 45.3% (95% CI: 36.1, 54.8) with the addition of two patients with CR and one patient with PR. The point estimates of the IRC assessed median duration of response remained the same at 11.1 (95%CI: 8.3, 15.6) months (the confidence interval changed).
L2159: As discussed in Section 8.1.1 under study endpoint, time-to event endpoints in single-arm studies have limited interpretability. Therefore, the PFS results are considered descriptive and exploratory.
L2160: ## Dose/Dose Response
L2161: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2162: PPK and E-R relationship after umbralisib administration was performed using data from Studies TGR-1202-101 and UTX-TGR-205. The exposure-efficacy analyses included 79 subjects with FL. The pooled PPK/E-R analyses robustly evaluated biologically inter-dependent endpoints of efficacy and safety. Overall, the dose response results demonstrate an acceptable balance between benefits and risk at the recommended dose of umbralisib 800 mg and justify the use of this dose regimen in subjects with FL.
L2163: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2164: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2165: ## Durability of Response
L2166: In the pivotal study (Study UTX-TGR-205), 35 of the 117 subjects with FL were exposed to umbralalisib for at least 12 months. No separate analyses were carried out on these subsets of subjects. Kaplan-Meier plots of DOR and PFS rates are used to provide estimated DOR and survival with continued umbralalisib treatment. For subjects with FL, the DOR rate at 12 months was 49.2% (Figure 4).

L2173: ## 8.1.3. Supportive Study for Efficacy - Study TGR-1202-101
L2174: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2175: Study TGR-1202-101 is a completed, Phase I, first-in-human, open-label study of umbralisib, administered as a single agent to subjects with relapsed or refractory hematologic malignancies. The study included eligible subjects aged 18 years or older with histologically confirmed Hodgkin lymphoma, B-cell NHL, peripheral T cell lymphoma, CLL, SLL, MZL, or other B-cell lymphoproliferative disorders whose disease had relapsed after, or was refractory to, at least 1 previous treatment regimen.
L2176: This study consisted of a dose-escalation phase and a dose-expansion phase. The dose-escalation phase used a traditional 3+3 design to determine the maximum tolerated dose (MTD). Subjects were to receive umbralisib daily in the absence of disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal from treatment. In the initial dose-escalation phase, subjects received [REDACTED] (b) (4) umbralisib under a fasted condition starting at 50 mg orally QD with increasing doses for each subsequent cohort until the MTD was identified. For the majority of the study, subjects received [REDACTED] (b) (4) umbralisib, which was subsequently used in Study UTX-TGR-205. Each treatment cycle was 4 weeks (28 days), with the first efficacy evaluation at 8 weeks (\pm 7 days) and at 8-
L2177: week intervals thereafter. After Cycle 12, restaging was performed every 6 months at the discretion of the treating physician on a subject-by-subject basis secondary to the clinical presentation of the subject. Disease assessments were conducted by treating investigators. Subjects with PD or unacceptable toxicity were discontinued, and subjects achieving stable disease or better were allowed to continue daily treatment with umbralisib. Subjects on umbralisib who received benefit at the conclusion of this study were eligible to enroll in an open-label extension study, Study UTX TGR-501, to continue to receive single agent umbralisib.
L2178: The primary objective of this study was to evaluate safety and MTD of umbralisib in subjects with hematologic malignancies and to evaluate the PK effects of umbralisib. The secondary objectives of the study included evaluation of ORR and DOR in subjects with hematologic malignancies treated with umbralisib.
L2179: Efficacy analyses were based on the ITT and modified intent-to-treat (mITT) Populations. The ITT Population included all subjects who received at least 1 dose of umbralisib and who had a baseline assessment and at least 1 post-baseline efficacy assessment. The mITT Population included all subjects in the ITT Population treated with \ge 800 mg dose of the (b) (4) DP of umbralisib or any dose of the (b) (4) DP of umbralisib.
L2180: Of the 90 subjects enrolled in this study, 19 subjects had FL. Treatment was discontinued for all subjects, and the most common reason for discontinuation was disease progression (12 subjects [63.2%]). The median age was 65.0 years (range: 43 to 76 years). The mean duration of exposure to umbralisib was 14.1 months (range: 0.1 to 55.4 months). Six of the 19 subjects were exposed for \ge 12 months.
L2181: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2182: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2183: ## 8.1.4. Efficacy Results - Study TGR-1202-101
L2184: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2185: Results from the Phase I dose expansion phase of Study TGR-1202-101 provide additional support for efficacy of umbralisib in subjects with FL. However, because of the small number of subjects in this study and the differences in response evaluation and dosing of umbralisib, direct comparison with the FL cohort in Study UTX-TGR-205 is not feasible. Of the 19 FL subjects enrolled in this study, the ORR was 47.1% based on the ITT Population and 57.1% based on the mITT Population. The CR rates were 5.9% based on the ITT Population and 7.1% for the mITT Population. The DCR was 82.4% based on the ITT Population and 92.9% based on the mITT Population. The median DOR was 16.3 months for both ITT Population and mITT Population.
L2186: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2187: The FDA's assessment of efficacy was based primarily on Study UTX-TGR-205. The efficacy data from Study UTX-TGR-101 is descriptive and considered supportive.
L2188: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L2189: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2190: Not applicable, the primary efficacy data for umbralisib is from Study UTX-TGR-205 as reviewed in the above sections.
L2191: ## 8.1.6. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L2192: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2193: Study UTX-TGR-205 was the pivotal study. Data from this study provide primary support for efficacy of umbralisib in subjects with FL. Data from the other study (Study TGR-1202-101) that included subjects with FL provide additional support for efficacy of umbralisib in subjects with FL. However, because of the small number of subjects in this study and the differences in response evaluation and dosing of umbralisib, direct comparison with the FL cohort in Study UTX-TGR-205 is not feasible, and efficacy results are instead presented side-by-side in Table 20. DOR and TTR were not calculated for TGR-1202-101 because of the limited number of subjects.

L2202: ## **8.1.7. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness**
L2203: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2204: The primary study to characterize the efficacy of umbralisib in FL is Study UTX-TGR-205. One additional study, Study TGR-1202-101, also included 19 subjects with FL.
L2205: A comparison of the efficacy results across studies was not conducted due to the limited number of subjects with FL enrolled outside of the pivotal study UTX-TGR-205 as well as the differences in response evaluation and dosing of umbralisib. Given the minimal number of additional subjects with FL in Study TGR-1202-101, interpretation of these results will be limited and will not meaningfully contribute to the description of efficacy.
L2206: Unlike Study UTX-TGR-205, Study TGR-1202-101 did not require FL subjects to have at least 2 prior therapies including an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent.
L2207: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2208: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the data from the two trials cannot be pooled together due to the differences in the inclusion/exclusion criteria of the two trials. The evaluation of efficacy is based on the data from UTX-TGR-205.
L2209: The efficacy of umbralisib in patients with FL is based on a single-arm cohort of Study UTX-TGR-205, an open-label, multi-center, single-arm, multi-cohort trial which included 131 patients with relapsed or refractory FL following at least two prior systemic therapies, including an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent. The trial excluded patients with Grade 3b FL, large cell transformation, prior allogeneic transplant, history of CNS lymphoma, and prior exposure to a PI3K inhibitor. Patients received umbralisib 800 mg orally once daily until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The primary endpoint was overall response rate (ORR) assessed by an independent review committee (IRC) based on modified 2007 International Working Group criteria. Secondary endpoints included duration of response and progression-free survival.
L2210: For efficacy, the Applicant provided data for 117 patients with FL. The median age was 65 years (range: 29 to 87 years), 38% were female, 80% were White, 4% were Black, 73% had Stage III-IV disease, 38% had bulky disease and 97% had a baseline ECOG performance status of 0 to 1. The median number of prior systemic therapies was 3 (range 1 to 10) and 57% of the patients had received three or more regimens with 36% refractory to their last therapy. One-hundred percent had received prior anti-CD20 therapy and 12% had received lenalidomide and anti-CD20 therapy. The median follow-up time was 20.1 months (range: 13.5 to 29.6 months). The overall response rate (ORR) per IRC was 42.7% (95% CI: 33.6, 52.2), with 3.4% achieving a complete response. The median duration of response was 11.1 months, 95% CI (8.3, 16.4).
L2211: Based on the characteristics of the FL patient population enrolled in UTX-TGR-205, specifically the median of 3 prior lines of therapy, the data support the determination that umbralisib has clinically meaningful activity in patients with relapsed or refractory FL who have received at least three prior lines of systemic therapy.
L2212: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L2213: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2214: Safety data for subjects exposed to single agent umbralisib from the pivotal Study UTX-TGR-205 as of the cutoff date of 01 September 2019 were integrated with data from 4 other studies (Studies TGR-1202-101, TGR-1202-202, UTX-TGR-103, and UTX-TGR-501) to form an integrated database (IDB), which included a total of 371 subjects (82 subjects with MZL, 147 subjects with FL, and 142 subjects with other hematologic malignancies) hereafter referred to as the Umbralisib Safety Population.
L2215: Safety data from Study TGR-1202-102, a completed Phase I study in 32 subjects with
L2216: solid tumor cancers, and safety data from 6 clinical pharmacology studies in healthy subjects (Studies TGR 1202-PK101, TGR 1202-PK102, TGR-1202-901, TGR-1202-902, TGR-1202-904, and TGR-1202-908) were not integrated with the other studies.
L2217: Data from these studies allow for an informed assessment of the safety profile of umbralisib 800 mg QD and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk in subjects with FL. This safety population is also considered appropriate for the detection and characterization of common AEs and to provide guidance on toxicity management.
L2218: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2219: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2220: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L2221: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2222: The Umbralisib Safety Population included all subjects who received at least 1 dose of single agent umbralisib at doses \ge 800 mg and was used for all analyses for single agent umbralisib. Pooled safety analyses included TEAEs, laboratory assessments, vital signs, and subgroup analyses (gender [male, female], age group [&lt;65 years, \ge 65 years], race [Caucasian, non-Caucasian], geographic region [US, non-US], prior lines of therapy [&lt;2, \ge 2], prior lenalidomide therapy [yes, no], previous treatment with lenalidomide in combination with an anti-CD20 therapy [yes, no], eGFR at baseline [mildly decreased, moderately decreased, severely decreased renal function, or end stage renal disease], and hepatic impairment [mild, moderate, severe]). In addition, physical examinations and ECGs were not pooled and were examined by study.
L2223: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2224: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The FDA reviewed data from the pivotal trial as well as the Safety Pool population (N = 371) and the 120-day Safety update, extending data collection to January 2020.
L2225: Safety endpoints listed below were used to evaluate the safety and tolerability of single agent umbralisib in this population of patients with FL and patients with hematologic malignancies:
L2226: - Incidence and severity of all Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAE)
- Grade 3-5 TEAEs
- Serious adverse events
- TEAEs leading to treatment modifications
- Adverse events of Special Interest
- Clinical laboratory test abnormalities
- Prior therapies
L2227: The frequency of TEAEs was tabulated by the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) Version 22.1. The NCI CTCAE version 4.03 was used to grade and summarize adverse events.
L2228: To inform the safety review, FDA used grouped preferred terms for more sensitive and informative safety analyses. The FDA preferred terms are listed in Section Error!

L2240: ## *Dose Modifications*
L2241: Overall in the Umbralisib Safety Population (N=371), 57.4% of subjects had at least 1 dose adjustment (46.4% of subjects due to AEs). Umbralisib dose was reduced in 16.7% of subjects (13.2% of subjects due to AEs), umbralisib dosing was interrupted in 52.6% of subjects (43.7% of subjects due to AEs), and umbralisib was withdrawn in 21.8% of subjects (14.8% of subjects due to AEs).
L2242: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2243: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of exposure. A median exposure of 7.3 months in patients with FL and approximately 6 months in patients with hematologic malignancies provides sufficient data to support the evaluation of safety. However, there is a paucity of data to evaluate long-term toxicity.
