L2264: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L2265: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2266: The evaluation of safety is based on data for subjects exposed to single agent umbralisib from the pivotal Study UTX-TGR-205 as of the cutoff date of 01 September 2019 and from 4 other studies (Studies TGR-1202-101, TGR-1202-202, UTX-TGR-103, and UTX-TGR-501). Further details are provided in Section 8.2. This population allows for an informed assessment of the safety profile of umbralisib and a judgment of the overall benefit-risk in subjects with FL.
L2267: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2268: The safety database was acceptable for review. The initial submission did not include toxicity grades in the laboratory (ADLB) dataset. An information request was sent to the Applicant to enable a lab-shift analysis by the reviewers.
L2269: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L2270: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L2271: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2272: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets and individual case narratives; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections; data in the CRFs and adverse event databases were consistent.
L2273: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2274: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2275: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L2276: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2277: Each AE term was mapped to a SOC and PT using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) version 22.1. The AE severity was graded using Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Event (CTCAE) version 4.03.
L2278: A TEAE was defined as an AE that first occurred or worsened in intensity from the first dose of study drug through 30 days after the last dose of study drug. The AEs that started before the first dose of study drug but considered related to study drug by the Investigator were assumed worsened in intensity during treatment and therefore were reported as TEAEs. The analysis of AEs included subjects with any TEAE, serious TEAE, CTCAE Grade \ge 3 TEAE, TEAEs that led to death, TEAEs that led to discontinuation of study drug, TEAEs that led to dose reduction or interruption of study drug, AESIs, and drug-related AESIs. In addition, the number and percentage of subjects with TEAEs, serious TEAEs, TEAEs leading to discontinuation of study drug, TEAEs leading to dose reduction, TEAEs leading to dose interruption, and TEAEs leading to death were summarized by period of exposure to umbralisib (&lt;3 months, 3 to &lt;6 months, 6 to &lt;12 months, 1 to &lt;2 years, and \ge 2 years).
L2279: Adverse events of special interest (AESIs) were predefined in the integrated SAP; the criteria used to define AESIs were drug causality assessment, serious adverse event (SAE) rate, severity grade, frequency of TEAEs, class effect, discontinuation rate, and clinical context. All AESIs had SAEs (at least possibly related) plus one or more of the other criteria. Standardized MedDRA Queries relevant to the AESI list were used to select any additional PTs associated with AESI.
L2280: The Umbralisib Safety Population (N=371) supported predominantly by the pivotal UTX-TGR-205 study, provide the primary source for adverse drug reaction (ADR) determination. Safety data were reviewed systematically for TEAEs irrespective of Investigator's causality assessment, which includes serious TEAEs, TEAEs leading to dose modification, and AESIs. This was accompanied by individual case reviews of selected events. New or worsening laboratory parameters were also analyzed.
L2281: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2282: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2283: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L2284: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2285: Laboratory assessments were pooled for the Umbralisib Safety Population and included changes from baseline, laboratory abnormalities based on CTCAE grade, shifts from baseline to post-baseline worst value based on CTCAE grade for hematology and serum chemistry (including liver function tests) parameters measured in the clinical trials.
L2286: The clinical monitoring of subject safety was considered adequate for the expected toxicities associated with umbralisib. Subjects were questioned about AEs at each clinic visit. In addition, AEs could also be detected when reported by the subjects during or between visits or through physical examination, laboratory test results, or other assessments. Further to the standard safety evaluations outlined above, AE categories expected to be associated with umbralisib were also analyzed.
L2287: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2288: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2289: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L2290: ## Deaths
L2291: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2292: Overall, 4 deaths due to AEs (pleural effusion, septic shock, myocardial infarction, and pneumonia legionella) were reported, and all 4 subjects died within 30 days of last dose of umbralisib (Table 24). The events of pleural effusion, septic shock, myocardial infarction were considered not related to umbralisib; pneumonia legionella was considered unlikely related to umbralisib.
L2293: Other deaths include death due to PD in 22 subjects (5.9%) and death due to "other/unknown/missing" after 30 days of last dose of umbralisib in 3 subjects (0.8%) due to heart failure, myelodysplasia, respiratory failure.

L2299: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2300: Overall, 7 deaths occurred in the patients with FL (N=131) and none were due to adverse events. Among these deaths, 3 were due to progressive disease. One death occurred within 30 days of last dose of umbralisib and was due to progressive disease.
L2301: Four patients died due to other reasons:
L2302: - A 72 year old male with relapsed/refractory FL developed MDS and died 4 months after discontinuing umbralisib.
- A 73 year old man with relapsed/refractory FL died due to unknown paraneoplastic disease 3 months after stopping umbralisib.
- An 83 year old man with relapsed/refractory FL died due to respiratory failure 10 months after umbralisib was discontinued.
- A 73 year old female with relapsed/refractory FL which was later transformed to diffuse large B-cell lymphoma and was started on multi-agent systemic chemotherapy died due to unknown cause 4 months after discontinuing umbralisib.

There was a higher incidence of any grade SAEs in the safety pool compared to the FL cohort (26.4% versus 22.1%) with diarrhea/colitis more pronounced. Adverse events leading to umbralisib dose interruption in this group were reported in 59 subjects (45%) overall, which were mostly diarrhea/colitis (16.7%), upper respiratory tract infection (6.1%), vomiting (5.3%) and transaminase elevation (5.3%).

L2369: ## *TEAEs of at Least Grade 3*
L2370: Overall, 189 subjects (50.9%) had Grade \ge3 TEAEs. Of these, 124 subjects (33.4%) had Grade \ge3 TEAEs related to umbralisib. The most frequently reported Grade \ge3 TEAEs (at least 5% of total pooled subjects) were neutropenia (11.3% of subjects), diarrhea (7.3% of subjects), ALT increased (5.7% of subjects), and AST increased (5.7% of subjects). The most frequently reported Grade \ge3 TEAEs related to umbralisib (at least 5% of total pooled subjects) were neutropenia (8.9% of subjects), diarrhea (6.7% of subjects), ALT increased (5.4% of subjects), and AST increased (5.4% of subjects).
L2371: ## *Adverse Drug Reactions*
L2372: The Umbralisib Safety Population, supported predominantly by the pivotal Study UTX-TGR-205, provide the primary source for ADR determination. Safety data were reviewed systematically for TEAEs irrespective of Investigator's causality assessment, which includes serious TEAEs, TEAEs leading to dose modification, and AESIs, observed in the Umbralisib Safety Population. This was accompanied by individual case reviews of selected events. New or worsening laboratory parameters were also analyzed. Each TEAE and laboratory parameter evaluated for ADR determination were assessed against pre-specified criteria as follows: frequency, severity, and seriousness; clinical experience to date with umbralisib; observed dose-response relationship; association with mechanism of action of umbralisib as an inhibitor of PI3K\delta and CK1\epsilon; consistency with non-clinical findings with umbralisib; underlying malignancy, comorbidities, or concurrent illness; and/or clinically meaningful shift for laboratory parameter. Common adverse reactions reported in \ge10% of subjects with MZL, FL, and other hematologic
L2373: malignancies treated with umbralisib are summarized in (Table 33). ADRs of any grade were reported in 351 subjects (94.6%), and ADRs of Grade 3 and Grade 4 were reported in 135 subjects (36.4%) and 31 subjects (8.4%), respectively. The most frequently reported ADRs of any grade (\ge 20\%) were diarrhea (52.3%), nausea (41.5%), fatigue (38.8%), musculoskeletal pain (28.6%), and vomiting (23.2%); none of these were of Grade 4.

L2410: ## Vital Signs
L2411: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2412: Vital sign results were not pooled due to the heterogeneity of assessment schedule across studies. In general, the changes from baseline were not clinically meaningful. Of the TEAEs associated with vital signs, the most frequently reported in the Umbralisib Safety Population were pyrexia (12.7%), hypertension (7.0%), and weight decreased (7.0%).
L2413: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2414: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position
L2415: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L2416: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2417: ECG data were summarized by study for Study TGR-1202-101 and Study TGR-1202-102. No pooled analyses based on IDB were performed due to limited data and heterogeneity of assessment schedules across studies. Of the TEAEs associated with ECG parameters, the most frequently (at least 1% total) reported in the Umbralisib Safety Population were atrial fibrillation (1.3%) and sinus tachycardia (1.1%). No TEAEs of supraventricular tachycardia or ventricular extrasystoles were reported.
L2418: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2419: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position
L2420: ## **QT**
L2421: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2422: A concentration-QTc analysis based on data from the 32 subjects of Cohort S (umbralisib monotherapy) in Study TGR-1202-102 was conducted to characterize the relationship of the change from baseline QTc interval with umbralisib plasma concentration. The concentration QTc analysis did not reveal a significant relationship or risk of \ge 20 ms corrected QT interval by Fredericia (QTcF) prolongation at the time points evaluated. The predicted \DeltaQTcF was higher at the 4-hour post dose time point than for other time points, reaching 3.6 ms with a 90% CI upper bound of 10.2 ms.
L2423: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2424: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that there is no risk of QTc prolongation with umbralisib. The Applicant's exposure-corrected QT interval (QTc) analyses were conducted using steady state exposure (C_{max,ss}) data in Study TGR-1202-102. The geometric mean C_{max} on Cycle 2 Day 1 was 4012 ng/mL (n=20), which was lower than the therapeutic exposure (7266 ng/mL) obtained from PPK analysis. The 1200 mg QD dose group in Study TGR-1202-102 did not provide higher exposure (4172 ng/mL) as compared to the 800 mg QD dose group. Thus, the Applicant's QT assessment was not adequate to extrapolate the risk of QTc prolongation to the higher exposure range. The FDA agrees that there were no large mean increases (i.e., 20 msec) with umbralisib treatment.
L2425: ## **Immunogenicity**
L2426: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2427: There are no immunogenicity data about umbralisib in this application.
L2428: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2429: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position

L2437: ## 8.2.5.1 Diarrhea
L2438: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2439: Overall in the Umbralisib Safety Population, the most frequently reported TEAE of special interest was diarrhea (194 subjects [52.3%]), including 83 subjects (56.5%) with FL (Table 37). A total of 27 subjects (7.3%) had Grade ≥3 diarrhea, including 13 subjects (8.8%) with FL. A total of 165 subjects (44.5%) had a TEAE of diarrhea that was considered related to umbralisib, including 73 subjects (49.7%) with FL. Of these, 25 subjects (6.7%) reported Grade ≥3 diarrhea related to umbralisib, including 12 subjects (8.2%) with FL. There were no Grade 4 TEAEs of diarrhea.
L2440: ## 8.2.5.2 Hepatic Dysfunction
L2441: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2442: Overall in the Umbralisib Safety Population, ALT increased was reported by a total of 56 subjects (15.1%), including 23 subjects (15.6%) with FL (Table 37). A total of 21 subjects (5.7%) had Grade \ge 3 ALT increased, including 8 subjects (5.4%) with FL. A total of 45 subjects (12.1%) had a TEAE of ALT increased that was considered related to umbralisib, including 17 subjects (11.6%) with FL. Of these, 20 subjects (5.4%) reported Grade \ge 3 ALT increased related to umbralisib, including 8 subjects (5.4%) with FL. Grade 4 TEAEs of ALT increased were reported by a total of 4 subjects (1.1%), including 2 subjects (1.4%) with FL.
L2443: Overall in the Umbralisib Safety Population, AST increased was reported by a total of 55 subjects (14.8%), including 18 subjects (12.2%) with FL (Table 37). A total of 21 subjects (5.7%) had Grade \ge 3 AST increased, including 9 subjects (6.1%) with FL. A total of 45 subjects (12.1%) had a TEAE of AST increased that was considered related to umbralisib, including 16 subjects (10.9%) with FL. Of these, 20 subjects (5.4%) reported Grade \ge 3 AST increased related to umbralisib, including 9 subjects (6.1%) with FL. There were no Grade 4 TEAEs of AST increased.
L2444: ## 8.2.5.3 Neutropenia
L2445: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2446: Overall in the Umbralisib Safety Population, neutropenia was reported by a total of 53 subjects (14.3%), including 19 subjects (12.9%) with FL (Table 37). A total of 42 subjects (11.3%) had Grade \ge 3 neutropenia, including 14 subjects (9.5%) with FL. A total of 42 subjects (11.3%) had a TEAE of neutropenia that was considered related to umbralisib, including 18 subjects (12.2%) with FL. Of these, 33 subjects (8.9%) reported Grade \ge 3 neutropenia related to umbralisib, including 13 subjects (8.8%) with FL. Grade 4 TEAEs of neutropenia were reported by a total of 18 subjects (4.9%), including 8 subjects (5.4%) with FL.

L2467: ## Adverse Events of Special Interest
L2468: The FDA's incidence of AESIs, differed in some instances due to grouping of preferred terms, consistent throughout this review. In the following section, the FDA considerations are provided for each AESI. Any grade AESIs occurred in 110 patients (84%) with FL in the UTX-TGR-205 FL cohort (N = 131).
L2469: ## Diarrhea/colitis
L2470: Diarrhea or colitis is an important identified risk for patients who receive treatment with umbralisib. The Applicant identified events of diarrhea or colitis based on selected MedDRA preferred terms for diarrhea or colitis. Because the clinical presentation of diarrhea or colitis overlaps, FDA used a grouped MedDRA PT (Appendix 19.4) to evaluate the incidence and pattern of umbralisib-associated diarrhea or colitis.
L2471: Diarrhea/colitis was the most common toxicity reported in the safety pool (52.3%), as well as the FL population in UTX-TGR-205 (58%). Grade 3 diarrhea/colitis occurred in 7.3% and 9.2% of patients in the safety pool and FL cohort, respectively.
L2472: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L2473: Based on the 371 patients in the safety pool, diarrhea or colitis led to dose interruption in 12.3%, dose reduction in 2.2%, and treatment discontinuation in 4.8% of patients. It is one of the most common reasons for treatment modification. For all grade diarrhea or colitis, the median time to onset was 1 month, with 75% occurring within 2.8 months. For patients with grade \ge 3 diarrhea or colitis, the median time to onset was 4.5 months, with 75% occurring within 8 months.
L2474: Based on the data, there is no established standard of treatment for umbralisib-associated diarrhea or colitis. Labeling will include a Warning and Precaution for diarrhea or colitis and dose modifications to ensure safe use of the drug. Patients should be aware that umbralisib should be discontinued in the event of severe diarrhea or colitis.
L2475: ## Hepatotoxicity
L2476: Based on lab-shift analyses in the safety pool population (N = 371), any grade transaminase elevation occurred in 29% (ALT) and 30% (AST) of the population, and grade 3-4 toxicities were 7% and 6%, respectively. These incidences were lower in the UTX-TGR-205 FL population in which 27% of patients experienced any grade ALT/AST elevation with 6% reaching severity of grade 3-4. In the safety pool, the median onset of grade 3-4 hepatotoxicity was 2 months, with 75% occurring by 3 months. Transaminase elevation led to umbralisib dose interruption in 4.6%, dose reduction in 1.1%, and treatment discontinuation in 3.2%. The Applicant reported no cases of liver failure in patients with hematologic malignancies. The study did not enroll patients with moderate or severe hepatic dysfunction.
L2477: Given the concerns for hepatotoxicity with umbralisib, labeling will include hepatotoxicity as a Warning and Precaution to ensure safe use of umbralisib. The pattern of umbralisib-associated hepatotoxicity, specific monitoring procedures, and dose modifications will need to be included in labeling to inform healthcare providers and patients.
L2478: ## Rash
L2479: In the safety pool, one patient developed a fatal case of Grade 3 exfoliative dermatitis. The event of exfoliative dermatitis led to bacteremia and sepsis and multi-organ failure. In the FL cohort of study UTX-TGR-205, 14% of the patients developed all grade rash. The incidence in the safety pool of any grade rash was 18.9%, with 3% experiencing Grade 3 rash events. Rash led to umbralisib dose interruption in 1.6% of the patients.
L2480: In addition, severe cutaneous reactions, including SJS, TEN, and DRESS, are a known class effect with PI3K inhibitors. Based on a fatal case of exfoliative dermatitis with umbralisib, the incidence of cutaneous reactions, and the known class effect, a Warning and Precaution for severe cutaneous reactions should be included in labeling.
L2481: ## Neutropenia
L2482: Based on lab-shift analyses in the safety pool population, any grade neutropenia occurred in 36% of the population, and grade 3-4 neutropenia of 17%. In patients with FL, any grade neutropenia was 32%, with 14% being Grade 3-4.%
L2483: For the 371 patients in the safety pool, the median time to onset of grade 3-4 neutropenia was 2 months, with 75% occurring by 3.7 months. Neutropenia led to umbralisib dose interruption in 5%, reduction in 2%, and discontinuation in 1%.
L2484: Given the concerns for Grade 3-4 neutropenia coupled with the potential risk for infection with umbralisib, labeling will include neutropenia as a Warning and Precaution to ensure safe use of umbralisib. The pattern of umbralisib-associated neutropenia, monitoring procedures, and dose modifications will need to be included in labeling to inform healthcare providers and patients.
L2485: ## Anemia
L2486: Based on lab-shift analysis data in the safety pool population, any grade anemia occurred in 30% of the population and grade 3-4 anemia in 5%. In patients with FL, any grade anemia was 28%, with 2% being Grade 3-4.
L2487: ## Thrombocytopenia
L2488: Based on lab-shift analysis data in the safety pool population, any grade thrombocytopenia occurred in 25% of the population and grade 3-4 thrombocytopenia in 5%. In patients with FL, any grade thrombocytopenia was 24%, with 3% being Grade 3-4. In patients that developed thrombocytopenia, there was no association with clinically significant bleeding event.
L2489: ## Pneumonia
L2490: Overall in the umbralisib safety population, pneumonia was reported 6.5% of the patients, including 3.5% with Grade \ge 3 pneumonia. In the FL cohort of study UTX-TGR-205, 6.8% and 0.8% of the patients had any grade and Grade \ge 3 pneumonia, respectively. Incidence of SAE in the safety population and FL cohort were 0.8% and 3.7%, respectively.
L2491: ## Infection
L2492: Though not included as an AESI, infection is a significant issue with umbralisib use. In the safety pool of 371 patients, there were 160 patients (43%) with the reported event of infection, with 37 (20%) of grade 3-4 severity. There were two deaths attributable to infection. These included pneumonia legionella and sepsis. Sepsis occurred in eight patients (2%) and led to two deaths.
L2493: Given the concerns for the risk of infection and the incidence of Grade 3-4 neutropenia, labeling will include infection as a Warning and Precaution to ensure safe use of umbralisib.
L2494: ## Pneumonitis
L2495: In the Safety Population, pneumonitis was reported in 1% of patients, with &lt;1% being Grade 3.
L2496: Pneumonitis was reported as a SAE and led to treatment discontinuation in 2 patients (&lt;1%) each, respectively.
L2497: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L2498: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2499: Not applicable.
L2500: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2501: There were no clinical outcome or patient-reported outcome data submitted with this application.
L2502: ## 8.2.7. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L2503: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2504: Subgroup analyses were conducted to identify potential safety issues restricted to particular subpopulations; no clinically meaningful differences were observed as compared to the overall Umbralisib Safety Population.
L2505: ## Sex
L2506: Overall, diarrhea, nausea, and vomiting were reported more frequently in female subjects than male subjects. Neutropenia was reported with a similar incidence between male and female subjects overall but more frequently in male than female subjects with FL. The observed differences by gender were not considered clinically meaningful.
L2507: ## Age
L2508: Overall, Grade ≥3 TEAEs, serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to discontinuation of umbralisib were reported more frequently in subjects in the age group ≥65 years than subjects &lt;65 years (54.5% versus 46.3%, 31.6% versus 17.9%, and 18.2% versus 11.7%, respectively). Nausea was reported more frequently in subjects in the age group ≥65 years than subjects &lt;65 years (44.0% versus 38.3%) overall and in subjects with MZL (41.2% versus 22.6%). Neutropenia and anemia were reported more frequently in subjects in the age group ≥65 years than subjects &lt;65 years (17.2% versus 11.7% and 15.3% versus 6.2%, respectively) overall and in subjects with FL (18.9% versus 8.2% and 14.9% versus 4.1%, respectively). The observed differences by age were not considered clinically meaningful.
L2509: ## Race
L2510: The incidences of TEAEs, serious TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to discontinuation of umbralisib were similar between White and subjects of other races. Vomiting was reported more
L2511: frequently in Non-White than White subjects (28.1% versus 22.1%) overall and in subjects with MZL (26.7% versus 17.9%). The incidence of hepatic dysfunction and rash were also higher in Non-White than White subjects (23.4% versus 17.9% and 18.8% versus 13.0%, respectively). The observed differences by race were not considered clinically meaningful.
L2512: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2513: There was limited representation of racial or ethnic minorities in the FL cohort of study UTX-TGR-205 with few African American (3.8%) or Asian (2.3%) patients to allow broad generalization of these results. In addition, only 4.6% were of Hispanic/Latino ethnicity. This reflects an overall limitation in the data as it applies to the US population and a postmarketing commitment was issued to address this concern (Section 13).
L2514: ## 8.2.8. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials

L2516: There were no special safety studies or clinical trials in this application.
L2517: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2518: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

The presence of umbralisib in human milk and its effects on the breastfed child or on milk production have not been investigated.

L2550: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2551: ## 8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L2552: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2553: The safety of single agent umbralisib was evaluated in 371 subjects, including 147 subjects with FL. Key safety findings were as follows:
L2554: - Death due to TEAEs were reported in 4 subjects (1.1%), and none were in subjects with FL.
- The common TEAEs of diarrhea, nausea, fatigue, and vomiting were reported at similar rates across duration of exposure groupings suggesting that these TEAEs occurred at generally the same rate regardless of the duration of exposure to umbralisib.
L2555: - Common ADRs of any grade (\ge 10\%) were reported in 94.6% of subjects, and ADRs of Grade 3 and Grade 4 were reported in 36.4% and 8.4% of subjects, respectively. The most frequently reported adverse reactions of any grade (\ge 20\%) were diarrhea (52.3%), nausea (41.5%), fatigue (38.8%), musculoskeletal pain (28.6%), and vomiting (23.2%); none of these were of Grade 4.
- Overall, TEAEs of special interest were reported in 77.6% of subjects. The most frequently reported TEAEs of special interest (at least 10% of total pooled subjects) were diarrhea (52.3% of subjects), ALT increased (15.1% of subjects), AST increased (14.8% of subjects), neutropenia (14.3% of subjects), and anemia (11.1% of subjects).
- The most frequently reported laboratory abnormalities with umbralalisib treatment (&gt;30\% of subjects and at least 5% of subjects with Grade \ge 3) were leukocytes decreased (38.8%, Grade 3: 5.1%), neutrophils decreased (36.7%, Grade 3: 9.0%), hemoglobin decreased (30.4%, Grade 3: 5.4%), and AST increased (30.1%, Grade 3: 6.0%).
L2556: In conclusion, single agent umbralalisib was well tolerated and had a manageable safety profile in the overall and FL populations.
L2557: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2558: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the safety data demonstrates that umbralalisib demonstrated a tolerable safety profile in patients with follicular lymphoma. Although, given the median exposure of 7 months in patients with FL and approximately 6 months in patients with hematologic malignancies, there is limited data to support longer-term safety.
L2559: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L2560: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L2561: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2562: There are no major statistical issues identified from this submission. The trials included in the review of this application were designed to be single arm open-label studies with different study objectives. No statistical inference on comparison was conducted. The primary efficacy endpoint was overall response rate (CR + PR). The single-arm aspect of the analyses limits the ability to perform any comparative evaluations.
L2563: There were insufficient number of patients in the race and ethnicity demographic subgroups to provide an assessment of efficacy in this patient subpopulation.
L2564: In the absence of a control arm, it was difficult to interpret the time-to-event endpoint of PFS in these trials. The results of the analysis were descriptive and considered exploratory.
L2565: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L2566: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2567: The benefit-risk assessment is favorable for umbralisib for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma who have received at least three prior systemic therapies.
L2568: ## Efficacy:
L2569: Efficacy of umbralisib in patients with relapsed or refractory (R/R) follicular lymphoma (FL) is based on the results from a single-arm cohort of Study UTX-TGR-205, an open-label, multi-center, single-arm, multi-cohort trial which included 131 patients. Patients received umbralisib 800 mg twice daily continuously until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. The primary endpoint was overall response rate (ORR) per independent review committee (IRC) based on modified International Working Group Revised Response Criteria for Malignant Lymphoma (Cheson et al. 2007). Among the 117 patients with FL evaluable for efficacy, the median follow-up time was 20.1 months (range: 13.5 to 29.6 months). The ORR per IRC was 42.7% (95% CI 33.6, 52.2), with 3.4% achieving a complete response. The median duration of response (DOR) was 11.1 months, 95% CI (8.3, 16.4).
L2570: ## Safety:
L2571: The safety profile was supported by analysis of the 131 patients with follicular lymphoma from the study UTX-TGR-205 and a combined safety database of 371 patients with hematologic malignancies [marginal zone lymphoma (22%), follicular lymphoma (40%), diffuse large B-cell lymphoma or mantle cell lymphoma (20%), and other hematologic malignancies (18%)] who received umbralisib doses \ge800 mg daily.
L2572: In 131 patients with relapsed or refractory FL, the median duration of treatment was 7.3 months (range 1 day to 22.5 months) and the most common adverse events (\ge20% of patients) were diarrhea-colitis, nausea, fatigue, musculoskeletal pain, vomiting, upper respiratory tract infection and abdominal pain. Treatment-emergent laboratory abnormalities of any grade occurring in \ge 20% of patients were increase in creatinine (75%), neutropenia (32%), anemia (28%), increase in ALT/AST (27%) and thrombocytopenia (24%). Serious adverse events occurred in 22% of patients and no fatal adverse events occurred. Dose discontinuations due to any adverse events were reported in 12% of patients, most often due to diarrhea-colitis.
L2573: The primary safety issues identified with umbralisib include serious, including fatal, infections and cutaneous reactions, and serious diarrhea or colitis, hepatotoxicity, and neutropenia. In the 371 patients with hematologic malignancies, 51% of patients experienced a grade 3 or 4 toxicity, the most common including neutropenia (17%), diarrhea-colitis (8%), and increased AST/ALT (6%). Fifteen percent of patients discontinued umbralisib due to an adverse event, most often due to diarrhea-colitis and increased AST/ALT.
L2574: ## **Benefit-Risk:**
L2575: Umbralisib has an overall favorable benefit-risk in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma; however, the benefit-risk determination was considered in the context of available therapy to support an accelerated approval.
L2576: The eligibility criteria for study UTX-TGR-205 required patients with relapsed or refractory FL to have received at least two prior systemic therapies. The patients with FL enrolled had a median number of 3 prior therapies, 36% were refractory to their last therapy and 31% were refractory to rituximab. The ORR of 42.7% (95% CI: 33.6%, 52.2%) warrants additional consideration in the context of available therapy in the 3rd line setting and beyond. Available therapy includes anti-CD20 therapy (i.e., rituximab and obinutuzumab) as a single agent and in combination, bendamustine monotherapy and in combination, and immunotherapy combinations such as rituximab plus lenalidomide. In the 3rd line setting and beyond, the ORRs for these therapies, including single-agent and combinations, ranges from 57% to 80% with associated durability. Taking into consideration the ORR of 42.7% with a lower bound of the confidence interval of 33.6%, and available therapies in a comparable treatment setting, the review team determined that the residual uncertainty regarding the benefit of umbralisib in these patients warrants restriction of the indication to patients with relapsed or refractory FL who have received 3 prior systemic therapies.
L2577: Therefore, the benefit-risk of umbralisib is deemed favorable to support accelerated approval under 21 CFR 314.510 Subpart H for the following indication:
L2578: - Treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma who have received at least three prior lines of systemic therapy.

L2617: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L2618: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2619: The review team recommends five postmarketing requirements (PMR) and two postmarketing commitments (PMC). The goal of the postmarketing requirements are to confirm clinical benefit in patients with FL and MZL, determine a safe and appropriate dose in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment, characterize QTc prolongation risk with umbralisib, and evaluate drug-drug interactions. The postmarketing commitments are to provide information on racial and ethnic minorities consistent with the U.S. FL and MZL population and evaluate the PK of umbralisib with a strong CYP3A inducer.
L2620: ## Postmarketing Requirements
L2621: - Conduct a randomized, Phase 3 clinical trial that verifies and describes the clinical benefit of umbralisib in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma and marginal zone lymphoma. The trial should include sufficient numbers of racial and ethnic minority patients to better reflect the U.S. patient population and allow for interpretation of the results in these patient populations. Patients should be randomized to receive immunotherapy with or without umbralisib. The primary endpoint should be progression-free survival, with secondary endpoints that include overall survival and objective response rate
L2622: Final Protocol Submission:	06/2021
Trial Completion:	12/2026
Final Report Submission:	06/2027
L2623: - Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic study to determine a safe and appropriate dose of umbralisib in patients with moderate to severe hepatic impairment. Design and conduct the study in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry titled Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling.
L2624: Draft Protocol Submission:	06/2021
Final Protocol Submission:	09/2021

L2626: the ICH E14 Guidances for industry titled E14 Clinical Evaluation of QT/QTc and E14 Clinical Evaluation of QT/QTc Interval Prolongation and Proarrhythmic Potential for Non-Antiarrhythmic Drugs Questions and Answers (R3) Guidance for Industry.

L2684: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L2685: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2686: ## 19.4.1. Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L2687: *Were relevant metabolite concentrations measured in the clinical pharmacology and biopharmaceutics studies?*
L2688: Plasma, feces and urine concentrations or amount of umbralisib were measured in clinical pharmacology trials (TGR-1202-902, TGR-1202-PK101, TGR-1202-PK102, TGR-1202-908, TGR-1202-901, TGR-1202-904) and clinical trials (TGR-1202-101, UTX-TGR-205, TGR-1202-102). Based on results of human mass-balance Study TGR-1202-902, umbralisib is the predominant circulating moiety (79%) in plasma.
L2689: *For all moieties measured, is free, bound, or total measured?*
L2690: The total plasma or serum concentrations of umbralisib were measured in the clinical trials. The average binding of umbralisib is about 99.7-99.8%.
L2691: ## *Bioanalytical methods*
L2692: The bioanalytical method validations and performance for measurements of umbralisib concentrations in all clinical trials are summarized in Table 40 and Table 41 (plasma method MET126) and in Table 42 (serum method MET193v1). The plasma method was modified to provide an alternative assay range, i.e., from 1.00 – 2000 ng/mL to 10.0 – 10000 ng/mL. This change was partially validated and is summarized in Table 43.
L2693: **Table 40: Summary Method Performance for the Determination of Umbralisib Concentrations in Human Plasma (Method MET126)**
L2694: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Analytical method and validation report for the determination of TGR-1202 (base) in human plasma by LC/MS/MS.
Method report MET126 and Addendum 3.
Method description	The method used an analog internal standard, RP-5182. An aliquot of each plasma sample (100 µL) was added to internal standard solution in methanol (750 µL), mixed, and centrifuged. Supernatant was analyzed by LC-MS/MS. Analyte separation was performed with a Synergi Polar RP column with a mobile phase of 25:75 0.1% formic acid in water and acetonitrile run isocratically at a flow rate of 0.4 mL/min. Analytes were detected using an Applied Biosystems Sciex API-3000 triple quadrupole mass spectrometer using turbo ion spray in positive ionization mode.
Materials used for standard calibration curve and concentration	Umbralisib tosylate, lot number: RP-5307/F/1905/45/PTSA.
Stock solution: 1.0 mg/mL in methanol.
Validated assay range	1.08 to 2029.85 ng/mL (and 10.00 to 10060.27 ng/mL [NEBA/STY202]; see Error!
Reference source not found.).
APPEARS THIS WAY ON ORIGINAL

L2735: ## 19.4.2. Pharmacometrics Review
L2736: A population pharmacokinetics (PPK) model was developed by the Applicant to characterize the PK of umbralisib in patients with select hematologic malignancies, previously treated indolent NHL, MCL, or DLBCL and healthy subjects. Subjects intrinsic and extrinsic factors which influence the PK and PK variability of umbralisib were identified. Individual umbralisib exposure metrics were generated for subsequent exposure-response (E-R) analyses for efficacy and safety. In this review, the FDA Pharmacometrics Reviewer validated the Applicant's PPK model and E-R analyses for both efficacy and safety.
L2737: ## 1. Population PK analyses
L2738: Three clinical trials were included in the PPK analyses: Study 101, 205 and 1102. Total of 2014 PK samples collected from 247 subjects across the three studies were included in the final population PK analysis. Concentration range for the analysis was from 1.08 to 16061.5 ng/mL. In Study 101, the subjects with select hematologic malignancies were administered with [REDACTED] (b) (4) umbralisib with the dose range of 50 to 1800 mg QD under fasted or fed condition. In Study 205, the subjects with previously treated indolent NHL, MCL or DLBCL were administered with [REDACTED] (b) (4) umbralisib at the dose level of 800 mg QD under fed condition. In Study 1102, the healthy subjects were administered with [REDACTED] (b) (4) umbralisib at the dose level of 200 mg QD under fasted condition.
L2739: The intrinsic and extrinsic factors of healthy subjects versus subjects with hematological malignancies, disease type (MZL vs FL vs others), age, baseline body weight (BW), sex race (White vs non-White), hepatic function, renal function, and coadministration of strong cytochrome P450 (CYP) 3A inhibitors, strong CYP3A inducers, or acid-reducing agents (ARAs) were evaluated.
L2740: The PK of umbralisib following oral administration was adequately described by a three-compartment disposition model with combined first- and zero-order absorption (**Figure 5**).
L2741: **Figure 5: Structure of the PK Model for Umbralisib**
L2742: Abbreviations: ka=first-order absorption rate constant; D1=duration of zero-order absorption, CL/F=apparent central clearance; PK=pharmacokinetic; Q2/F=apparent intercompartmental clearance 1; Q3/F=apparent intercompartmental clearance 2; Vc/F=apparent central volume; Vp1=apparent peripheral volume 1; Vp2=apparent peripheral volume 2.
L2743: Source: Applicant's population PK and E-R study report
L2744: The equations for the final model are presented as below:
L2745: CL/F \text{ (L/h)} = 15.5 \times e^{\eta_{CL}}
L2746: Vc/F \text{ (L)} = 312 \times e^{\eta_{Vc}} \times (0.205 \text{ if healthy subject})
L2747: F = 1 \\ \times (0.702 \text{ if in Study 1102}) \\ \times (1.41 \text{ if in Study 205}) \\ \times (1.93 \text{ if DP containing } \text{DS}) \\ \times (1.38 \text{ if fed condition with DP containing } \text{DS in Study 101})
L2748: The PK parameter estimates of the final model are presented in the table below.

L2768: The Applicant evaluated the impact of concomitant acid reducing agents (ARAs) and strong CYP3A4 inhibitors using population PK analysis to support the labeling claim. For ARAs coadministration, the PPK analysis included 20 subjects who had quantifiable umbralisib concentrations during the period of co-administration of ARAs. Out of the 20 subjects, 6 subjects had intense PK and 14 subjects had sparse PK. For strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, 10 subjects were included in the PPK analysis. Out of the 10 subjects, 4 subjects had intense PK
L2769: and 6 subjects had sparse PK. As shown in **Table 46**, the change in objective function values (OFV) relative to the base model were all statistically non-significant (p&gt;0.01). Thus, the Applicant determined that the use of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and ARAs had no clinically meaningful impact on the PK of umbralisib.

L2779: ## **Reviewer's comment:**
L2780: *The Applicant's PPK analysis is acceptable for describing the PK of umbralisib in the healthy subjects and select hematologic malignancies patients. The CL of umbralisib estimated by the Applicant's PPK model across different studies are comparable (Figure 8).*
L2781: Figure 8: Clearance of umbralisib estimated by the final PPK model across different studies
L2782: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2783: *The evaluations of the impact of mild/moderate renal impairment on the PK of umbralisib using PPK analysis appear to be reasonable. In the Applicant's PPK analysis for renal impairment, the renal function was categorized based on eGFR estimated by Modification of Diet in Renal Disease (MDRD). FDA Reviewer also evaluated the CL in different renal function groups categorized by creatinine clearance estimated by Cockcroft-Gault equation. As shown in Figure 9, the CL of umbralisib across different renal function groups based on creatinine clearance was comparable, further supporting (b)(4) that no dose adjustment would be needed for mild/moderate renal impairment subjects.*
L2784: **Figure 9: Clearance of umbralisib across different renal function groups categorized by creatinine clearance estimated by Cockcroft-Gault equation**
L2785: Note: normal renal function: \ge 90 mL/min (n=116); mild renal impairment: 60 – 89 mL/min (n=85); moderate renal impairment: 30-59 mL/min (n=46).
L2786: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2787: *In the Applicant's evaluation of the impact of concomitant strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and acid reducing agents (ARAs), the frequency and dosing time of the co-administrated strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and ARAs were not clearly provided. In addition, out of the 10 subjects who were co-administrated with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors, only 4 of them had intense PK, while 6 subjects only had sparse PK of 1-3 PK samples; out of 20 subjects who were co-administrated with ARAs, only 5 of them had intense PK, while 14 subjects only had sparse PK of mostly 1-3 PK samples. Based on the limited PK data and information on the administration time of concomitant medications, the Applicant's proposal that no dose adjustment would be needed for concomitant administration of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and ARAs based on PPK analysis is not acceptable. The Applicant conducted dedicated study (TGR-1202-904) to evaluate the effect of ARAs. The result from that study should be used as the primary evidence to make the conclusion for dose adjustment when ARAs are co-administrated.*
L2788: *In the Applicant's evaluation of impact of mild hepatic impairment, only 6 patients with mild hepatic impairment were included. Out of the 6 patients, 4 of them had only 1 PK sample. Although the observed dose-normalized umbralisib concentrations from mild hepatic impairment patients were within the range of those from normal hepatic function patients (Figure 10), the available data were limited to conclude the effect of mild hepatic impairment on PK of umbralisib.*
L2789: **Figure 10: Comparison of umbralisib observed concentrations between normal hepatic function and mild hepatic impairment patients**
L2790: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2791: ## 2. Exposure-Efficacy Analyses in FL Population
L2792: The PPK and exposure-safety analyses were based on the pooled data from both MZL and FL populations. Thus, the review regarding PPK, exposure-safety analyses and integrated efficacy/safety analyses refers to the Appendix in the Assessment Aid for FL population. In the following pharmacometrics review, the Reviewer will discuss about the exposure-efficacy analyses for FL population.
L2793: Summary of the observed efficacy endpoints for the FL population in the E-R analyses for efficacy is shown in **Table 48**. Statistically significant relationships were observed for C_{minss} and ORR, TTR and best %CfB in target lesion dimensions. **Figure 11** shows the univariate E-R relationships for ORR and %CfB in target lesion dimensions in the FL population. From the multivariate exposure-efficacy analysis, at 800 mg QD, the model predicted ORR was 56.1% (90% CI: 46.2, 65.1) (**Table 49**), and the model predicted probability of best %CfB in target lesion dimensions was -47.3% (90% CI: -56.9, -38.4) (**Table 50**).

L2815: ## **Reviewer's comments:**
L2816: *The Applicant's E-R analyses for efficacy are acceptable. The positive E-R relationships for ORR and %CfB in FL population indicated that higher dose than the recommended 800 mg will provide better efficacy. However, this interpretation is limited as only a small number of patients received doses other than 800 mg QD.*
L2817: ## 3. E-R Analyses for Safety
L2818: The exposure-safety dataset included 223 subjects (N=83 from Study 101 and N=140 from Study 205). Of the safety-endpoints evaluated, the AESI with an incidence rate \ge 10\% were diarrhea, elevated ALT/AST, rash, neutropenia, pneumonia, and anemia. Among these AESI, only diarrhea (any grade) and elevated ALT/AST (any grade and Grade \ge 3) were statistically correlated with umbralisib exposure (Figure 12).
L2819: Figure 12: Relationships between exposure and diarrhea or elevated ALT/AST
L2820: Abbreviations: AIC=Akaike information criterion; ALT=alanine aminotransferase; AST=aspartate aminotransferase; Cav C1=Cycle 1, Day 1 average concentration; Cavss=average concentration at steady state. Note: Horizontal boxplot below shows the exposure distribution for the 800 mg dose group. The p-value is for the slope of the logistic regression fit.
L2821: Source: Applicant's population PK and PK/PD study report
L2822: For any grade diarrhea, following the covariate analysis, a statistically significant effect of sex on the intercept of the relationship was identified and retained in the final model. For Cav-any grade elevated ALT/AST, following the covariate analysis, statistically significant effects of ECOG and MZL on the slope of the relationship were identified and retained in the final model. No E-R relationships were identified for any grade rash, neutropenia, anemia, thrombocytopenia and pneumonia.
L2823: **Reviewer's comment:**
L2824: *The Applicant's E-R analyses for safety are acceptable. The positive E-R relationships for diarrhea and elevated ALT/AST indicated that increasing the dose in MZL/FL population may lead to higher incidence of adverse events.*
L2825: ## 4. Integrated E-R Analyses
L2826: The Applicant conducted simulation to predict the probability of key efficacy and safety endpoint rates for doses ranging from 400 to 1200 mg QD in MZL and FL populations (Table 51). For exposure-efficacy analysis for FL population, detail refers to Pharmacometrics Review in Appendix in FL Assessment Aid. Figure 13 shows the relative change in event rates relative to the 800 mg QD dose.
L2827: For MZL population, similar ORR was projected at different doses, higher rates of diarrhea and ALT/AST elevation were predicted at higher doses. For FL population, there was a substantial reduction in ORR and tumor shrinkage projected at doses lower than 800 mg QD. On the other hand, any grade and Grade \ge 3 diarrhea was projected to modestly increase by &lt;30%, while any grade and Grade \ge 3 elevated ALT/AST were projected to increase by 62% and 32% for the 1000 mg QD dose, and by 131% and 72% for the 1200 mg QD dose.

L2834: **Reviewer's comment:** Based on the Applicant's simulation, a lower dose in MZL population may provide better safety profile while maintaining efficacy. However, there is no efficacy data available at doses lower than 800 mg QD in the MZL patients to discern that. For FL population, while the reduced efficacy at lower doses, and increased risk of diarrhea and ALT/AST elevation at higher doses appear to support the proposed 800 mg QD regimen, these projections were potentially limited by small number of subjects at doses outside of 800 mg in the E-R analysis dataset, particularly for efficacy.
L2835: In conclusion, the Applicant developed a PPK model that reasonably described the PK behavior of umbralalisib in MZL and FL patients. The PK of umbralalisib following oral administration was adequately described by a three-compartment model combined with first- and zero-order absorption after a lag time and first-order elimination. Flat E-R relationships were observed for MZL population while positive E-R relationships were identified for ORR, TTR and %Cfb in target lesion dimensions in FL population, with potential uncertainties due to limited number of subjects at doses outside of 800 mg QD. Positive E-R relationships were observed for diarrhea and elevated ALT/AST.
L2836: ## 19.4.3. Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic (PBPK) Modeling Analysis
L2837: ## Executive Summary
L2838: The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the applicant's physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analyses to evaluate
L2839: - the inhibitory potential of umbralisib on the pharmacokinetics (PK) of substrates of CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19
- the inhibitory and induction potential of umbralisib on the PK of midazolam, a sensitive CYP3A substrate
L2840: The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the PBPK analyses report (R_TGT/1/B) submitted with NDA 213176, responses to FDA's information requests (IRs) submitted on July 24th and 31st, 2020, and the modeling supporting files, and concluded that the effects of umbralisib on CYP3A, CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 could not be confidently predicted using the PBPK modeling approach. The potential of drug- drug interactions (DDIs) of umbralisib with substrates of these enzymes cannot be ruled out.
L2841: ## Background
L2842: The proposed umbralisib dosing regimen is 800 mg once daily with food. The maximal dose examined is 1200 mg once daily with food. Following oral administration of umbralisib (b)(4) once daily under the fed condition, umbralisib AUC and Cmax increased approximately dose proportionally across the dose range of 200-mg to 1000-mg on cycle 1 day1 (C1D1) and C2D1, and steady-state was achieved by C3D1. Umbralisib had a long half-life (166 h) and its accumulation ratio ranged from 5.2 to 11.3 for AUC0-t and 2.8 to 5.9 for Cmax across the doses from 200- to 1200-mg.
L2843: Umbralisib was determined in vitro to be an inhibitor and inducer of CYP3A, and an inhibitor of CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19. The applicant intended to use a PBPK modeling approach to evaluate the potential of drug interactions of umbralisib with the substrates of these CYP enzymes. Refer to the Clinical Pharmacology review section for detail information on umbralisib regarding its ADME properties, in vitro and clinical studies used in PBPK modeling.
L2844: ## Methods
L2845: All simulations were performed using the PK/PD Profiles mode in the Simcyp® Simulator (Version 18 Certara, Sheffield, UK). A scheme of the PBPK simulation strategy is shown in Figure 14, which summarizes the studies used for model development and verification, and model applications in DDI predictions with CYP substrates. The model structure, model optimization and final model parameters for the umbralisib PBPK model are summarized in Table 52. For prediction of DDIs with umbralisib as a perpetrator, Simcyp library substrate files of midazolam, repaglinide, tolbutamide, S-mephenytoin were used without any modifications. All simulations were completed using the sim-Healthy Volunteer population aged 20-50 years with 50% female subjects under the fed condition. All DDI simulations were

L2847: conducted after 168 days of hypothetical QD umbralisib administration to ensure a maximum systemic umbralisib exposure for DDI prediction.

L2854: ## Results
L2855: ## 1. Can the PBPK model adequately describe the PK profiles of umbralisib?
L2856: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L2857: Yes, the PBPK model of umbralisib described umbralisib PK following administration of multiple doses of umbralisib in cancer patients (Figures 15 - 16 and Table 53).

L2866: ## **Additional comments:**
L2867: *The multiple-dose PK data from the 800-mg umbralisib cohort (n=3) in Study TGR-1202-101 were used to build and optimize the umbralisib PBPK model during the model development. (b) (4) drug product was given under the fed condition in this study. Figures 15 - 16 and Table 53 show that the model could reasonably well recover umbralisib plasma concentrations in cancer patients, and the estimated exposures (C_{max} and AUC_{0-24h}) were mostly within 25% of the values observed or estimated from population PK (PopPK) analysis.*
L2868: *It should be noted that the applicant developed the PBPK model of umbralisib to predict DDI in cancer patients. However, all simulations were performed using the default sim-Healthy Volunteer population model. To apply the simulation results, there are two underlying assumptions. One is that umbralisib PK in healthy subjects is similar to that in cancer patients. This assumption may be supported by umbralisib PK at 200-mg dose in healthy subjects (TRG-1202-PK101) and cancer patients (TGR-1202-101) as shown in Figure 17. The second assumption is that there is no difference in enzyme abundance or activity of CYP3A and CYP2Cs between healthy subjects and cancer patients. This is the case for sim-Healthy Volunteer population and sim-Cancer population models. However, there are evidences that CYP enzyme abundance or activity may be altered in cancer patients (Coutant DE 2015; Schwenger H 2018). Therefore, the simulation results should be interpreted with caution.*
L2869: Figure 17: PK parameters following oral administration of a single dose of 200-mg umbralisib (b) (4) under the fasted condition
L2870: Source: Figure 4-4, the population PK analysis (TGTX-PMX-UMBRAISIB-1100-001)
L2871: ## 2. Can PBPK analyses predict the effects of umbralisib on the pharmacokinetics of CYP substrates?
L2872: No, the PBPK analyses are inadequate to predict the complex inhibitory and induction effects on CYP3A, and the effects of umbralisib on the PK of the substrates of CYP2C8 (repaglinide), CYP2C9 (tolbutamide) and CYP2C19 (S-mephentytoin) due to the uncertainties about in vitro to in vivo extrapolation. (b) (4)
L2873: (b) (4)
L2874: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 213176
## UKONIQ (umbralisib)
L2875: Source: the reviewer's analysis
L2876: ## Conclusions
L2877: The effects of umbralisib on CYP3A, CYP2C8, CYP2C9 and CYP2C19 cannot be confidently predicted using the PBPK modeling approach. The potential of drug-drug interactions of umbralisib with substrates of these enzymes cannot be ruled out.

L2879: - Austin RP et al. The influence of nonspecific microsomal binding on apparent intrinsic clearance, and its prediction from physicochemical properties. *Drug Metab Dispos* 2002;30(12):1497-503

L2898: Graph showing Tumor Size (\text{mm}^3) versus Days post Tumor Inoculation (16 to 40). The graph compares three treatment groups:
Vehicle (5% Polysorbate 80 + Methyl cellulose, 4000 cps (0.5% w/v)) Bidx25 p.o. (Blue line, highest tumor growth).
ICZ-515 150mg/kg Qdx25 p.o. (Red line, intermediate tumor growth).
Ara-C 50mg/kg, 5 days on and 2 days off, 25mg/kg, 5 days i.p. (Green line, lowest tumor growth).
The Vehicle group shows rapid tumor growth, reaching approximately 3700 \text{mm}^3 by Day 40. The ICZ-515 group shows moderate growth, reaching approximately 1800 \text{mm}^3 by Day 40. The Ara-C group shows the most significant inhibition, with tumor size remaining below 500 \text{mm}^3 throughout the study period.

L2904: Diagram illustrating the structure of the PK Model for Umbralisib. The model consists of three compartments: Oral dosing, Central, and Peripheral (1) and Peripheral (2). Oral dosing is connected to the Central compartment via parameters ka, D1, and lag time. The Central compartment is connected to Peripheral (1) via Q2/F and to Peripheral (2) via Q3/F. The Central compartment is also connected to the elimination pathway via CL/F. The Peripheral compartments are labeled with their respective volumes: Peripheral (1) Vp1/F and Peripheral (2) Vp2/F.
L2905: Figure 6 displays five plots for GOF Diagnostics of the Umbralisib Final Model. The top row shows scatter plots of Observations versus Population predictions (left) and Individual predictions (right). Both plots show a strong positive correlation, with data points clustered around the dashed diagonal line (y=x). The middle row shows Conditional Weighted Residuals (CWRES) versus Time (h) (left) and CWRES versus Population predictions (right). In both residual plots, the CWRES values are centered around zero, with a solid gray line representing the smoothed regression line. Dashed black lines indicate the boundaries of the CWRES \pm 2 and \pm 5 intervals. The bottom plot is a histogram of CWRES, showing a distribution centered near zero, with the highest count (around 400) between CWRES values of -1 and 0.
L2906: Figure 7 displays four plots showing the prediction-corrected visual predictive check (pcVPC) for Umbralisib concentration versus time after last dose (TALD) for the final model. The plots compare prediction-corrected observed concentrations (blue dots) against simulated percentiles (black lines) and 95% prediction intervals (gray bands).
The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid:
Top Left: All Data, Umbralisib Concentration vs TALD for the First 25 hours.
Top Right: All Data, Umbralisib Concentration vs TALD Until >150 hours.
Bottom Left: Subjects With Hematologic Malignancies, Umbralisib Concentration vs TALD for the First 25 hours.
Bottom Right: Healthy Subjects, Umbralisib Concentration vs TALD for the First 25 hours.
The Y-axis for all plots is pred-conc concentrations (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale (1 to 10000). The X-axis for the first 25 hours plots is Time after last dose (h) (0 to 25). The X-axis for the >150 hours plot is Time after last dose (h) (0 to 180).
L2907: Box plot showing Clearance of umbralisib (CL/F in L/h) estimated by the final PPK model across three different studies (101, 205, and 1102). The y-axis ranges from 0 to 60 L/h. The median CL/F for Study 101 is approximately 20 L/h. The median CL/F for Study 205 is approximately 18 L/h. The median CL/F for Study 1102 is approximately 18 L/h. The interquartile ranges (IQR) are similar across all three studies, indicating comparable clearance.
L2908: Box plot showing Clearance (CL) of umbralisib across three Renal Function Groups: Mild, Moderate, and Normal. The Y-axis is CL, ranging from 0 to 60. The Mild group (Red) has a median CL around 18. The Moderate group (Green) has a median CL around 20. The Normal group (Blue) has a median CL around 20. All groups show a wide range of CL values, with outliers extending up to 60.

L2910: Figure 11 displays two scatter plots showing the relationship between exposure (Cminss) and clinical outcomes (ORR and %CfB) in the MZL population.
The left panel, titled "Exposure-ORR", plots Probability of ORR (Y-axis, 0 to 1) against Cminss (ng/mL) (X-axis, 2000 to 12000). Observed data points (blue circles) are compared against a predicted logistic fit (red line). The p-value for the slope of the logistic fit is p=0.828. The plot shows a wide confidence interval for the predicted probability, ranging from approximately 0.3 to 0.7 across the observed Cminss range.
The right panel, titled "Exposure-%CfB", plots Best %CfB in Target Lesion Dimensions (Y-axis, -100 to 0) against Cminss (ng/mL) (X-axis, 2000 to 12000). Observed data points (blue circles) are compared against a predicted linear fit (black line). The p-value for the slope of the linear fit is p=0.258. The plot shows a negative correlation between Cminss and Best %CfB, with the predicted line decreasing slightly as Cminss increases.

L2919: Chemical structure and molecular weight	
Chemical Structure of umbralisib (Source: NDA213176, Module 3.2.S.1.2. Figure 1)
Molecular weight of umbralisib tosylate: 743.75 g/mol (571.56 g/mol salt form)
Log P: 4.84
Log D: 4.79 at pH 7.4
Umbralisib is a weak base with pKa of 2.71 and exhibits pH dependent solubility characteristics (Table 9).
Table 9: Aqueous Solubility of umbralisib tosylate at 25°C.
Solubility - Aqueous (25°C)	0.032 mg/ml @ pH 1.2
0.018 mg/ml @ pH 2.2
0.002 mg/ml @ pH 4.5
0.003 mg/ml @ pH 7.4
0.003 mg/ml @ pH 10.2
(Source: NDA213176, Module 3.2.S.1.3. General Properties, Table 1)
Pharmacology
Chemical structure of umbralisib tosylate. The structure consists of a central core linked to three substituents: a fluorophenyl group, a methyl group, and a pyrimido[2,1-b]quinazolin-2(3H)-one derivative. The pyrimido[2,1-b]quinazolin-2(3H)-one derivative is attached to a 4-fluorophenyl group, which is further attached to a 2,4-dimethyl-5-fluorophenyl group. The structure is associated with a tosylate counterion (tosyl group).
L2920: Food effect (Fed/fasted)	
There were two studies (TGR-1202-PK101 and TGR-1202-908) evaluating the effect of food on the PK of umbralisib with two different formulations (b) (4).
Study TGR-1202-PK101 was an open-label, single-dose, randomized, crossover study to determine whether high-fat, high calorie food has an effect on the PK of umbralisib in 24 healthy subjects who received a single dose of 200 mg (b) (4) tablet. Results showed that the exposure of umbralisib was increased 67% and 2.7-fold for AUC and C_{max}, respectively, with food following a single dose of 200 mg (b) (4) tablet in healthy subjects (Table 11).
Table 11: Statistical analysis (test/reference) of umbralisib PK parameters (umbralisib (b) (4) tablets) under fasted (reference) and fed (test) condition
AUC0-inf
(GMR, 90% CI)	Cmax
(GMR, 90% CI)	Tmax (hours)
(range)
1.67 (1.42-1.98)	2.73 (1.77-4.83)	Fast
3.0 (2.0-4.0)
Fed
4.0 (2.0-8.0)
Study TGR-1202-908 was an open-label, single-dose, randomized, crossover study to determine whether high-fat, high calorie food (approximately 917 calories with 171 calories from protein, 232 calories from carbohydrate, and 502 calories from fat) has an effect on the PK of umbralisib in 24 healthy subjects who received a single dose of 200 mg (b) (4) tablet. Results showed that the exposure of umbralisib was increased 40% and 2.2-fold for AUC and C_{max}, respectively, with food following a single dose of 200 mg (b) (4) tablet in healthy subjects (Table 12).

L2931: Figure 11 displays two scatter plots showing relationships between exposure metrics and response rates in the Follicular Lymphoma (FL) population.
The left panel, titled "Exposure-ORR", plots the Probability of ORR (Y-axis, 0 to 1) against Cminss (ng/mL) (X-axis, 0 to 12000). Observed data points (blue dots with error bars) are shown, along with a predicted logistic curve (red line) and a shaded confidence interval (red area). The p-value for the logistic fit is p=0.0033. Data points are labeled with ratios (e.g., 5/20, 10/19, 10/20, 18/20).
The right panel, titled "Exposure-%CfB", plots Best %CfB in Target Lesion Dimensions (Y-axis, -100 to -20) against Cminss (ng/mL) (X-axis, 0 to 12000). Observed data points (blue dots) are shown, along with a predicted linear fit (blue line). The p-value for the linear fit is p=0.001.
A horizontal boxplot below the X-axis of both panels shows the exposure distribution for the 800 mg dose group.
